She settles, then holds still: her eyes on the child's eyes, her hands resting on her thighs away from both things, her face in its neutral set, no act (`Conduct.still`, which W2 reads), 44 ticks of it before the sentence (`consts.SETTLE`: a look that follows her placing them has ended, Brooks and Meltzoff 2005's 6.5 s). She says the single test sentence, in the design's never-taught forms (section 12, A28, A55; `consts.TRIAL_FORMS`): "where is the X?" of a known word's thing in a place within its view it has never been seen in, or from a new angle (M6(a)), or of a never-seen exemplar beside a known non-X (Quinn, Eimas and Rosenkrantz 1993); "where is the C X?", a combination never heard, the colour twin beside its original, only once both its words are understood alone (A28), its held-out pair allowed in that line alone. Only her mouth moves. Which of the two is named and the sides are drawn by her own trial stream (the body's seed, spawn key 6), each a fair coin, so chance is 50% by counterbalancing: for any child whose looking does not depend on the word said, a follower of her gaze or her hands, a side's or a toy's favourite, which of its trials named a thing is a fair coin independent of how it looked at it.
    - **Its measure** (the lead's decision A60b, P3's fifteenth round: the proportion of looking over a fixed long window, as infant labs score it, not whether an event lands at a moment; Golinkoff, Hirsh-Pasek, Cauley and Gordon 1987; Fernald, Zangl, Portillo and Marchman 2008; Bergelson and Swingley 2012). The window runs from the test word's onset tick + 2 to its onset tick + 23 (`consts.TRIAL_LOOK`: 22 ticks, 300 ms to 3.6 s after the onset; 300 ms the earliest a shift the word guides can begin, Fernald et al. 2008, "Looking while listening"; a window of seconds as looking-while-listening's proportion of looking to the target is taken over the seconds after the onset, Bergelson and Swingley 2012), its onset on the trial's one timeline (below), so its ticks are the same whichever is named and none of them is timed from the word's sound. On each of its ticks her reading of the child's head line (A40: held 3 ticks, as every rule of hers reads it; `Percept.child_target`) is on the target (T), on the distractor (D) or on neither, and the trial's measure is p = T / (T + D); nothing else about the ticks counts, not when they fall nor their order (`Conduct._trial_look`). A pair's trial with T + D < 4 (`consts.TRIAL_LOOK_MIN`, 600 ms) is void, not scored, and counted (`Ledger.voided`, each word's `voids`), as a lab drops a trial with too little looking at either picture; so is a trial whose attention log shows anything but her mouth moving on any tick from the sentence's first to the window's last (a tick not read among it: 4.3), one with the child's pain, distress or hit (she answers it: 4.10), one whose sentence the world stopped (her voice is stopped only by her conduct, never in a trial), and one whose sentence was not its stimulus's timeline. Nothing the child's voice does voids one. Where the child looked before the window, and when in it it looked, count for nothing: the onset's reading of where it already looked (P3's fourteenth round) and intention to treat (its thirteenth: every trial whose window opened scored, a none or a void against it) are retired with the first look they were built for. For any child whose looking does not depend on the word said, a trial and the same trial with the other thing named end alike, the window's ticks on each thing the same (on and off turned over): its looking is the same whichever was drawn, so the draw labels of its trials are exchangeable and the ledger's permutation test (below) is null by construction, a void among it or not (`test_sim_lang` 63, 68–72). The price, disclosed: a knower's trial with under 4 ticks on either thing, or with her body moving, is dropped as a lab drops it, and its window's first 2 ticks, and every tick after its 23rd, count for nothing whatever it does in them.
    - **Time-matched stimuli, by construction** (P3's twelfth to fourteenth rounds; the lead's decision on C67, as infant labs match their stimuli). The eleventh round put each trial on one timeline by reading the latest onset and the latest end of the sentences its draw could give, but her voice still said them at different lengths ("where is the ball?" 10 ticks, "where is the block?" 11, "where is the stacker?" 12), and its verifier found the same class again: a child that knew no word and hit her 2 ticks after her sound stopped had every ball trial voided before its look was judged and every block trial met ("block" understood in 6 of 6 lives). The twelfth round made every sentence a trial's draw could give one timeline on the clip's tick grid, and its thirteenth one carrier recording before the test word; the thirteenth verifier then found that with one recording a word, where the sentence stopped as the child perceives it (her mouth's last opening above -22 dB, its ears 0.7 m away above 21 dB SPL, her last 10 ms above -44 dB) was the same against every other word ("drum" last, "bear" and "cup" first), so a child that knew no word, with a favourite toy, one look at a fixed tick and one timed from her sound's stop, had the order of its two looks follow the word said: "drum" understood in 12 of 12 lives whatever its distractor (P4's schedule and all 21 pairs), "bear" and "cup" alike, a child that learned its delay from her smiles in 9 of 12, and "pip" in 12 of 12 by a turn gated at tick 15. So since P3's fourteenth round **every sentence of a form is one carrier phrase with its test word spliced into it**, as labs build theirs (looking-while-listening's "where's the X? can you find it?", Fernald et al. 2008; `lang/stimuli.parts`, `splice`): one recording before the test word ("where is the" of the form's carrier word's sentence; for the name "hi."), the test word's own sentence from its onset tick through its last loud tick and one silent tick, and one recording after it, the tag ("see?"; for the name "hi."): "where is the X? see?", "where is the C X? see?", "hi. pip. hi." (`templates.FRAMES`, the test word its focus, before the tag). Its test word is said at the engine's own per-word rate (a prosody on the word, whose steps the tool measures: 1, 4, 10, 16, 22, 30, 38, 50, 62, 74, 92% of the engine's default and up) within a factor of 2 of its natural rate (`consts.TRIAL_RATE_SPAN`, the span her own registers' rates run, 0.15 to 0.30), at the pitch that matches its contour, its F0 at the 10th, 50th and 90th percentile of its voiced frames within 12% of the form's (`consts.TRIAL_F0`), and at the form's one level: every test word of a form at one loudest 10 ms (its gain), set so that its slot is under **the level ceiling**: every window of her sound from 2.5 ms to a tick that overlaps the slot at least 1 dB quieter than the loudest wholly before it (the carrier) and the loudest wholly after it (the tag) (`consts.TRIAL_CEILING_DB`, `stimuli.headroom`), and every 10 ms frame the child's own ears hear of it (either ear, her mouth 0.3 to 3 m away at any angle round its head) 0.5 dB under their loudest before it and after it (`TRIAL_CEILING_EAR_DB`). Everything she sounds before the test word's onset tick and from the tag on is then one recording, sample for sample, whichever is named, and at every level her sound's first moment above it lies in the carrier and its last in the tag: her voice's start and her sound's stop, 10 ms by 10 ms, tick by tick as her mouth shows them, and at the child's own ears summed over their 40 bands (there to the ears' own float rounding, under 10^-6 dB, a tick or two past the slot), are one moment whichever is named. Band by band at its ears they are not: in most of the 80 band-ear channels her voice's start and her sound's stop fall inside the slot and differ by word, as the words' spectra differ (the fourteenth verifier, at 15 places round its head: 53–64 channels for "where is the X? see?", 34–37 for the name; C75b). The recipe is measured before birth (`tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial` writes `lang/trial_lines.json`: each form's timeline is the one the most test words reach, each word at the step nearest its natural rate, then the form's level against the ceiling at the clip and through `body/sim/ears.Ears` at 42 places), and nothing is taken on its word: before anything is brought the conduct makes every sentence the draw could give (its parts made ahead, never kept as heard) and times each on its spliced clip (`lang/stimuli.timeline`: the clip's ticks; each word's first and last tick; every sample before the test word's onset tick and from the tag's first tick, by their digests; each tick of the slot loud, its RMS above -36 dB of full scale (within 20 dB of her speech's level), or silent, its loudest 10 ms below -60 (the edge of hearing at a metre), none between, the loud ticks the same and the last silent, `consts.TRIAL_LOUD_DB` and `TRIAL_SILENT_DB`; the slot under its ceiling at the clip; and while the word scaffold labels her lines, the ticks the words channel carries a symbol and END). A set that is not one timeline (`stimuli.same`), a sentence whose word runs past its slot, or a word with no stimulus in the table, is not used: the probe is dropped and logged, never brought (`Conduct._trial_stimuli`). The clip she says is held to its stimulus again, and a trial whose sentence differs (a voice that changed) is void. So the test word's onset (the window's first tick), its slot, her sentence's end and her voice's ticks fall on the same ticks whichever is named, nothing she sounds outside the slot differs at all, and a child that times a look, a reach, a sound, a hit, pain, distress or a turn from her voice's start or her sound's stop as a whole (10 ms by 10 ms, her mouth, its ears summed over their bands) times it to the same tick whichever is named: every such trial ends the same with the other thing named (the lead's invariance property; the order in which a tick's events and its look are read, a hit, pain or distress still voiding as 4.10 requires, can no longer depend on the draw). What stays is the test word itself (C75, C75b): inside its slot two sentences differ as their words do (their segments, where each word's own sound rises and falls within the slot, 10 ms by 10 ms, band by band where it starts and stops at the child's ears, its spectrum; and a process whose memory reaches back into the slot, a loudness integrator over seconds, carries that on), and a child whose act is timed from there hears the word said. For any child whose looking does not depend on the word, however its acts are timed, the scoring answers it: the proportion of looking over a fixed window, its draw labels exchangeable, the permutation test null by construction (above; A60b).
      - *The matched sets* (her voice as measured on 2026-09-25). "Where is the X? see?": 15 ticks, its noun on ticks 4-7 (its slot ticks 4-8, loud through tick 7, tick 8 silent), the tag from tick 9: ball, bear, car and ring at their natural rate, block, bottle, cup, drum, rattle and tower at 50% of the engine's default (1.43 times the natural 35%), each at pitch +25 to +36% (the emphasis's +30%), their contours within 5% of the form's, each at one loudest 10 ms of -13.8 dB of full scale (gains -0.4 to -1.3 dB; the ceiling's least margin 1.21 dB at the clip and 0.55 dB at its ears); not matched, never tested: "duck" and "book" (their final /k/'s release puts tick 7 at -44 and -50 dB by its RMS, -34 and -42 at its loudest 10 ms: between loud and silent, at every rate) and "box" and "stacker" (longer than their slot at any rate within the span). The name, "hi. X. hi.": 15 ticks, "pip", "viv", "vib" and "pew" each at the natural rate on ticks 5-7 (its slot 5-8), the tag from tick 9, their contours within 8% of the name's (the name said as she always says it), each at one loudest 10 ms of -14.3 dB (gains -0.9 to -1.3 dB; margins 1.83 and 0.55 dB). The combination, "where is the C X? see?": the ball's "blue", "green" and "red" (17 ticks, its slot 4-10, the colour and the noun: its held pair, the blue ball beside its red original, matched), the car's "blue", "green" and "red" (its pair needs "orange", not her word), the cup's "green" alone (18 ticks: its pair, the yellow cup beside its green original, not matched: "yellow" leaves a tick between loud and silent), the block's none; gains -1.1 to -2.9 dB.
      - *The words channel.* While the scaffold labels her lines (A29: from birth until its removal test), the channel carries a birth word's token on its word's last tick and a later word's letters one a tick from its onset, then a space (`lexicon.Words`): a token and a spelled word, or two spelled words of different lengths, are never one timeline on it. So while it runs only the seven birth toys pair among themselves (ball, bear, block, bottle, car, cup, drum: 21 pairs); "ring", "rattle" and "tower" (4, 6 and 5 letters) pair with nothing; the name ("pip", a token) is never one timeline with its spelled foils, nor is any colour with another (3 to 6 letters): the name test and the combination run only once the channel is silenced, as section 12's word claims are (A29; C68).
      - *Tested* (`test_sim_lang` 69, failing on 1387a44 at its own first assertion): the eleventh verifier's two children end every trial the same with the draw turned over; so do 26,040 children and trials, each with one act timed from what it hears or sees (her voice's start; her sound's stop at -36, -48 and -60 dB and her mouth's closing at those levels, on the clip's tick grid; her clip's end; the test word's onset and end; the words channel's END; the window's close) at every offset from -4 to +30 ticks, a look or a reach on either thing or her face, a sound, its token, a hit, pain or distress, in the suite's timed voice and her real voice, stage 1 and 2, a pair and the name; and 293 trials of 54 random lives (children with 1-4 such acts a trial, wandering and babbling, W2-like motions breaking their contract, her imperfection, the channel on and off), each run again from its draw with the other thing named: the same void and why, the same none, a met for a missed, on the same tick. A knower's trials differ (6 of 6: the check tells it apart). The matched sets measured again on the rendered audio, each form's carrier one recording (as the engine says them, every sentence's carrier its own: 10 of the 10 sentences of "where is the X?", 3 of 3 for each combination), and the eleventh round's voice refused pair by pair (68). Since P3's thirteenth round (`test_sim_lang` 70, failing on f7cde4c at its own first assertion): every child whose looks, reaches, turns, hits, pain and distress land on any tick after the test word's onset, the tick any function of the sentence it hears (so of any moment of its sound, however it perceives it), its looks on a thing the word never chooses (32 kinds of child for a pair and 29 for the name, one act or a look and an event, one fixed and one on the sentence's tick), each trial run with each draw at each of 32 ticks, in the timed voice and her real voice, stage 1 and 2: a trial and the same trial with the other thing named are both scored, and never both where the word sent them (for the name, at ticks within 5 of each other, since P3's fourteenth round the name's slot and the ear's tick; 140 pairs of ticks further apart are, the child's own difference, disclosed); a knower's are (the check tells it apart). The moments as a child perceives them differed in her real voice as the verifier found (her last 10 ms above -36 dB in 18 of the 21 birth-toy pairs and the name against every foil; her mouth's last opening above -30 dB, and her sound at its own ears above 21 dB SPL 0.7 m away, on different ticks in 12 of 21); since the fourteenth round her sound's stop is one moment for every pair and the name against every foil at each of those, and within the test word's slot they differ (her last 10 ms above -36 dB there in 20 of 21 pairs, her mouth above -30 dB and her sound at its ears above 21 dB SPL on different ticks in 12 of 21: C75), and the twelfth verifier's children rebuilt from the slot's moments (looks 17 to 17.6 ticks after its last 10 ms above -36 dB, after its ears last hear it above 21 or 33 dB SPL or after her mouth last opens above -30 dB within it, a hit 6 ticks after that before a look, a turn to her face 18.5 ticks after her mouth last opens above -31 dB or her last 10 ms above -36 dB within the name's slot) reach "understood" in no more lives than her draws alone give a child on one thing in every window (2 of 12 in the timed voice, 2 of 6 in her real voice), where the rule before scored every life (12 of 12, 6 of 6). Since P3's fourteenth round (`test_sim_lang` 71, failing on 13fb5dc at its own first assertion): on the rendered audio (her real voice and the timed voice) each form's sentences are the same outside the test word's slot, sample for sample, the slot under its ceiling (its least margin at the clip 1.21 dB for "where is the X? see?", 1.83 for the name), and the first and last moment above every level from -90 to 0 dB (0.5 dB steps) one moment for every word in 12 measures (10 ms by 10 ms, her mouth's tick, and either ear 0.3 and 0.7 m ahead, 0.7 m to either side and 1.5 m behind), the ears' onsets the same outside the slot's reach; the name and its foils at one loudest 10 ms (within 0.001 dB); every child with a favourite and two looks, one at a fixed tick and one timed from her voice's start or her sound's stop at 11 levels and measures, over 7 delays, 3 fixed ticks, either look on the favourite and ties either way (2,016 in her real voice, 1,344 in the timed voice), 360 gated name children and 3,168 children each with one act of any kind (a look or a reach on either thing or her face, its voice, its token, a hit, pain or distress) timed from her sound's stop so perceived at every other offset from -4 to +30, a pair and the name, in each, ends each trial the same with the other thing named; over 12 lives each, the thirteenth verifier's children reach "understood" no more often than her draws alone give (the drum child in 2 of 12, her draws alone 2, on all 21 pairs 1 of 12 against 1; the bear and cup children 0 against 2; the timed voice's cup child 0 against 2; the child that learns its delay from her smiles 3 and 1 of 12, a fair coin's trials' bound 5; the gated name child 0 against 2; no other word past the coin's bound), where on 13fb5dc, measured the same way, each reached it in 12 of 12; a view lost, or a look first read, on the onset's own tick scored, one read the tick before void and unscored; an older save's record started again. What stays, disclosed (C68, C75): the test words are matched on the tick grid, in pitch and in their loudest 10 ms, and outside their slot sample for sample, not in their own sounds within it, so a child whose choice of thing depends on how a word sounds tells the words apart by their sound, which the paradigm credits and a lab's matched recordings allow too; with one recording a word, a child with a favourite and a look timed from a moment within the slot can pass for one word (a car child timed from its last 10 ms above -36 dB within the slot, "car" understood in 12 of 12 lives where her draws alone give 2: C75). Since P3's fifteenth round (A60b; `test_sim_lang` 70, failing on f5ed8cd at its own first assertion): the window's share counts only how many of its ticks are on each thing, never when (8 ticks on the ball at the window's start, at its end, 4 and 4 apart, four of 2, or with 6 before and 6 after it: each 8 of 8; 6 on each thing in any order: 6 to 6); 3 ticks on either thing void, 4 scored; every child whose looks, reaches, turns, hits, pain and distress land on any tick after the onset, a function of the sentence it hears, its looks on a thing the word never chooses, in both voices, stage 1 and 2, a pair and the name, alike with the draw turned over (7,808 trials each way), a knower's and a name-knower's not; the twelfth verifier's children, one look timed from a moment within the slot to the old window's close, void whenever the word that moment puts later is said (fewer than 4 ticks), so never testable (0 of 6 lives each). And (`test_sim_lang` 72, failing on f5ed8cd at its own first assertion) the lead's acceptance: 39 kinds of child that know no word, 12 lives each, 0 of 468 lives understood; knowers detected; the fourteenth verifier's children keyed on one band of the child's own ear disclosed, their trials not the same with the draw turned over (C75b).
    - **A probe that cannot run** within 400 ticks (its things not placed, her body not settled, the display not holding) is dropped and logged, never said (`consts.TRIAL_WAIT`); once its two things were brought into view (the present act not refused) its never-taught items count as presented all the same (the ledger's `displayed`: her follow-in naming may label a thing in its new place). Her trial's line leaves her carrying no object (`Say.line.refs` empty): W2 and the world hear its words and are told nothing of which thing is named.
    - **The name test** (Mandel, Jusczyk and Pisoni 1995): its name ("hi. pip. hi.") or a foil name, a stress-matched name she never uses for it or anything ("hi. viv. hi.", "vib" or "pew", `consts.NAME_FOILS`: none within edit distance 1 of any of her 128 words, the only lines she says that are not her words, never Claude's), matched to "pip." in her voice as measured (its stimuli since P3's twelfth round: the name and each foil at her natural rate, their contours within 8% of the name's, since its fourteenth in one carrier phrase, "hi." before and "hi." after, each at one loudest 10 ms (C66 closed); with the words channel labelling her lines the name's token and the foils' letters are not one timeline, so the name test runs only while it is silent, C68; P3's tenth round, of the 993 such consonant-vowel-consonant names: each clip 5 ticks as the name's, its energy 99–103% of the name's, its rise to half its loudest 10 ms 100 ms as the name's, its median F0 242–276 Hz against 250; its loudest 10 ms, said alone, 90–93% of the name's (C66: in her trials each is at one); the ninth round's "tib.", "vek." and "jem." ran 6 ticks and 109–176% of its energy, so a child drawn to short or quiet sounds would have turned after its name more), drawn by the same stream, in the same voice (the calling register) and the same stillness, no things, from where her face already rests in its periphery; said only while the child can see her and is not looking at her face (the display's condition, before the sentence). The measure (the lead's decision A60b) is its face's share of the window: of the window's 22 ticks (the name's onset + 2 to + 23, the same whichever is said), those on which her reading of its head line is on her face (F), after its name against after a foil; every name trial is scored (no looking can void one: her body, the child's pain, distress or hit, and a tick not read can), and the old span a foil's "no turn" was held to past the window (`TRIAL_EAR_TICKS`) is retired with the turn it timed. A turn timed from her voice's start or her sound's stop as a whole lands on the same tick after the name and after a foil (the thirteenth verifier's gated child, "pip" understood in 12 of 12 lives on 13fb5dc, 0 of 12 since; `test_sim_lang` 71, 72). A child that turns to any voice turns after both, its share alike. A child that looks at her more after its name than after a foil, however it tells them apart by their sound within the slot (the name's own sound: band by band, where it starts and stops at its ears), is credited, as Mandel et al.'s method credits any differential response to its name (C75, C75b).
    - **No feedback** (the lead's decision A60b, after 535e32b): she gives nothing for a trial: no smile, no confirm, no line about it; her face holds its neutral set through the window and after it, and nothing she does after it reads which thing was named, as infant labs give no feedback in test trials (Fernald et al. 2008; Golinkoff et al. 1987). A rewarded trial is trainable by construction: under 535e32b, where her smile followed a trial whose looking went more to the target, children keyed on one band of the child's own ear that redrew their delay whenever a trial with their favourite brought no smile reached "understood" in up to 8 of 12 lives (the drum's 167 Hz band 8, the bear's 1684 Hz 7, the cup's 136 Hz 1), and now in 0 of 12 each (`test_sim_lang` 72). Her smiles and her teaching stay in her everyday asks, which never count (A28's "never withheld, never added" is theirs). What her pacing reads of the trials is the ledger's "understood" alone (the next word, the combination's gate, the call's smile), which a child whose looking does not depend on the word reaches in at most 1 life in 100.
    - **The ledger's rule** (the lead's decision A60b), at level 1. A trial scores for its named word only when its thing is a fresh never-taught item: a place, an angle or an exemplar on its first 3 presentations (as either thing, counted in trials by the ledger and outside them by P4), a combination on its first 3 trials, whichever is named (its twin shown beside its original each time; A28; `consts.NOVEL_PRESENTATIONS`; P3's eleventh round: counted only when said, its fresh trials had depended on her coins). A trial scores for each of its two things that is fresh, whichever was named (its word's yoked trial when the other was): its registered trials. Each registered trial gives q, its thing's share of the window's looking at either thing (T / (T + D) when its word was said, D / (T + D) when the other's was; `ledger.perm_test`). The word maps to its thing (level 1, `Ledger.maps`) when the mean q over the trials naming it exceeds the mean q over the trials naming the other (the same thing, the foil condition: the design's "or its yoked rate where higher", A19's "the same body's own rate", the M6 exemplar test's stated chance) at one-sided p < 0.01 by a permutation test over the draw labels: every way of choosing which k of its n trials named it, each as likely, the p the share whose difference is at least the one seen (exact, every relabeling counted, up to 36 trials, `consts.PERM_EXACT_N`; past them 20,000 relabelings or 20 over the level drawn from one fixed stream, p = (1 + those as far) / (draws + 1), valid at any count: Phipson and Smyth 2010). The permutation test is the invariance property: which of a trial's two things is named is a fair coin her trial stream draws fresh, so for any child whose looking does not depend on the word said (a favourite of either thing at any strength, one that grows or shifts, a side's, a follower of her gaze or hands, a child timed from her voice's start or her sound's stop as a whole), its looking is the same whichever was named, its labels are exchangeable, and the difference is null by construction, no rate estimated. A life re-tests a word as its trials come, so the 0.01 is a life's, spent over its tests (`consts.UNDERSTOOD_P`, `UNDERSTOOD_FIRST`; C64's "alpha spent over the looks", ours): its first at its 12th registered trial at 0.005, then at its 24th at 0.0025, its 48th at 0.00125, each over all its registered trials so far, a test taken only where a perfect separation could reach its level (1 / C(n, k) under it: at 12 trials when 3 to 9 named it), so such a child reaches "understood" in at most 1 life in 100 however long it lives (a union over the tests, each exact); kept once reached. The name: its face's share of the window, F / 22, after its name against after a foil, the same test and tests. Under the ninth round's rule a voice-turner's name was understood in 15 of 40 lives, under the tenth's (two binomial tests on its last 20 trials at 0.05, re-tested after every trial) a fair coin's trials reached it 23–63% of the time (C64); now every child whose looking does not depend on the word, among 15 kinds (voice-turners at 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, rising, falling or stepping, one that turns again once smiled at; favourites at 0.6–0.95, shifting or rising; one that repeats what her smile rewarded; one that looks where the last word sent it), at one fixed test at 0.01 at most at its level, and over a life's tests at 12, 24 and 48 trials in at most 1% of lives (`test_sim_lang` 65). Disclosed, the yoked comparison's own limit: a child that knows only the other word looks at the other thing when that is named and at either when this one is, so this word's difference is positive and it is credited (in every life, `test_sim_lang` 65); the lab's comparison has the same limit, and the old rule's (i) (its first looks on its thing when named, above 1/2), which held it, is gone with the first look. It holds at level 1, "maps"; level 2's never-told foil closes it for "understood" (below; A60b 9). Each word's standing keeps its registered trials in order and its tests (`Ledger.test`), and `Ledger.pooled` gives section 12's test of a form: over the form's scored trials, each once however many of its things were fresh, x = (T − D) / (T + D) each, their sum by the flip test over the draw labels (every trial's draw turned over or not, all 2^n as likely; `ledger.flip_test`; the name form by its permutation test), one-sided at p < 0.01 (a form whose trials are too few for a perfect score to reach 0.01, fewer than 7, is "not testable yet"), its voids counted beside it.
    - **Two levels** (the lead's decision A60b, after 535e32b; Waxman and Booth 2001, and the category-label comprehension literature). Level 1, "maps": the test above over the word's record, trials of its trained thing ("place": a known thing in a place or at an angle it has never been seen in). Level 2, "understood", for an object noun (a word with exemplars of its kind): the same test passed also in its **new-exemplar block**, a separate registered block of "exemplar" trials, each a new exemplar of the named kind (A53's calendar) never named by her before the probe (no line of hers has had it among its referents: `Ledger.was_named`, fail-closed for an older save with no file to read them from) and fresh on its first 3 presentations (A28), with the same window, void rule, statistic and permutation test, the same flip invariance (a non-knower's exemplar trials alike with the draw turned over), its levels spent as level 1's (its 12th registered trial at 0.005, its 24th at 0.0025, ...). **Its control is a never-told foil** (the lead's decision after 200e57a, the skeptic's design in `docs/audit/first2_word_clause.md`; A60b 9): she sets the new exemplar down with two things of two other words (`consts.KIND_DISTRACTORS`), the three in a row in an order her stream draws (each of the 6 as likely), and says "where is the X? see?" or, by her stream's fair coin, "where is the F? see?", F a word she never tells, of X's written syllables (`consts.WORD_FOILS`: zeb, fep, gub; tuma, modi, zibo; `NOUN_SYLLABLES`), drawn by the same stream and made on the form's one timeline in her voice like every sentence the draw could give (`trial_lines.json`'s foils, `tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial-foils`); q is its new exemplar's share of the window's looking at the three (T on it, D on either other; void under 4), after its word against after a foil. So a child that knows only the other words, turning from what it can name at any word it cannot, looks alike after both and passes no word there by exclusion (`test_sim_lang` 73: understood in 0 of 12 lives, where on 200e57a, against the distractor's known word, the same child was understood in 12 of 12). While the word scaffold labels her lines, a word's token and a foil's letters are never one timeline on the words channel, so exemplar probes are dropped then, as the name's are: level 2 is testable once the scaffold is silenced (A29). A child keyed to one particular thing (by the test word's sound, band by band, or by knowing that thing alone) passes level 1 and fails level 2; a knower of the kind passes both (`test_sim_lang` 72, 73, 12 lives each in either voice: a knower of the kind whose looks at a foil spread over the three understood in 12 of 12 at 12 trials, one whose look at a foil goes to one of the three in 0 of 12 at 12 trials and 10 of 12 at 24; a knower of its trained things alone and the child keyed by its ear's 167 Hz band to the drum mapping in 12 of 12 and understood in 0). The name, the colour words and a combination's key have one level, their own test ("understood" when it passes; the name's one level the paradigm's limit, A60b 11); the combination's gate (A28) reads "understood", so its noun must pass level 2. Since the lead's decisions after 67741fd (A60b 12–14): its **block is 24 registered trials** (`consts.KIND_FIRST`: its first test at its 24th at 0.005, then 48 at 0.0025, ...; A53: at least 8 exemplars a tested noun, a void's presentation spent and a further exemplar drawn when needed), its **foil matched in exposure** (one foil a registered noun, `consts.NOUN_FOILS`; her chatter carries it at its noun's running rate, "oh. F." with no act and no object named, only while the child attends and holds nothing, `Conduct._foil_chatter`; a probe runs only while the foil has been heard at least its noun's hearings less 10% or 2, `FOIL_EXPOSURE`, each trial logging both; ours, disclosed: the standard studies use novel foils, ours are matched-exposure nonwords), and its **things matched in newness** (the conduct draws each trial's three from the room's new exemplars: its noun's and one each of two other kinds presented exactly as often, fresh and never named, `Conduct._exemplar_set`, each noun's pool serving as targets and as others). A child that turns from what it can name only at a word heard often enough now meets its foil as familiar, and a knower of its trained things that looks at the newest thing when its thing is absent finds nothing newer than another (`test_sim_lang` 73: each understood in 0 of 12 lives); a knower of the kind is understood at 24 trials, 9 of 12 lives when its look at a foil goes to one of the three things, 12 of 12 when it spreads over them. What stays open is disclosed under A60b.
    - **Her everyday asks are her teaching, recorded as teaching only.** Each is judged, for her smile, as before: by the word X (either colour twin answers "where is the ball?", A28), from the tick X has been heard, never from the line's start; void when an X is where she reads it looking, in its hand or reached toward then, or out of its view, or in her own hands, or (a give) none within its reach; a turn or a reach before the word is no answer; the call judged from the end of its name, void when the child cannot see her, from the hall not judged; a give's act done before its word void, another toy given missed; a name ask met only by a word begun once its question was heard. Its outcome goes into the ledger's record of asks, which paces nothing and claims nothing: no ask counts toward "understood", "says" or any milestone.
    - This is her ledger, which paces her words. It is not a claim of understanding: every claim rests on section 12's tests, one-sided p < 0.01 on fresh items, pooled by A19's rule, with the word scaffold silenced for word claims (A19, A29, A55). Its tests, at a word's 12th, 24th, 48th, ... registered trial at 0.005, 0.0025, ..., spend 0.01 over the life, so a child whose looking does not depend on the word reaches it in at most 1 life in 100 (A60b; the tenth round's window of 20, re-tested after every trial at 0.05, was reached by a fair coin's trials 23% of the time over 60 trials, 40% over 120 and 63% over 240: C64, closed). The instruments, never she, read the fovea.
  - **says:** the child says X with X where she reads it looking, in its hand or reached toward (A40), or right after doing the act; not within 10 ticks of the parent saying it, and never begun or ended while an ask of hers is open (from its line to its window's end, even once it is judged) or while a trial is (from its sentence to its window's end), whatever the word: "mama" on answering her call, another word to "what is this?", the distractor's word in a trial's window (teaching only, A60; P3's tenth round, where only the ask's own word had been kept out); 3 times over at least 2 life days. Anything said within those 10 ticks counts only as an echo: toward nothing in her ledger, neither "says" nor "understood" nor a met ask (a name ask answered by an echo is void), though she may smile at it (4.3). "more?" at the meal is the feed's own line, a question with its expectant pause, never an ask (P3's third round: it had been a name ask, so an echo of "bottle" one tick after "more bottle?" was a met ask worth 2).
    - Said by the silent token output: its token, or letters within edit distance 1.
    - A word with no referent or act ("hi", "more") counts only in context: among the words she expected at that moment (A27's set; every word her ear accepts from the tract is, while a token's letters read as a word she did not expect are not: "z" read as "a" never counts, P3's fix).
    - Said by the tract: accepted by the parent's ear in context (section 4.9). The ledger keeps the two apart.
- **"Learned" means understood.** Saying the word is the next rung, and using it in a never-taught way is the test of understanding (section 12).
- **Growth pace.** About 12 content words are active at a time.
  - The next word enters when at least half of the active set is understood.
  - At least one new word every 2 life days, at most 3 a life day and 1 a minute (A14; the conduct holds both, whoever asks). The day's new words are each kept until the night, when they join her words together (P3's fix: a second new word no longer displaces the first).
  - A new word is said sentence-final, in 3 lines within a minute (one variation set, its lines of distinct words: 4.5), on the line's pitch peak and lengthened in every line of the set, whether it ends in ".", "?" or "!", and only in frames measured to put it on the peak (A34; 4.4: measured on all 331 birth lines by ending, the word 1.14 times as long and its F0 1.30 times the same line's unemphasized, 1.32 and 1.30 times the plain line's; the set at 2.40–2.83 words a second).
  - The growth queue's introductions (`tools/sim_voice_check.py --growth`; since P3's third round each word's lines on its pitch peak, A34, for each object "fell" is shown on and each colour on the room's toys of it, never its held-out twin: 81 words or word and object, 353 lines, the word emphasized, in the new-word register; P3's fourth round, with the tie rule and the colours' new frames): 2.20 words a second pooled on average, 1.38–2.92; any 3 of a word's lines of distinct words, a set she may draw, at most 2.97 (C25), once "mouth" lost "the mouth." and "the mouth!", with which 3 of its lines ran at 3.19 (`body/tests/test_sim_lang.py` test 32 checks every such set against the table's spans).

### 4.9 The child's voice: the vocal tract, and the word scaffold

The owner's decision 5: the voice is an articulatory vocal tract, so the child babbles, hears itself through its two ears, and can learn to match the parent's words. The word and letter channel stays as a scaffold at birth and is removed later. The first word from its own tract is a milestone.

**The tract** (`body/sim/tract.py`). It is the voice effector's physics, run on the world's side like MuJoCo. The body sends one step per articulator per tick and hears the result only through its own ears.
- **A source-filter articulatory synthesizer in numpy,** in the manner of Sondhi and Schroeter (1987). The sources are made in the time domain, and the tube's response is applied in the frequency domain.
  - Ten articulators set a 24-section tube 12 cm long (a child's), plus a nasal branch, updated every 10 ms.
  - Each articulator moves toward its target with damped muscle dynamics, so one tick's move finishes within 57–120 ms.
  - The tube's losses (viscous, thermal, soft walls), lip radiation and the nasal side branch follow Flanagan.
  - Airflow is quasi-static: the glottis and the narrowest point act as orifices in series. So voicing stops by itself during a closure.
  - One turbulence law (noise grows as Re² − Re_c² above Re_c 1,800) makes the breathy /h/, hisses and release bursts, at the glottis and at the narrowest point.
  - The voice is glottal pulses with pitch (180–546 Hz, resting about 265 Hz), jitter, shimmer and a breathy-to-pressed spectral tilt.
  - A breath reservoir (400 cm³: about 2.6 s of speech, refilled in 0.8 s at rest) makes breath groups.
- **No phoneme is written anywhere.** Vowels, the nasal murmur, hisses, bursts and a stop's silence all come from the tube's shape and the airflow.
- **Calibration** (all ours, disclosed):
  - the four corners of the tongue's range were fitted to children's vowel means (Peterson and Barney), with each constriction held at its anatomical place. The results: /i/ 344 / 2,719 Hz, /a/ 938 / 1,625 Hz, a rounded /u/ about 375 / 1,250 Hz;
  - an honest gap: the vowel space is about the size of an adult woman's. Front vowels fall short in F2 (2,700 against 3,200 Hz) and low vowels in F1 (about 900 against 1,030 Hz);
  - levels: /a/ at the parent's speech level, ANSI S3.5-1997's normal vocal effort, 62 dB SPL at 1 m (A26); a hiss sits at −14 dB and /h/ at −13 dB relative to /a/.
- **Cost** (measured on this Mac while other sessions shared it):

| case | ms per 150 ms tick |
|---|---|
| sounding (vowel, glide, fricative), a quieter moment | 1.8–3.0 |
| sounding, under heavy load | 7–10 |
| at rest | 0.12 |
| its own voice to both ears | +0.37 a sounding tick (0.75–0.95 under heavy load), +0.05 at rest |
| the babble average (6% of ticks sound), under load | about 1.6 |

**The alphabet and the gate.**
- **The voice effector is 10 articulators:** lungs, glottis (which also decides voicing), pitch, jaw, tongue front/back, tongue height, tongue tip, lips, rounding and velum.
- **Each takes one of {−0.6, −0.2, 0, +0.2, +0.6} of its range per tick,** from where it is (the servo law): 50 per-joint striatal rows and one softmax per articulator.
  - The steps were enlarged on purpose. The first steps, ±0.1 / ±0.3, could not open the lips in one tick; in real speech the lips and tongue tip cross their range in about 70 ms.
- **The gate** is born like the others (`birth_act` 0.25). Its own inputs: its own act last tick, the forward error on its hearing, and breath left.
- **At rest the targets relax to a silent resting posture** (nose breathing, lips nearly closed, the velum down), with a time constant of 1 tick, so a rest means quiet.
- **Pace.** One articulator target per tick gives at most 3.3 syllables a second, about canonical babbling's rate. The accepted words took 4–6 ticks (0.6–0.9 s).
- **The born babble,** measured with movement units (seed 7, 4,000 ticks; seed 8, 12,000 ticks):
  - 6–7% of ticks sound, and only 21–22% of the ticks when the gate is open;
  - 6.4–7.8 utterances a minute, averaging 3.6 ticks;
  - 0.6–0.7 closure-then-voiced-opening transitions a minute (canonical-like syllables, by chance).
  - The born babble is sparse breaths, squeals and glides. So the first thing the voice must learn is to make sound at all, which stage 1's smile for a vocal turn rewards.

**How the child hears itself.**
- The tract is a software organ driving the G1's own loudspeaker, as the software fovea keeps the body stock. That the real G1 has a speaker, and where it sits, is from memory and still to check (B5).
- In the sim the sound comes from the head's front and is spatialized to the two microphones.
- It goes through the same cochleas and brainstem (channel 2). Its own voice arrives about 19–20 dB above the same sound from 1.5 m in front (the exact sphere's level for a source on its surface: +19.8 dB at 500 Hz, +19.1 dB for a held /a/, 78.7 dB SPL; the earlier "about 14 dB" was the prototype's 0.3 m level floor), and reads at the midline. There is no bone conduction.
- An act at tick t is heard at t+1, as with the limbs.
- The tract's 10 positions and velocities, plus breath left, join the body channel (21 numbers).

**Vocal learning within the laws** (proposed; only the inverse model's starting point was measured):
- **The voice declares the ears as its consequence sense,** as the limbs declare their joints, so this stays body-general.
  - Its forward half (the stream plus the step → the next ear code) and `act_inv` (the ear codes at t and t+1 → the steps) learn from its own babble, labelled by the efference copy.
  - The forward error tells its own sound from someone else's.
- **The parent's words are demonstrations under the arm's law.** While the voice rests and its ears change in a way it did not predict, `act_pred`'s target is `act_inv`'s reading of that change, weighted by `act_inv`'s reliability.
  - This is R6's rest law with the ears as the voice's consequence sense, nothing more. It cannot tell speech from other sounds and is never told: her words, a toy's squeak and a footstep are all read as acts it could have made. It acts from birth, and it is the only imitation route.
  - The mapping is learned from its own babble, so there is still no mirror module (A10).
- **Reward comes only through the face:**
  - stage 1: +1 for a vocal turn;
  - stage 2: smiles for words the parent's ear accepts: right names, met asks, and context approximations (a recast, then a smile until the exact form has been said 3 times).
  - Nothing rewards sounding like the parent. A songbird-style inner "matches the tutor" signal was refused as an intrinsic reward, and stays refused (A56).
  - **The tract's gate does carry the songbird's performance error** (A41; Gadagkar et al. 2016): the forecast's belief in the act it made against that act's usual belief, in the gate's credit only, never the reward. It compares the tract with its own past, not with the parent, and it is zero-mean once its expectations catch up (3.5).
- **The born cry** (A47) sounds through the same tract on pain, and the tract's own acts override it. The child hears its cries, as it hears its babble.
- **The measured starting point** (`t_imitate.py`):
  - a linear inverse model trained on 16,000 babble ticks (1,082 sounding) fits its own voice poorly. Held-out R²: glottis 0.92; lungs, pitch and lips about 0.5; jaw and tongue 0.2–0.37;
  - it read 12 of the parent's words into echoes with a median rank of 22 of 50 (chance 25.5). The parent's ear accepts 2 of 12 in context ("see" 100%, "ball" 28%).
  - So early imitation rests on the cortex learning to hear across voices. `act_inv`'s reliability is measured on its own voice, so it overstates on the parent's.
  - A method for the parent: echo the child's babble back (Goldstein and Schwade 2008).

**How the parent hears the child's words** (`body/sim/parent_ear.py` and the transcriber `body/sim/lang/transcriber.py`, built in P3 on the child's own cochlea; `tools/sim_parent_ear.py` builds and measures it).
- **Hearing the tract.** The child's sound reaches her head at 1/distance (she attends to its voice, as a person does by where it comes from; no other source is mixed in, and there is no room echo until B9). A tick sounds to her when any band of her cochlea is over her 10 dB SPL threshold; a turn ends after 2 quiet ticks (4.6), and its sound goes to her ear with the words she expects at that moment. A turn begun while she spoke is the talk-over (4.6).
- **Features:** the child's own cochlea (`ears.cochlea`: 1.00 ERB, calibrated in Pa²) → log(band power + her threshold) → the frames within 25 dB of the loudest → a shift of 0–4 bands (the parent adapting to a shorter tract) → cepstra c1–c12, mean-normalized → slope-constrained DTW (Itakura).
- **Her templates:** each word said alone by 11 voices: her own at plain and approval pitch, the same synthesizer at the old child pitch, and 8 other macOS voices (Flo, Sandy, Shelley, Eddy, Reed, Junior, Kathy, Fred) at their own pitch and the plain rate (0.25; ours, the study's rate was not recorded). Each clip is made through the life's voice cache (`VoiceCache.clip(..., prosody=)`), so every template is in its ledger: 550 for the 50 birth words, in 20 s. The queue's words are made before birth too (`tools/sim_parent_ear.py --words all`, the default since P3's fix: 1,408 templates for the 128 words), and heard only once they are hers; a word her ear has no templates for is never introduced, and at each night the conduct checks her ear knows every word she has.
- **The bank:** the babbler's utterances as she hears them (the transcriber's own turns, at 1 m): 241 from 12,000 ticks of the voice study's babbler (seed 8; a unit of 1–8 ticks with probability 1/14.5 a tick, each articulator's step drawn and held). It stands in for W4's babbler, which records the bank before birth (P3v); every figure below is this stand-in's until then.
- **The decision rule** (A27, and P3's δ). A word c is accepted when all hold:
  1. c is the nearest of the words the parent expects in this situation;
  2. d(c) − d(the child's own babble bank) < −m (A27);
  3. c is the nearest of all her words, or d(c) − d(that nearest) < δ (P3).
  - Exact: accepted, and c the nearest of all her words. Approximate: accepted, and not exact.
  - m and δ are set per expected-set size by one principle: a sound that is not the context word passes as the context word at most 2% of the time (for m, the held-out babble, seed 7, 4,000 ticks, 93 turns, 200 random sets each; for δ, the 8 other voices' words she does not expect, each voice's own templates held out, 80 random sets each).
  - Both are fixed before birth and hold as her vocabulary grows (P3's fix, the ear built with all 128 words): m over sets drawn from the 120 words she can come to have (the 8 function words no line ends on never enter) comes out more lenient at every size (−4.50, −4.65, −4.55, −4.85, −4.75, −5.05, −5.15, −5.30), so the birth words' m, the stricter, is kept (the tool takes the stricter per size); δ only tightens as words are added, since "the nearest of all her words" can only come nearer.
  - Why δ: under the first two alone, a clear word she did not expect passed as an expected one on 36.2% to 94.9% of trials (sets of 1 to 8 words), so the child saying "ball" while looking at the duck would be heard as an approximate "duck", recast and smiled at. The babble bank tells speech from babble, not one word from another. With δ such a word passes as each context word 2% of the time, the bar babble meets (1.9% of trials for a set of one word, 16% for a set of eight), while the words she expects still pass 95%.

| expected-set size | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| m | −4.15 | −4.20 | −4.20 | −4.40 | −4.40 | −4.40 | −4.45 | −4.85 |
| δ | 3.30 | 3.35 | 3.55 | 3.55 | 3.65 | 3.90 | 3.90 | 3.90 |
| held-out babble passing as the context word (both tests) | 1.5% | 1.6% | 1.4% | 1.9% | 1.7% | 1.9% | 1.7% | 2.0% |
| the other voices' words, expected: accepted | 95.8% | 95.6% | 95.6% | 95.5% | 95.3% | 95.2% | 95.1% | 94.8% |
| the other voices' words, not expected: accepted as another word (with δ) | 1.9% | 3.5% | 6.0% | 7.6% | 9.6% | 13.1% | 14.1% | 16.0% |
| the same, without δ (A27's rule alone) | 36.2% | 57.7% | 70.3% | 79.7% | 85.4% | 89.0% | 91.8% | 94.9% |

- **How well it recognizes, as measured** (on this cochlea): the 8 other voices, each held out, are nearest of all 50 words 91.5% of the time (the study, through its own cochlea: 72–96%); in context their words are accepted 94.8–95.8% (without δ 96.2–98.8%), exactly 91.5%.
  - A fixed distance cutoff fails (the study): where 1% of babble passes, 0% of the other voices' words do.
  - The bank is recorded from the babbler before birth and kept fixed. Adding rejected near-words in life would lock those pronunciations out.
  - The study's figures through its cochlea (m = −0.35 on 193 utterances, 94–100% within the synthesizer) are superseded by these. "2 of 12 echoes" (above) is still the study's: P3v measures it again on this ear. The tract's reach is measured on this ear below.
- **Cost** (this Mac at load about 6, shared with other jobs): 143 ms per utterance on average (median 144, 95th percentile 296, most 398), at the utterance's end, on the world's side; with the stand-in babbler's 9.3 turns a minute, 3.4 ms a tick on average (listening on every tick: 0.04 ms). Only the expected words and the bank are scored unless the bank test passes; then her other words (for δ and exactness). A turn longer than twice her longest template is refused unscored (no template can match it). Measured again on the ear with all 128 words (P3's fix, load about 5–7): 69 ms a turn (median 72, 95th percentile 152, most 180), 1.63 ms a tick; the P3 verifier's rebuild measured 109 ms and 2.57 ms; P3's second fix, at load about 6 with five other jobs running, 128 ms (median 127, 95th percentile 255, most 371) and 3.02 ms a tick. `verify()` with the life's pin takes about 18 ms, at load and at each night. Her ear with all 128 words (1,408 templates) has the digest c81c8df05d3c14f6; with the birth words alone, 1a417934237721fe (both unchanged by the second fix, which records how she hears, `method_digest()` 884e6ea3593663ab, in the ear's file beside the digest, not in it). Costed with her vocabulary at its most (P3's third round; the verifier found the exact test costed against 50 words, and it grows toward 128 as her words do): on the held-out babbler's turns, a turn whose bank test passes costs 174 ms at 50 words (the exact test over the rest 82 of it) and 333 ms at all 128 (240), at load about 5; over the stand-in babbler's stream the bank test rarely passes, so her ear costs 2.2 ms a tick at 50 words and 2.1 at 128 (a turn's 95th percentile 220 and 261 ms, most 318 and 432); were every turn to pass it, 7.7 ms a tick at 128 and 4.0 at 50, at 9.3 turns a minute. In lockstep, wall time only.
- **Chance:** 2% of the babbler's turns per context word (the margins' definition): at the stand-in's 9.3 turns a minute, about 0.19 accepted approximations a minute per context word (at the born babble's 6.4–7.8, 0.13–0.16), written down as chance.
- **The tract's reach, on this ear** (P3's fix; `tools/sim_parent_ear.py`, the reach section): the voice study's instrument productions for 9 words (its hand scores and its searched acts, written by us and never given to the body; the acts were searched against the study's ear, not this one) through the committed tract (seed 1), heard at 1 m, each in 50 random expected sets of each size 1–8 holding the word; accepted, mean over the sizes, with δ as fixed / with A27's rule alone (no δ), and its rank among the 50 words (the word nearest of all):

| word | hand score | its rank (nearest) | searched acts | its rank (nearest) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mama | 100% / 100% (exact) | 1 | 100% / 100% (exact) | 1 |
| see | 100% / 100% (exact) | 1 (see) | 92.8% / 92.8% | 2 (car) |
| up | 91.2% / 91.2% | 2 (foot) | 86.0% / 86.0% | 3 (uh) |
| bye | 93.2% / 93.2% | 2 (a) | 0% / 0% | 9 (a) |
| no | 0% / 0% | 11 (uh) | 90.5% / 90.5% | 2 (oh) |
| ball | 0% / 0% | 17 (uh) | 84.2% / 84.2% | 4 (uh) |
| hi | **0% / 80.8%** | 4 (cup) | 0% / 0% | 28 (uh) |
| duck | 0% / 0% | 15 (a) | **0% / 38.5%** | 15 (the) |
| pip | 0% / 0% | 16 (foot) | **0% / 37.5%** | 16 (it) |

  - **What δ costs, for the owner to see:** δ was set on clear synthetic voices, and on the tract's own productions it removes "hi" (hand, 80.8% to 0%), "duck" and "pip" (searched, 38.5% and 37.5% to 0%), each heard nearer another of her words by more than δ. Only "mama" and "see" are ever exact; every other accepted production is approximate. So as it stands δ hides the child's name ("pip") from her ear. It stays the teacher's method under its disclosed principle (2%, the bar babble meets), and it is not tuned to let the tract through (that would fit the rule to the child). δ stands only once P3v has measured it again on W4's bank and measured this table again with it (C27); the table is the stand-in babbler's and the study's instruments until then.
  - The study's figures through its own cochlea (its table, superseded): mama 100% / 100%, ball 0% / 100%, duck 0% / 100%, up 100% / 0%, see 100% / 0%, no 0% / 82%, pip 78% / 0%, hi 68% / 0%, bye 0% / 0% (hand score / searched).

**The scaffold, and removing it.**
- **Input (channel 0):** a fixed born table of 50 word tokens, 26 letters, a space, rest and end (79 rows), shared by the ear and the output.
  - A word after the first 50 arrives as its letters, one a tick from its onset, then a space. There are no new rows during life.
  - Tokens arrive only while the parent is audible.
- **Output (effector 1):** the same 79 rows as a silent effector with its own gate. The parent reads it as an exact transcript, matching a letter string to a word when it is exact, within edit distance 1 (2 for words of 6 letters or more), or a prefix of at least 2 letters of what the child sees or holds. It replaces the synthesized child voice.
- **Words change the world** (from either output):
  - a toy's name said in a pause brings that toy;
  - "mama" brings the parent close;
  - "up" starts the pull-to-sit;
  - "more" gets more of the feed or the game;
  - "no" stops the parent's offer;
  - "peekaboo" starts a round.
- **Removing the input scaffold,** on a copy, at a night boundary. The removal only silences the channel: it reads "no token", as on every tick the parent is silent, and nothing in the body changes, so it is a change of the world, not the body (A29).
  - An earlier draft gave the words head a new target after removal: the voice's inverse model's reading of what was heard. That reading is articulator steps, not a word, so it cannot be a words target; and a rule for "heard speech with no token" needs the body to know which sounds are speech, which is world truth. It is dropped. After removal, the words live in the ears alone, which is what the two tests below measure.
  - Remove it when both hold:
    1. the words channel's own forecast (today's `latent_pred`), made on the tick before each token arrives and so from the sound alone, names the parent's word for at least 70% of the birth words;
    2. a copy day with the words channel silent keeps at least 80% of the token day's comprehension hits and right names.
- **Removing the token output** (ours, on a copy at a night boundary), when all three hold:
  1. the input test passes;
  2. at least 10 words reach "says" from the tract alone;
  3. a copy day without the token output keeps at least 80% of the right names.
- Re-test every 5 life days after M6 begins.
- **Risk:** tokens are the easier road to reward (risk 10).

**The milestone M6t, "first word from its own tract"** (section 12):
- the ledger's "says", judged on the tract's sound alone with the tokens ignored: accepted in context, not within 10 ticks of the parent saying it, 3 times over 2 life days;
- chance is its own rate with the referent absent, plus the babbler's 2%;
- a precursor rung, "first accepted echo", shows the imitation route working;
- its never-taught test: the word used in a never-paired place, or used to get the toy.
- Not forecast.

### 4.10 Its conduct: the behaviour system

The owner's decision 6 calls the parent the environment's most important part. Its conduct runs in four layers.

| layer | runs | does | latency after the child's act |
|---|---|---|---|
| L0 motion | every physics step | her muscles toward her interpolated plan, each within her strength; her trunk carried by its capped support (A25b); capped-spring holds; the yield (A4) | — |
| L1 reflexive | every tick | gaze to the child's act, to a sudden event, or to its gaze target (each in her attention log as it happens, and none while an ask is pending or a formal trial holds her still: 4.3, 4.8, A51); the face from the feelings (in her attention log as it moves); a hand withdrawn when hit | gaze ≤ 2 ticks (0.3 s); face ≤ 1 tick |
| L2 conduct | every tick, on events | judgments; replies; follow-in naming; offers and guides | voice 3–5 ticks; a hand act starts within 7 ticks (1 s). In lockstep, the solver's wall time costs no sim time. |
| L3 episode | at boundaries | the day plan's episode and its routine | — |

**Every tick, before her plan is carried out** (A25b): her planned pose is raised off the floor; her planned legs, trunk and head are measured against the child's body and legs where they are now, and nearer than 3 cm she straightens and moves back off it; beside the child she moves her legs only while its limbs near them are still (the calm step, at most 3 s a phase).

**Priority**, highest first: the child's pain (concern and comfort); being hit (withdraw); the child's vocal turn (reply); finishing her word; judging a pending ask; joint attention; the episode's act; idle (watch, at most one line per 40 ticks; a registered noun's foil line takes the slot when one is due, P4's idle line otherwise: A60b 13, 16, `Conduct.idle_free`, `note_idle`).

**Contingency.**
- Every outward act of the child gets her gaze within 0.3 s, unless she misses it (below).
- A judged act gets the smile and the approval word within a tick.
- A vocal turn gets a reply 3 ticks after it ends on average, often echoing and expanding its babble.
- **She is imperfect, as a person is** (A52; her method, ours). Real dyads are coordinated only part of the time, and mismatch and repair are how an infant learns to cope (Tronick and Gianino 1986); 5-month-olds look longer at a view of legs that does not follow their own movement than at the perfectly contingent live view of their own (Bahrick and Watson 1985), which Watson read as a turn, after about 3 months, from the perfect contingency of the self toward the imperfect contingency of people. So she misses a share of its acts, her reply latency is jittered, and she has spells of distraction during her own tasks, each from its own seeded stream at rates read from the human data before birth and never fitted to the child's rates (C57). No miss ever touches a judgment she has made: a judged act still gets its smile within a tick. Built in P3's third round (`lang/consts.py`, `conduct.py`; her stream IMPERFECT_STREAM): her latency and misses as in 4.6; her copies below; her spells of distraction come with P4's own-tasks episode. `Conduct.turns` counts the turns with no judgment she answered and missed, for P6's ruler.
- P6's ruler: her answered share of the child's acts within 7 ticks, while she is within 3 m and not away, equals her declared rate within its binomial error (C24). It checks that her method is as written; it is not a target.

**Joint attention.**
- The child's target, as she reads it (A40; B14 answered): the nameable object nearest the line its head's camera faces, within the fovea's reach of that line (±38° × ±20°), held 3 ticks running (P3's fourth round: her Reader holds it, `Reader.look`, and the Percept carries only the held reading, so every rule reads it; the third round held it only for follow-in naming, and a one-tick flicker of her error onto a near toy, the tick its word was read, had earned a right name and a "says": test 41); or the object its hand holds, or reaches toward (the hand's path over the last 3 ticks closing on it). She reads the camera's line with a person's error, drawn per reading from her seeded stream (its size from a source on judging another's head direction, before birth: C58). A real G1 shows no eyes, so she reads what a person would see: its trunk, its head and its hands. The fovea's window stays the child's own and the instruments'. Built in P3's third round (`lang/percept.Reader`, her stream READ_STREAM; the Percept's `child_target`, `child_holds` and the new `child_reaches`): the world gives it the head camera's pose, the hands' places and the things' places, never the fovea's window; her error is 4.0° per axis (yaw and pitch, each normal), a mean angular error of 5.0°, as Poppe, Rienks and Heylen 2007 measured judging which target another's head faced (C58); a thing is "in the child's view" when it is before the camera's field about the line she reads; a hand reaches toward the thing its distance to which fell on each of the last 3 ticks, by 3 cm or more in all (ours). Her asks and their voids, the ledger's "understood" and "says", her right names, follow-in naming, expected words and Claude's situations read only these (`Percept.attended`).
- She looks there within 2 ticks.
- If her voice is free, the object is nameable, and it has not been named in the last 20 ticks, she names it with a variation set. Her eyes are on the object during the naming word only, then back on the child's eyes.
- **She copies its movements** (A52; her method, ours). Parents imitate their infants' acts, and being imitated is what builds the infant's own mapping from seen acts to its acts (Ray and Heyes 2011). When its arm or hand makes a visible movement (a raise, a wave, a shake, an open hand) while she attends, she makes the matching movement with her own arm, mirrored as she faces it, within 1–2 s, at a rate from the same data (C57). It earns nothing and asks nothing: a movement she shows it, which its own eyes may or may not use. Built in P3's third round: a raise, a wave, a shake or an open hand, reported by the world as an event with its side, while she attends it, is copied with her other side 7–13 ticks later (1–2 s), the next copy no sooner than an exponential gap of mean 10 s after it (at most about 6 a minute, as mothers match their infants' acts: Pawlby 1977, as cited by Ray and Heyes 2011 and de Klerk et al. 2019); none while an ask is pending (A51): a copy due then is dropped, not made late.
- She redirects ("look! the drum.", with a point) only after 40 ticks with no target. A redirect is a show, never judged as an ask and never a probe, so it may point (A51). Follow-in naming outnumbers redirects at least 2 to 1 (Tomasello and Farrar 1986). Built in P3's third round: the redirect intent and any Claude line naming a toy the child does not attend (as she reads it) are said only after 40 ticks with no target and while the life day's follow-in namings number at least 2 for each redirect, the one to be said counted; a Claude line naming only what it attends is a follow-in naming.
- Showing: the toy beside her own face at about 40 cm from the G1's eyes, shaken for its sound.
- With the tract: a babble her ear accepts as a visible referent's name is echoed and expanded within 3 ticks ("ball! the ball!"). There is no separate syllable matcher (A27).

**Scaffolding** (Wood and Middleton's contingent-shift rule, per task): after a success at help level k, the next attempt starts at k−1; after a failure, at k+1; every task starts at 1. Give and release alone has a level 0, a judged trial with no help, which comes before its scaffolding (below); the other ladders' level 0s give help and follow this rule. Every top level ends with her letting go. The smile comes only from the child's own completion.

**Give and release, reconciled with A51** (the lead's decision in P3's sixth round; this paragraph is the one place for it; amended by A60). The judged ask is level 0 alone: her open hand held out to the child and the ask, her eyes on the child, nothing else (4.3). Only after its 40-tick window has closed unmet does scaffolding begin, always at level 1 (a point at the toy), since level 0 is the judged ask and always comes first; the contingent shift acts within one scaffolding: after a failure at level 1 the next help is level 2 (a touch on the toy), and after a failure at level 2 she lets go. The next scaffolding, after the next unmet judged ask, starts at level 1 again, whatever the last ended in (k−1 from level 1 or 2 is never below 1). A met give at level 0 earns her smile at worth 2, a give after scaffolding at worth 1 (4.3's worth table); both are her teaching and count toward nothing in the ledger: "understood" comes from formal trials alone (4.8, A60), and her point or touch never enters one.

| task | ladder |
|---|---|
| reach and hold | 0: a toy at the reach boundary + 5 cm, on the side its fovea is on · 1: at 80% of its reach · 2: 20 cm from its hand, shaken · 3: touched to its palm (narration only, no smile) |
| roll | 0: she kneels where the roll would end · 1: plus a toy just beyond reach · 2: guides the far arm across (≤ 65 N) · 3: bends the far knee over (≤ 76 N), then lets go. She never does the whole roll. |
| sit | the pull-to-sit by the forearms, rising only with its own flexion (A9); the prop near upright, eased 100 / 70 / 40 / 20%, then hovering |
| on its front (its own roll; no tummy time) | 0: her voice from beside it · 1: a toy on the mat where its eyes look, under its chest · 2: after 100 ticks of distress, she turns it onto its back (A7). No lift is asked for: face down its eyes see only the mat (A22). |
| give and release | 0: an open hand held out to the child plus the ask, her eyes on the child: the judged ask · 1: plus a point · 2: a touch on the toy (levels 1 and 2 only after the judged ask's window has closed unmet: the paragraph above). She never pulls. |

**Routines** (Bruner's formats: a fixed opening, order and closing): the wake, play and show, winding down, goodnight (section 4.7; the meal is gone, A88), and her own day (tidying, the sofa, her own meal at the low table).

**Leaving and returning:**
- she leaves with "bye bye pip." and a wave at the door;
- she calls from the hall about every 600 ticks, spatialized;
- she answers a bid from afar within 5 ticks ("mama is here.");
- she returns early after 3 bids in 40 ticks;
- the reunion is "hi pip! mama is here.", arriving in the child's periphery, and the flash at its first look. No smile without an act.

**Being hit.** Her own pain threshold is 150 N (a 10 ms mean) on any segment. This is ours, a person's, not the child's 1,012 N. The G1's arm pushes 65 N static, and its swings exceed that.
- Stage 1: surprise, "oh!", and she withdraws her hand. No frown.
- Stage 2: a frown (−1) and "no.", but only for the child's own motor act. A reflex she triggered is logged as her defect.

**What Claude steers between minutes** (A14, extended; each "minute" is a steering window of 400 ticks since A59):
- **May:** choose the episode, the focus toys and which scaffolding task to work on; write situation lines and variation-set frames, each passing the line check; choose the day's new word and the away time; write notes; propose method changes, applied only at a night boundary after a test on a copy.
- **Must never:** set or nudge a feeling, the face, the gaze, a judgment, a worth, a help level or a force; write praise or use the approval register; loosen a criterion, or choose activities for their smile rate; request or time a formal trial (the day plan schedules them by stage, never by the child's rates: 4.8; a trial's sentence requested as an intent is refused); see the child's insides (the digest reports face events, not feeling numbers); act inside a minute or at night; exceed the word pace, or name what cannot be shown within the minute.

## 5. The world

### 5.1 The living room

- **The room:** 5.2 × 4.6 m, 2.6 m high, with an oak floor.
  - **The play mat: 2.8 × 2.0 m of foam, 7 × 5 tiles of 0.4 m.** That is about 2.1 body lengths of the G1, the same ratio the 1.6 m mat gave the custom child (B11).
  - A sofa against the back wall, a low table, cube shelves with baskets.
  - A window with curtains, a doorway to a hall (the house to come), a floor lamp, a plant, wall art.
- **Lights:** a sun (directional, casting shadows) through the window, a key spot and a fill. Their colour and direction change across the life day (section 5.4).
  - A ceiling spot light blacked out the ceiling in MuJoCo 3.9's renderer on this Mac, so it was removed; the ceiling is most of what a child on its back sees.
- **Textures** (A53). The toys, the furniture, the rug and the parent's clothes carry textures, so that a thing's identity is not its colour alone. In the first build only the floor, the window and her face were textured, and the eye check read the toys mostly by colour (the nearest mean 0.43 against a small readout's 0.85). A texture barely changes the render's cost (section 9).
  - The sun's shadow stays in the child's eyes. Dropping it would save about 21 ms a tick but take a piece of the owner's complete reality (decision 3). B3 is answered, and the shadow stays (A65), never decided by the eye check or by the tick.
  - The G1 file's own directional light (a Menagerie scene light, not part of the robot) is switched off at load.
  - **No headlight.** MuJoCo's headlight is a lamp at the viewing camera: for the child's eyes, a lamp shining from its own head, which the G1 does not have. It is off, and the eyes refuse to render with one (a verifier found it on; C3's first figures were measured with it). MuJoCo computes no bounced light, so each light carries the room's indirect light as its ambient term, a third of its own diffuse (ROOM_INDIRECT, ours: about the prototype's headlight ambient carried onto the lights).
- **The play space is the whole floor** (B7). The mat has no walls. The parent cannot carry the G1 back, so she comes to it.
- **The G1 in the file.** The room includes `assets/unitree_g1/g1_with_hands.xml` first, so its `stand` keyframe still addresses its own joints.
  - MuJoCo applies the last `compiler` element to the whole model, so the room is written in radians (every `euler` converted in one place), and the G1's mesh folder is given from `body/sim/`.
  - The room's contact defaults sit in their own class, so the G1's defaults are untouched.
- **Collision classes:** world 1 (floor, walls, mat, furniture); the G1 keeps its own contype 1 and conaffinity 1 (self-collision on, as shipped); toys 4; the parent 8, with conaffinity 1 so it touches the G1.
- **Physics:** MuJoCo 3.9, 2 ms steps with `implicitfast`, 75 steps a tick, **the elliptic friction cone, multi-point collision detection off, and impratio 10.**
  - With the pyramidal cone, a block squeezed in a Dex3 hand stopped MuJoCo ("FactorizeHessian: rank-deficient sparse Hessian"); with the conjugate-gradient solver or a dense Jacobian it gave NaN accelerations and a reset.
  - With the elliptic cone and multi-point detection on (MuJoCo's default), 8 of 360 grasp trials blew up to NaN about 0.1 s into closing the fingers, and MuJoCo silently reset them. With it off: 0 of 360, and the babble ran clean.
  - **impratio 10, chosen by the physics of these contacts** (A32), never by pain rates or by which toys can be held. Rubber fingertips on plastic toys, toys and the body on a foam mat, and motor housings on housings all hold without a slide below their sliding force. MuJoCo's soft contacts creep there by design (Overview, "Softness and slip"), and its documentation names the remedy: "using the Newton solver with elliptic friction cones and large value of impratio is the recommended way of reducing slip" (ibid.); "When contact slip is a problem, the best way to suppress it is to use elliptic cones, large impratio, and the Newton algorithm with very small tolerance" (Modeling, "Solver settings"; both the stable documentation, read 2026-09-24). Menagerie's hand and gripper models (the Shadow hand, the Allegro hand, the Robotiq 2F-85, ALOHA) ship elliptic cones with impratio 10.
    - Measured (`tools/sim_friction.py`): a box on the mat pushed at 0.3–0.97 of its sliding force slid 3.6–21 mm in 2 s at impratio 1 and 0.4–1.8 mm at 10 (a real one would not slide); the resting G1 pushed at the pelvis with 100 / 150 / 200 N slid 1.9 / 3.1 / 4.4 cm at 1 and 0.4 / 0.7 / 1.9 cm at 10; above the sliding force both follow Coulomb's law within 1%.
    - **Its cost, disclosed** (C5, C22). The convex contact model couples a slip to the normal direction ("the only way to initiate slip is to generate some motion in the normal direction", Computation, "Physical realism and soft contacts"). A pressed box that starts to slide is pressed 22–24% harder than its load for about 10 ms, at either setting. In the G1's hip housings, pressed together and slid apart by the withdrawal, friction raises the normal force at 10 (1,795 N against 1,553 N with the pair frictionless) where at 1 it lowers it (1,333 against 1,646): a pressed housing that slides reads harder at 10.
    - The noslip solver, the documentation's next step, was refused: its cascade "is no longer solving a well-defined optimization problem (or any other problem); instead it is just an adhoc mechanism" (Modeling, "Solver settings"), and it nearly doubled the tick.
  - The world catches `mujoco.FatalError`: two hands posed 3 cm into each other raised it inside `mj_forward` (A18). It also disables MuJoCo's auto-reset (`<flag autoreset="disable"/>`), so a bad state is never silently replaced. Built (W1): every tick checks MuJoCo's warning counters and the end state against MuJoCo's own bound (`mjMAXVAL`, 1e10) on positions, velocities and accelerations, with the tick's closing forward pass inside the catch; on any fault the world puts back the state the tick began with and raises `WorldFault`, so the fault never reaches the body.
  - The world's geoms (floor, mat, furniture, toys) take contact priority 2, so real frictions are set on the world's side (built; the surfaces' own values are still 1.0, C26).
  - **The mat's collision box reaches 10 cm below its top,** sunk into the floor. In a thin mat a 5 mm foot sphere was trapped at 1,900 N and gave pain on 151 of 400 babble ticks; with the deep box, 2 of 400, both real blows.

### 5.2 Toys and their sounds

Every sound comes from an event in the physics: a toy's own clip on its event, scaled by the impulse or speed; contact clicks; the parent's voice and footsteps (from its gait); the child's tract. Each source is placed in space for the two ears.

| toy | colour | size | held by a Dex3 hand? (3.8, impratio 10) | its sound |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ball | red | 12 cm | yes, 9 of 9; also pushed, rolled and named | a soft rubber bounce when it lands or is struck |
| block | blue | 7 cm cube | yes, 9 of 9 | a hollow wooden knock on contact |
| duck | yellow | 11 cm | yes, 7 of 9 | a squeak when squeezed |
| cup | green | 0.8 scale, 6.7 cm across (B1) | yes, 9 of 9 (and 9 of 9 at full size) | a plastic clink when it strikes something |
| rattle | purple | a handle and a head | yes, 6 of 6 hand-overs | beads shaking, louder with speed |
| car | orange | 14 cm long | yes, 8 of 9 | a wheel rattle while it moves |
| bear | brown | 12 cm | no, 2 of 9 (B1) | a soft bell inside when it moves |
| stacker | white, with coloured rings | 11 cm base | yes, 9 of 9 | the rings' plastic clack |
| drum | cyan | 15 cm | no, 0 of 9 (B1): hit | a boom when hit on top |
| ring (teether) | pink | 10 cm | yes, 6 of 6 hand-overs | a crinkle when handled |

- **Where they lie at birth:** around the G1 on the mat. The block, cup and rattle are within its lying reach. The rattle was moved 7 cm nearer the right hand, and the block and duck were moved out from under its arm and leg.
- **Colour twins** for the never-taught word test (B2): a blue ball, a red block, a yellow cup and a green car, each a copy of its toy in another colour, introduced before the colour words. The twins are the test's targets, and each colour word is heard on at least 2 kinds of toy before it (A28, A55).
- **A richer room** (A53; the lead's decision for the owner, whose room it is):
  - **Containers:** a hollow cup and open baskets built from box geoms, so that "in", a birth word, can happen.
  - **A cover:** a light cloth-like lid over a shallow tray that a Dex3 hand can lift (measured before birth, C60), for hiding games and M3's search test (section 12).
