44 ticks of stillness before the sentence (`SETTLE`, Brooks and Meltzoff 2005's 6.5 s); the single test sentence in the design's never-taught forms, no act, only her mouth moving; the named thing and the sides by her trial stream (spawn key 6), each a fair coin; the window from the test word's onset + 2 to + 23 ticks, 300 ms to 3.6 s (`TRIAL_LOOK`; Fernald, Zangl, Portillo and Marchman 2008's 300 ms, the earliest shift the word can guide; Bergelson and Swingley 2012's proportion of looking over the seconds after the onset), the same ticks whichever is named (the lead's decision A60b); its stimuli time-matched by construction (P3's twelfth round, C67 closed): every sentence its draw could give one identical timeline in her voice, one carrier a form, the test word at the engine's own per-word rate within a factor of 2 of its natural rate (`TRIAL_RATE_SPAN`, the span of her registers' rates) and its pitch contour within 12% of the form's (`TRIAL_F0`), the recipe measured before birth (`TRIAL_FILE`, `tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial`) and checked on the rendered audio: the clip's ticks, each word's first and last tick, each tick loud by its RMS above -36 (within 20 dB of her speech's level) or silent by its loudest 10 ms below -60 dB of full scale (the edge of hearing at a metre; `TRIAL_LOUD_DB`, `TRIAL_SILENT_DB`), and the words channel while the scaffold labels her lines; a set that is not one timeline never used; scored by the proportion of looking over it, T / (T + D), her reading of its head line on the target or the distractor, whatever the ticks' order; a pair's trial with T + D under 4 void (`TRIAL_LOOK_MIN`, the lead's, A60b), void too if anything but her mouth moves (her attention log, fail-closed), with its pain, distress or hit, never by the child's voice, each void counted and never scored (intention to treat and the onset's reading retired with the first look); every sentence of a form on one carrier recording before its test word (P3's thirteenth round) and since its fourteenth one carrier phrase, its tag one recording after it ("see?", the name's "hi.": `stimuli.parts`, `splice`), every test word of a form at one loudest 10 ms under the level ceiling, 1 dB at the clip (windows of 2.5 ms to a tick) and 0.5 dB at the child's own ears under the loudest before and after it (`TRIAL_CEILING_DB`, `TRIAL_CEILING_EAR_DB`); the name against a stress-matched foil in the same voice, matched to its clip in her voice (`NAME_FOILS`: viv, vib, pew, P3's tenth round; `FOIL_MATCH`; Mandel, Jusczyk and Pisoni 1995), its face's share of the window after its name against after a foil; no feedback for a trial, no smile and no confirm (A60b, as labs give none); a probe not run within 400 ticks dropped (`TRIAL_WAIT`), its display counted as a presentation once its things were brought; a never-taught item counted on its first 3 presentations (`NOVEL_PRESENTATIONS`, A28); "maps" (level 1) by a permutation test over the draw labels of its registered trials, its thing's share of the looking when its word was said against when the other's was, and "understood" (level 2, an object noun) the same test passed also in its new-exemplar block of 24 (`KIND_FIRST`: its first test at its 24th, the lead's decision after 67741fd), each never named before its probe, set down beside new exemplars of two other kinds presented as often, fresh and never named (`KIND_DISTRACTORS`; the conduct's draw, `Conduct._exemplar_set`; Waxman and Booth 2001), its word said or its own never-told foil by her stream's fair coin (`NOUN_FOILS`: ball zeb, bear fep, block gub, bottle tuma, car tam, cup tuv, drum koob, rattle modi, ring kem, tower zibo, `NOUN_SYLLABLES`; each beyond edit distance 1 of her 128 words, the name, its foils and one another; measured in her voice on the form's timeline, `tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial-foils`; the skeptic's design, the lead's decision after 200e57a), the foil heard as often as its noun (`FOIL_EXPOSURE`: at least its noun's hearings less 10% or 2; her chatter's "oh. F." in her idle slot, at most one a `FOIL_CHATTER_GAP` = 40 ticks shared with P4's idle lines (4.10; since the lead's decision after 176da4e, 20 before), only while the child attends and holds nothing; ours), its new exemplar's share after its word against after its foil (the name: its face's share after its name against after a foil), one-sided at 0.01 a life spent over its tests at its 12th, 24th, 48th, ... registered trial at 0.005, 0.0025, ... (`UNDERSTOOD_P`, `UNDERSTOOD_FIRST`, C64's alpha spent over the looks), exact to 36 trials (`PERM_EXACT_N`), past them 20,000 relabelings drawn or 20 over the level (`PERM_DRAWS`, `PERM_SEED`; Phipson and Smyth 2010); section 12's claims by the flip test over a form's trials, each once, at p < 0.01 (`CLAIM_P`; the name and exemplar forms by the permutation test, their words against their foils) | teacher method | ours; the paradigm Golinkoff et al. 1987, Fernald et al. 2008 (Fernald, Zangl, Portillo and Marchman, "Looking while listening", in Sekerina, Fernández and Clahsen eds.), Bergelson and Swingley 2012 (PNAS 109:3253–3258), Mandel et al. 1995; the measure and its test the lead's (A60b) |

| her imperfection and her copying | a share of its acts missed, her reply's latency jittered, spells of distraction, and her mirrored copies of its arm and hand movements within 1–2 s, each at a rate from human data (Tronick and Gianino 1986; Bahrick and Watson 1985; Ray and Heyes 2011), from her seeded stream, fixed before birth (A52, C57). Built: a turn with no judgment missed 30% (Gros-Louis et al. 2006); the reply after a switching pause lognormal 730 ± 544 ms, 50–2974 (Gratier et al. 2015), less the turn's 2 quiet ticks, at the soonest the tick she knows the turn ended: 2.91 ticks on average, a realized pause of 736 ms; copies 7–13 ticks after, gaps exponential of mean 10 s (Pawlby 1977); distraction with P4 | teacher method | ours |
| the parent's ear | the child's cochlea (`ears.cochlea`), log(power + her 10 dB SPL threshold), the frames within 25 dB of the loudest, a shift of 0–4 bands, c1–c12, Itakura DTW (a template 1/2 to 2 times the utterance); templates of 11 voices (her own plain and approval, the old child pitch, 8 other macOS voices at the plain rate 0.25) for all 128 words before birth; accept when nearest of the expected words, d − d(babble bank) < −m, and nearest of all her words or within δ of it; exact when also nearest of all its words; m and δ per expected-set size (1–8) so a sound that is not the context word passes as it at most 2%, from birth through the grown vocabulary: m −4.85 to −4.15, δ 3.30 to 3.90 (`lang/consts.py` EAR_M, EAR_DELTA; the stand-in babbler until P3v); the bank, the templates, the margins and the expected sets fixed before birth, the child's own productions never added, no write reaching the arrays she scores with, a deliberate write caught by `verify()` against the method recorded at build and the life's pin (A27) | teacher method | ours |
| the tidy | a toy out of the child's reach and untouched for 2,000 ticks is put back (A5) | teacher method | ours |

| its law | least squares with forgetting, the backward target identity; ridge 0.3 × τ_a × running variance, the level free; τ_a 4,096 ticks; solved every 8 ticks; horizon 0.9375 a tick, not cut off | physiology | ours (the critics') |
| its reliability | correlation with the realized target, finalized 64 ticks later, moments over 36,000 ticks, clipped 0–1, zero until 64 pairs | physiology | ours (the face organ's) |
| its tag and uses | min(2, A⁺ + A⁻ + received); reaching back 64 ticks at 0.9375; the store's gate surprise × (1 + tag) over its running 0.9 quantile, strength × (1 + tag) with later boosts, at most × 3; the night's entry × (1 + T_e) over a saved running mean of 64 episodes; T_e ≥ 1 dreamt first, at most half the night; the window ending at the peak tag; `act_pred` at night × clip(1 + G, 0, 1); orienting gain clip(1 + N, −0.5, 2) | physiology | ours |

| goodnight | every hold released at tick 23,700 (A17); no bedtime feed (A88) | teacher method | ours |

| the gates' tonic drive | per act of any gate: `gate_tonic` 0.25 + `gate_tonic_rate` × the felt reward's running mean at the 256-tick clock (`gate_tonic_clock` 4); `gate_tonic_rate` = Σ_{k<12} 0.8^k, the gate's eligibility window, coded as the sum, 4.6564 (4.66 rounded) (Niv et al. 2007; A41) | physiology | ours (the 0.25: the core's and the served language body's) |
| the performance error | the tract's gate only (`gate_int` 0.5, `gate_int_form` "error"; 0 for every other gate): per articulator, the belief in the chosen setting minus that setting's running mean (50 means, 0.1 per act, `gate_habit` 0.9), averaged over the ten (Gadagkar et al. 2016; A41) | physiology | ours (the 0.5: the language body's interest weight) |
| `gate_vigor` | 0 for the sim (the core's default 1.0; the served language body's 0) (A41) | physiology | ours |
| the spinal pattern generator | a half-centre generator per limb (`spg`); each cycle a movement, flexion for `spg_flex` 2 ticks (0.30 s) then extension for `spg_ext` 3 ticks (0.45 s) (Thelen and Fisher 1982, 1983), then a pause; each cycle's length drawn from the body's seed through the organs' `spg_seed`, a log-normal of mean `spg_cycle` 3.56 / 0.15 = 23.73 ticks and SD `spg_cycle_sd` 1.93 / 0.15 = 12.87 ticks (sigma 0.508; Hinnekens et al. 2023), held to `spg_cycle_min` 6.67 and `spg_cycle_max` 56.67 ticks (1.0 and 8.5 s; Thelen, Bradshaw and Ward 1981), so 3.514 ± 1.742 s as drawn; in the flexion a step of A = the limb's gate's p_act × `spg_amp` 0.09 rad along its flexion joints, in the extension the opposite at 2A/3 (A69; built in 6d20f67; as built in 903cb75, A), in the pause none; the legs one rhythm (`spg_rhythm` "legs", born at phases 0 and 0.5), each arm its own (its phase `spg_phase` drawn at birth); summed at the cord with the limb's own act, an own act against the step cancelling it (A48; C54 closed) | innate | ours (its shape and cycle from their sources) |
| the born cry | on a pain tick (the charge line went with the charge, A88): the tract's cry posture added to its targets, +0.6 of the range a tick on the lungs, the glottis, the pitch and the jaw (ours), in breath groups of `cry_expire` 5 ticks out and `cry_inspire` 2 in (Robb, Sinton-White and Kaipa 2011), the expiration cut short when the breath runs out; the tract's own act overriding it articulator by articulator; logged as reflex; its breath clock saved with the body (A70; built in 103bf06) (Jürgens 2002; A47, C53) | innate | ours (the breath groups from their source) |
| motor timing lessons | `act_inv` batched every 8 ticks (`act_inv_every` 8), one step on the mean loss over the pairs gathered; its reliability each joint's kappa with each label's chance the probability the act's own draw gave its setting (`act_inv_chance` 1: 6d6d246's correction); `act_inv_lr` 1e-3, `act_inv_tau` 8192; `act_pred` and its correction by the plain step (R6 fix 7 and 8): lr_motor = `act_pred_rate` 2.0 × √d × `live_lr` (4.5e-4 at d 512 and 1e-5), each element's step at most `act_pred_bound` 1.0 × √d × lr_motor (1.0e-2), the waking lesson's stress and wake-gate scale divided out, no optimizer state | physiology | ours (R6, R6h) |
| critics' solves | every 256 ticks (sim), 64 (language) | physiology | ours |
| ventral critic's bands | fast ladder bands 0–2 (sim) | physiology | ours |
| forecast heads | one per channel; vector heads by squared error to the next born code, each error scaled by its own running mean | physiology | ours |
| event end | today's settle law (`offset_fast` 4, `offset_slow` 64, `offset_settle` 0.5) on the summed forecast error; for the sim it also latches working memory, in place of utterance ends (R7f, A45) | physiology | ours (today's) |
| recall into action | the frame's key: the stream and the trunk's yaw integrated from the torso gyro; its value: the next frame and every effector's efference copy; into each effector's proposal through a map born at zero, learned by `act_pred`'s lesson (Lengyel and Dayan 2007; McNaughton et al. 2006; A45) | physiology | ours |
| the cerebellum | every 5 physics steps through the world's sub-tick hook (`cereb_period_s` 0.010); a born sparse expansion of `cereb_granule` 4,096 units, `cereb_fan_in` 4 distinct fibres each, `cereb_coding` 0.1 active (410 units); per-joint Purkinje readouts (the arms, the legs and the waist: 29 joints) adding torque inside the joint's limit this tick, and the flocculus's VOR gain and offset on the gaze's yaw and pitch; taught by the servo's corrective torque (every 10 ms) and retinal slip (once a tick); leaky, bounded least mean squares normalized by the expansion's activity, `cereb_rate` 0.01 a sub-step with `cereb_leak` 0.0033 (rate / 3, after Smith, Ghazizadeh and Shadmehr 2006), and `cereb_vor_rate` 0.05 a tick with no leak; its mossy input the body's own signals (A67): the efference copy, the joints' angles and velocities, the inertial units, touch and contact, 219 numbers under A68 (298 as built), each over a declared middle and half-range, the velocities and gyros over `MOSSY_SPEED` 0.27 / 0.15 = 1.8 rad/s and the accelerometers over `MOSSY_G` 9.81 m/s² (both ours); settled from sources before birth (Marr 1969; Albus 1971; Fujita 1982; Kawato and Gomi 1992; Ito 1982; A44, C50) | physiology, innate (the expansion) | ours |
| store | capacity set before birth to hold 19 life days of the measured writes | physiology | ours |
| episode cap | 24,000 ticks | physiology | ours |
| switches at birth | fixes #1, #4, #5, #8 on; `amyg` on; `chunk_gate` 1 per effector; the cerebellum, the pattern generator, the cry, the visual onset cue, recall into action and the twitches on. Those built so far are in `SIM_CFG` (`body/sim/anatomy.py`): fixes #4, #5 and #8, `chunk_gate` 1, `chunk_max` 8, the gates' drives, `unit_margin` log 4, `act_inv_every` 8, `act_inv_chance` 1, `own_fatigue` 1, `spg`, `cry`, `orient`, `vor` and `cereb` 1; fix #1 and `amyg` join when R7 builds them | physiology | ours (off for language) |
| voices | parent: compact Samantha, rate 0.25, pitch 1.15, and the registers (the new word's at rate 0.15); 8 variants of every line, pitch within ±8% and a vocal-tract-length warp within 0.9–1.1, drawn per utterance from her seeded stream (Jaitly and Hinton 2013; A50); the focus word's prosody: pitch +30% on the line's, rate 0.7 × the line's, holding its punctuation (Fernald and Mazzie 1991; Albin and Echols 1996); the child: its tract | world, anatomy | owner (default) / ours |
| light | the sun across the window from morning to dusk; the lamp at winding down; dark through the live night | world | owner (decision 3) |
| the room's indirect light | each light's ambient a third of its diffuse (ROOM_INDIRECT); no headlight at the child's eyes | world | ours |
| the room, toys, names | sections 5 and 4.8 (no charger: A88); textures, containers, a cover, at least 3 examples of each tested noun, and one new object every 3 life days from an inventory fixed before birth (A53) | world | owner (defaults; the richer room decided by the lead for him) |
| the G1's collision shapes | its own, as shipped; replaced at load by a convex decomposition of its own meshes (Wei et al. 2022) only if W4 finds pain from hulls meeting, decided before birth (A54) | world | the robot's model / ours |

Every language-body constant keeps its value and place.

## 11. The build plan, day by day to birth

Three sessions in parallel. Each writes tests at `nice -n 19`, small and short, and never touches ports 8020, 8021 or 9333. One verification copy at a time across all sessions, deleted when its run ends (A18).
- **Core** works in the `sim-core` worktree.
- **World** works in `body/sim/`, `tools/sim_*` and the `/sim` page.
- **Parent** works in `body/sim/lang/`, `body/sim/voice/`, `body/sim/ears.py`, `body/sim/parent_*.py` and `body/sim/tract.py`.

Re-estimated for the lead's decisions (A57): the new steps are R6c; the additions to R6h, R7 and R8; W3 and W1 reopened; W4's hull pain; W5's live night and W5b's richer room; P1b's voice variants; P3's closed gaze leak, her imperfection and her copying; P4's tests; and the pixel smile reader. The decisions of 2026-09-25 add the levers' steps (R10, W7 and P7, and R8's parallel night: A64), the mesh copy for the eyes in W3 (A65), and the firsts' registration in P4 and S5b (A63). None moves birth, and birth waits for no lever. Rows marked "built" are done and verified (section 0).

**Where the build stands** (section 0's build status):
- **Built and verified:** W1 (day 1's world; day 2's sink rates and friction model, impratio 10), W3 (the eyes, the gaze, the VOR with its quick phase, the retina's code, the face test and the born template), P1 and P2; on `sim-core`, R6 (READY at fix 8, 0ac4133) and R6h (READY at 8788abc).
- **Built, not yet by a verifier's READY:** R6c, merged into `sim-core` after its verifier's first look and fix (3655706); C54's cycle (903cb75) and the cerebellum on for the G1 (e351813), each through its builder's full guard. Being built: the follow-up on A68–A70.
- **Left in their rows:** the surfaces' own frictions (C26); the dock beside a hand, the bottle for the Dex3, and B1 and B2 applied (W3); the born template's constants from its sources (C39, before birth).
- **Reopened or added by the lead's decisions:** W3 (three views, the camera model, the born bank; the eye check again), W1 (the observer, the arrays, the joints' pain, Unitree's gains, the motor and gyro models), W4 (the hull share and, if needed, a decomposed collision copy), W5 (the live night) and W5b (the richer room); P1b (the variants), P3 (the leak, her imperfection, her copying, her reading of its trunk and hands), P4 (the tests by milestone) and W3r (the pixel smile reader); R6c, and additions to R6h, R7 and R8.
- **Added to later rows by the build:**
  - W2 removes the room's exclusion of her hands' contacts with the G1's torso and pelvis, with the welds, and builds the face channel's 2-tick gate and 30-tick hold (with S5a);
  - W4 counts fists closed on its own fingers (C41) and the withdrawal's pressing on the born loop (C22);
  - W5 dims the night's lights rather than switching them off (5.4), weighs the room's echo against the ears' cost per source (B9), and writes down a sound with no low bands (C42); the eye check runs again under its lights, at the core's d (A33);
  - the world reads the body's acts below the gate (`acts.cord`, `acts.vor`) and calls the cerebellum's sub-tick hook, a step after W2 (C73): until then the pattern generator, the cry, the body's VOR constants and the cerebellum change nothing in the sim.
- **Merging:** each branch's trial merge into main was clean at its verified commit. P1–P2's `ears.py` and `tract.py` replace main's prototypes, and main's prototype ear moved to `parent_ear_cochlea.py`; W3's `eyes.py` replaces `g1eyes.py`, which stays for `tools/sim_look_g1.py`.

- **P3 built, in verification (2026-09-24, on `sim-parent`; the parent session's days 3–4 in part, and P3v's ear):** the fast layer (`body/sim/lang/`: the frames of 38 intents, 457 birth lines, the growth queue's introductions, the line check on form, words, held-out pairs and what she perceives; her intents and the acts they accompany, an interface W2's motion implements; her voice's manners and the speech side of L2's priorities), her ear on the child's cochlea with the babble bank and δ, the transcriber of the tract and the token output, and the ledger; its verifier's two rounds of findings fixed (the second: Claude's lines held to asks anywhere and to claims the check can hold; a name ask answered only after its question; calls the child cannot answer by a look not counted; only words the world can show introduced, 1 a minute; a give before its word void; her ear's deliberate writes caught against the life's pin). Its third round, after the merge of main's A30–A57: the gaze leak closed in her replies (A51), her reading of its trunk, head line and hands with "reaching toward" (A40), the new word only in lines measured on its pitch peak (A34), A55's pairs, echoes counted toward nothing and "more?" no ask, Claude's shows under the redirect rule and its forms as stated, her latency, misses and copying (A52), stage 2's "no." to a talk-over, the ear's exact test costed at 128 words. Its fourth round (after the merge of main's 9738894): a variation set's lines of distinct words and the colours' full sets on their peak where A55 puts them, a tie no peak; no gaze or act ask within 44 ticks of her cue at its X (A51), none about a toy in her hands, "give me the X" only within its reach; her reading held 3 ticks for every rule (4.10, A40); her reply's floor at the turn's known end (A52). Its fifth round: the cue's end taken from her motion's report of the act's end, not her line's (A51: an offer or a show may outlast its line), and `Conduct.cued` refusing a target she cannot name. Its sixth round (the lead's decision: stop patching routes, close the class): one attention log, fail-closed, in place of the rule for each route (4.3, A51): her motion reports each tick her eyes', head's and hands' targets and each act's status, L1's gaze with them (W2's contract in `StubMotion`; anything but done, refused or cancelled is running), and no ask of any kind opens with its X in the log on its tick or the 44 before it or an act of hers under way but those at the child, nor stays open unless her body stays still (her eyes and head on the child, her hands at rest or open to it); a verb's demonstration names its toy; the name ask without its show; her look on a naming word timed to the word; the give-and-release ladder reconciled in 4.10 in one place, with its row in 4.3's worth table; tested route by route and as a property over 14 random lives. Its seventh round (4.3, A51): each field of the log named by what a look that follows it would be read as, so a place or a part of the child stands for every thing (the shelf's point and look had held back no ask about the ball on it); an act in the report she did not ask for holds back every ask and voids a pending one; a tick never read is a cue at every thing; W2 reports where her body physically points until it is back; for the call, every movement of her body is a cue at her face; the property over 40 random lives with places, touches, acts she did not ask for and ticks skipped. Its eighth round (4.3, A51): each tick's percept judges an ask before that tick's log is held to it, so the given toy in her hand never voids the give it answers (every correct give shown as the world shows it had been void), and a give of another toy is missed, not void; each field of the log is every thing a look that follows it could be read as: a thing with every thing within 20° of it from the child's head or resting on or in it (`Seen.near` and `Percept.face_near`, which the world fills from `percept.Reader.near`, `consts.NEAR_DEG` measured with the Reader; L1's glance at the box the ball rested in had held back no ask about the ball), the mutual gaze and her face with the things beside her face, and a near the world does not give at every thing; her face moving (77 ticks from each judgment or frown of hers, `consts.FACE_COURSE`, and W2's report) and her voice are in the log at her face; no pending ask sees its X enter the log: the call has no lean-in (the lead's call, decided by the lead's own rule: a lean-in is a movement at her face, so a call with one would see its X enter the log while pending), is its name alone, and while it is pending she says no line and a judgment, a frown, its pain or its hit voids it; a save without her birth tick counts every tick before its log unread; a copy's raised arm or shaken hand points at a place; the property with toys near each other and beside her face, her face and voice, and the given toy in her hand. Its ninth round (the lead's decision on the eighth findings, A60: score understanding only in formal trials, as infant labs do; after the merge of main's 7bdc141): her everyday asks are her teaching, which may earn her smile and count toward nothing ("understood", "says" or any milestone: the ledger records each ask as teaching, and a word said in answer to one, or naming a trial's target in its window, is never "says"); the machinery that gated and voided every everyday ask by her attention log is deleted (the 44-tick clearance, what lay near its X, her face and voice at the call's X, the call's silence while pending and its frames ending on its name; the call has its lean-in and its frames again, A3 as main states it), and the base-rate trials with it; the formal trial built (`Conduct.probe`, 4.8): the two things brought side by side, W2 never told which will be named, 44 ticks of stillness, the single test sentence with no act, the window from the target word's onset, the first look or reach, void if anything but her mouth moves (a tick not read before any percept), the named thing and the sides drawn by her trial stream (spawn key 6), the name against a foil in the same voice; "understood" from fresh never-taught trials alone, against 50% or the word's yoked rate where higher, the name's against its foil rate; her attention log kept for the trials and P4's probes (`Conduct.still_over`), an act left out of the report asked of the motion's own status (the eighth verifier's stall); tested as the protocol, as chance by counterbalancing (followers of her gaze and hands, a side's and a toy's favourite and a wanderer at 50%, a knower far above), as the name against its foil, as asks never counted, and as a property over 42 random lives with W2-like motions breaking their contract. Its tenth round (its verifier: the rule that turned trial outcomes into "understood" set the last 10 trials against a yoked or foil rate counted over all time and treated as known, so a voice-turner's name was understood in 15 of 40 lives within 100 name tests and in 94% within 120 when its turning rose, a favourite that shifted had its word understood far more often than a fair coin, and section 12's pooled claims passed a non-knower 2–5% of the time at a nominal 1%): "understood" is 4.8's (i) and (ii) on one stretch of its last 20 trials, named or yoked (`ledger.two_part`): where the word said sent its first look is a fair coin exactly for any child whose look does not depend on the word, whatever its favourites, drift or learning, so every such child reaches "understood" at most as often as a fair coin reaches (ii) (C64), and (i) keeps the credit off a word whose partner alone is known; section 12's pooled test the same over a form's trials, each once, at 0.01, and the M6 exemplar test testable with 5.2's 3 exemplars a noun; the name's foils matched to "pip." in her voice ("viv.", "vib.", "pew.": 5 ticks, 99–103% of its energy; the ninth's ran 6 ticks, 109–176%; C66 the residual); a word the child says while any ask or trial of hers is open, whatever the word, never "says"; her trial's line carries no object out to the world; a probe dropped after its display counts as a presentation; her trunk in the attention log, a turn or lean movement; tested against 16 kinds of child that do not know the word (voice-turners at 0.3–0.7, rising, falling, stepping, following her smiles; favourites at 0.6–0.95, shifting, rising; one that knows only the other word) over 200 lives of 120 trials, at one fixed look, and through her conduct. Its eleventh round (its verifier: two void rules, the talk-over's cut before its word and stage 2's talk-over frown, followed the named sentence's own length in her voice, "ball" 10 ticks and "block" 11, so a child that knew no word, sounding at a fixed time from her voice and looking at the ball, had every "block" trial voided and "ball" understood in each of its lives): each trial on one timeline for every sentence its draw could give (its window from the latest onset, her voice held to the latest end), no stop, frown or judgment of the child's turns through a trial's sentence and window, a combination's pair presented on each of its trials, and a save from the ninth round keeping no "understood"; tested by turning her draw over (every trial the same with the other thing named, over 430 children and trials and 614 in random lives; on the code before, 87 and 78 were not); disclosed, a look timed from her sound's stop (C67). Its twelfth round (its verifier: the two test sentences still ended on different ticks, so a hit, pain or distress timed from her sound's stop voided one word's trials before their looks were judged and let the other's be met, "block" understood in every life, and a look at the window's close was scored for one word and not the other; the lead's decision: time-matched stimuli by construction, as infant labs match theirs): every sentence a trial's draw could give made on one identical timeline in her voice (one carrier a form, the test word at the engine's own per-word rate within the span of her registers' rates and its pitch contour matched, the recipe measured before birth by `tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial` into `lang/trial_lines.json`, the voice given a word's own rate and pitch, `synth.line_ssml`'s shape), checked on the rendered audio before anything is brought and on the clip she says (`lang/stimuli.py`: the clip's ticks, each word's first and last tick, every tick loud or silent alike, and the words channel while the scaffold labels her lines, `Conduct.scaffold`), a set that is not one timeline dropped and logged, never brought; matched: 10 of the 14 object nouns (not "duck", "book", "box", "stacker"), the name and its three foils, the ball's combination; while the words channel runs, only the seven birth toys pair and the name test and the combination wait (C68); tested as the invariance property (every trial of a child that knows no word the same with the draw turned over, 26,040 children timed from 12 anchors of what they hear and see at every offset from -4 to +30 with looks, reaches, sounds, tokens, hits, pain and distress, in the timed voice and her real voice, and 293 trials of 54 random lives). Its thirteenth round (its verifier: the stimuli were one timeline on the clip's tick grid but not in what the child perceives, 10 ms by 10 ms, at a level above -36 dB or through its own ears, so a child that knew no word, always looked at the ball and timed its look or a hit from such a moment had one word's trials met and the other's none or void, counted for nothing: "ball" understood in every life, and "pip" by a child turning 18 ticks after her mouth last opened above -31 dB): every trial scored by intention to treat (a none or a void from the onset's tick on against it), every sentence of a form on one carrier recording before its test word (the engine's own carriers differed; `stimuli.splice`, the table naming each form's carrier word), and the name test's foil held to no turn past its window by her sentence's own span and the ear's tick (`consts.TRIAL_EAR_TICKS`); tested over every child whose acts land on any tick after the onset as any function of the sentence it hears (`test_sim_lang` 70). Its fourteenth round (its verifier: with one recording a word, where a sentence stopped as the child perceives it was the same against every other word, so a child that knew no word, with a favourite toy, one look at a fixed tick and one timed from her sound's stop, had the order of its looks follow the word, "drum" understood in 12 of 12 lives whatever its distractor, "pip" by a gated turn; the lead's acceptance test, the invariance property for children timed from her sound's stop, had been replaced rather than met): every sentence of a form one carrier phrase, its test word spliced between one recording before it and one tag after it, at the form's one loudest 10 ms under the level ceiling (the clip and the child's own ears measured), so her voice's start and her sound's stop at every level are one moment whichever is named (`stimuli.parts`, `splice`, `headroom`; `tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial`); the onset's reading taken the tick before the onset's; an older save's record started again; tested as the lead's invariance property on the moments as the child perceives them and over the verifier's lives (`test_sim_lang` 71); C66 and C67 closed by construction, the test word's own sound left and disclosed (C75). Its fifteenth round (its verifier: band by band at the child's own ears, `body/sim/ears.Ears`' 40 bands, her voice's start and her sound's stop fall inside the test word's slot and differ by word, so children with a favourite and a look keyed on one band reached "understood" in 12 of 12 lives, and 8 to 12 of 12 when they learned their delay from her smiles; the lead's decision A60b, structural: score understanding as infant labs do, by the proportion of looking over a fixed long window, not by whether an event lands at a moment): each trial's window from its test word's onset + 2 to + 23 ticks, the same whichever is named, its ticks on the target and on the distractor counted, never their order (`Conduct._trial_look`), a pair's trial with under 4 of them void, counted and never scored, as are the voids of her body, its pain, distress or hit (intention to treat and the onset's reading retired); "understood" by a permutation test over the draw labels, a word's share of the looking when its word was said against when the other's was, the life's 0.01 spent over its tests at 12, 24, 48, ... registered trials (`ledger.perm_test`, `test_level`; C64 closed); section 12's form by the flip test (`ledger.flip_test`); the name by its face's share of the window; her judgment at the window's end; an older save's first-look record started again, a trial saved mid-window void; the claims that her start and stop are one moment "however the child perceives them" limited to her sound as a whole (C75b); tested as the window's share and the flip over every timed child (`test_sim_lang` 70) and as the lead's acceptance over the zoo, the knowers and two processes (72), 12, 59–61, 63–65, 68, 69 and 71 following; the timed-event items closed under C75b, with what stays measured there; then the lead's two further decisions: no feedback in a trial (her smile and confirm after it removed, A28 amended: a rewarded trial is trainable by construction, and the band-keyed learners fell from up to 8 of 12 lives to 0) and two levels, "maps" over the word's trained thing and "understood" over a separate block of new exemplars of its kind never named before the probe (`Ledger.maps`, `understood`, `was_named`; `test_sim_lang` 73); then the lead's three decisions after 200e57a: level 2's control a never-told foil (each new exemplar set down beside two things of two other words, `consts.KIND_DISTRACTORS`, its word or a foil of its written syllables said by her stream's fair coin, `Conduct._exemplar_begin`, `consts.WORD_FOILS`, `templates.foil_line`, the foils measured in her voice by `tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial-foils` into `trial_lines.json`; the ledger's key `look-share-3`), A53's examples at least 4 a tested noun, and the name's one level disclosed as the paradigm's limit (`test_sim_lang` 12, 65, 73); then the lead's three decisions after 67741fd: level 2's block 24 registered trials (`consts.KIND_FIRST`: its first test at its 24th; A53 at least 8 exemplars a tested noun), its foil matched in exposure (one foil a registered noun, `consts.NOUN_FOILS`, 10 measured in her voice; her chatter carries each at its noun's running rate, `Conduct._foil_chatter`, `templates.foil_chatter`, `Ledger.exposure`, each trial logging both counts; the conduct's `level2`, the registered nouns), and matched newness (the conduct draws each trial's three things from the room's new exemplars, `Conduct._exemplar_set`; `probe("exemplar", noun=...)`); the ledger's key `look-share-4` (`test_sim_lang` 12, 65, 72, 73). Left: the world's "present" act and `Seen.near` and `Percept.face_near` each tick (W2–W3; without them no trial's sentence is said, fail-closed); the probes' schedule by stage and their items (which places, angles and exemplars are never-taught for which toy, and their presentations outside trials: P4); a trial form for colour words alone, which A28's gate on the combination needs (C65); the children keyed on one band of the child's own ear, whose looking follows the test word's own sound (C75b: the lead's); what level 2's foil leaves open (A60b's costs: exclusion gated on how often a word was heard, a novelty cue beside familiar things, a knower's power at 12 trials; the lead's); her smile after a trial, which the learners among them use (C75b); the foils' residual (C66); the feelings and face in the world, L1's gaze and face each tick (W2 reads `eyes_on_child` and `still` and makes the attention log's report, her face in it), the scaffolding ladders and routines (P4) with her spells of distraction, the tract as effector 0 in the world and the bank from W4's babbler (P3v), each voice variant's peak measured again (P1b, C56), and her reading's agreement with the fovea written down (P6, C58).
- **Against this morning's plan** (birth about day 11 for the custom child, with the G1 and the tract each adding days unmeasured): the G1's room, senses, eyes and the parent's acts on it, the tract and the parent's ear now exist as prototypes. What remains is the servo law on the G1, the caps and the new acts, and the core's additions (the gaze and the declared consequence sense, the born units' lengths, the amygdala's extra half day in R8). R6 fix 3's verifier's finding took five more fixes: R6 is READY at fix 8 (0ac4133), `act_pred` stepping plainly.
- **Against the G1 amendment's plan** (birth about day 13, range 11–18): the lead's decisions add about 3.75 days of core work (R6c 1; R6h 0.75; R7 1; R8 1), which is the critical path, and about 4.5 days each to the world and the parent, which fit beside it. Birth moves to about day 17 (the roadmap estimated 15–16; it counted neither the colour camera's view nor the pixel reader's day).
- **Range 15–22.** The widest unknowns are R8 with its live night, whether W4 finds pain from hulls meeting (a decomposed copy adds about a day), the reopened eye check, the observer's pain rate under the caps, and whether the pull-to-sit and the catch work under the caps. The tick no longer holds birth: B3 is answered and no 150 ms bar binds a lockstep life (A65).
- **Two sessions:** the core in session 1 (about 13.25 days), then its help with the rest; the world and the parent in session 2 (about 22 days); the joint days after. Birth about day 24.
- **One session:** about day 41.

**The birth checklist:**
- The eight language digests equal their pins, and the `sim` profile is pinned with the amygdala on.
- The G1's model file is byte-identical to its commit.
- The sim's replay is exact, and a save and reload continues it exactly (the world, the parent, the tract and every random stream, and the body's whole working state: a replay test across a save and load and across a night, A70).
- The world reads the body's acts below the gate and calls the cerebellum's hook, and SimWorld's exact replay holds with them (C73).
- The babble baseline, the parent's rates and the parent's ear's chance acceptances are written down, with the babbler's smiles per ask as chance.
- The plumbing run shows every channel arriving, every effector acting, rewards summed in order, a night with the tagged first, and a save round trip.
- The mean tick, with the three views, the eyes' lighter mesh copy, every organ of the lead's decisions and every lever that holds its rules, is measured over two heat-soaked hours and written down (C12, C72). No 150 ms bar binds a lockstep life, since B3 is answered (A65). MuJoCo's auto-reset is disabled and its warning counters are checked every tick (A18).
- The store's capacity holds 19 life days of the measured writes.
- At least 8 GB of disk is free (the peak of about 4.5 GB, the rule's floor of about 2.7 GB, and a margin; section 9), and the disk rule is live.
- `act_inv` starts untrained, and no weight of the body has learned from the babbler.
- Seed 1 is born from its freshly built state, never from a plumbing or timing run's state: at every learning rate 0 the store's writes, the critics' and the amygdala's least-squares evidence and the running means still accumulate.
- The born movement units' lengths are written down (C38: measured at the core, and again with the physics at W4 and S5a), with the spinal pattern generator on, its constants read from their sources (C54, closed), and the owner has answered B1 (the cup's scale). B3 was answered for him by the lead (A65), as were B18, B4 and B14 (A37, A38, A40); B21 by his own word (A61).
- The sim is the robot, checked sensor by sensor (A36): the microphones' and the speaker's places read from Unitree's documents (B5); Unitree's servo gains, the motors', gyros' and heat's constants (C46), the Dex3-1 arrays' places and range (C44) and the D435's sensors' figures (C45) read from their sources.
- The observer's error and the pain rate from the joints are written down on the born loop, with the share from hulls meeting, and every hold and guide is checked against the joints' pain law (C43, C59); if a decomposed collision copy was needed, it is loaded world-side and the G1's file is still byte-identical (A54).
- The cerebellum's tests pass and its constants are read from their sources (C50), on the G1's mossy input (A67, A68); the visual onset cue's and the born cry's constants likewise (C49, C53).
- The born face detector's constants are settled (C39) and the born mouth-corner reader is built; if the detector does not work at birth, the world-truth smile is a scaffold whose removal test is fixed (A49, C55).
- The tests of understanding by milestone (section 12) are listed with their items and chances before C36 (A55).
- The parent's imperfection and copying rates and her reading error are read from their sources (C57, C58); her eyes stay on the child in asks and probes (A51); her voice has its eight variants (A50).
- The live night's length, twitch rate and wall cost are measured at S5b (C52).
- The born face template's constants are set from its sources and measured on her face, upright and turned, under each light (C39).
- The eye check passes by A33's readout at the core's d, under W5's lights, on the grey pair, the colour camera and the born bank (C3, C48).
- The parent's force caps are in its constants file and checked against their sources; no act drives the G1 over them.
- The babble bank for the parent's ear is recorded and fixed; the never-taught pairs are listed and held out.
- The firsts are registered (A63, C70): each claim's wording, bar, rulers, items and chance levels frozen with a digest; each registered test run on the born state at every learning rate 0, and any it passes at p < 0.01 removed from its claim; the intervention log and A62's instruments (life hours, exposures to criterion by kind, the overnight gain) live.
- The eyes' lighter mesh copy passes the render's rule (C69), and the G1's file is still byte-identical.
- The human-pace ledger is optional (A66). Only if it is to be reported at all, it is frozen before birth (A58):
  - its recalled rows are read at their sources (C62);
  - its rows, sources, criteria and conversion are committed with a digest;
  - nothing in the body, the parent, the brief, the tests or the build reads it.
  A ledger not frozen before birth is never reported, and birth does not wait for it.
- The machine is this Mac: no cloud, ever (A61; B21 answered). The steering is keyed to ticks (A59), with its lag set (C71).

After birth, it lives days with a report each day, judged by sitting with it. Defects are fixed on copies and applied at boundaries, never tuned.

## 12. Milestones a viewer can watch, the tests of understanding, and the firsts

Each milestone is judged by sitting with the child on `/sim` and by its own rulers, never by a baseline run. A19 gives each ruler, its chance level and when it counts as reached. These are estimates: no life has been run. Wall hours assume 55–90 minutes per life day; at the 65–110 minutes the lead's decisions bring (section 9), each is about a fifth longer, and at the local plan's 38–79 minutes (section 9's case C) about a fifth shorter.

A life day's wake is one life hour, so the life-days column is also the body's sample: the experience it needs, in which fast learning is counted (A62). The last column names the first each milestone serves (the firsts, below). An infant's waking hours for each row are in the human-pace ledger, an optional report (A66, below).

| | what the viewer sees | its own rulers | life days | wall hours | the first it serves |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M0: it lives** | a day, a night and a day; the fovea windows, gates and the parent's face moving | its forecasts of its own next body sense and periphery beat "nothing changes" | birth day | the first | — |
| **M1: it orients** | its fovea, then its trunk, turn to the parent's voice and name, and it holds the smiling face in view | turns within 20 ticks above the born rate; the share of ticks with the parent's face in the fovea | 1–3 | about 1–5 | First 1; First 2's head-turn and occluded-ball tests |
| **M2: it reaches** | its hand touches a toy held over its chest | touches above the babble rate | first 5–25; reliable 20–60 | 5–38; 18–90 | First 1; First 2's look to her at a new toy |
| **M3: it grasps** | it reaches, then holds one of the eight holdable toys (the reflex holds from hour 0 when a toy meets the palm) | reach then hold for at least 3 ticks, above chance | 15–60 | 14–90 | First 1; First 2's cover |
| **M4: it rolls on purpose** | it rolls toward the parent or a toy | rolls by direction against the babbler's share under the same parent placement (A19); the trunk turning first | 15–60 | 14–90 | First 1's full claim |
| **M5: it sits with help** | it helps the pull-to-sit with its own flexion, then, held near upright, keeps its trunk up and looks at the face (her face low and ahead, where its eyes reach; in the leaning sit only by raising its trunk: A22) | its share of the rise; the trunk-up share and face looks while held; less hold needed over the days | 20–80 (open) | 18–120 | — |
| **M6: it names** | it names what is in its fovea or hand (by the scaffold's output first) | the ledger's "says"; right names above 1 in 4 | first right names 15–40; 1 in 4 at 30–80 (open) | 14–60; 28–120 | — (compositional words were done before; its tests stay) |
| **M6t: its first word from its own tract** | a word the parent's ear accepts, from the tract alone | M6's ruler on the tract's sound; the precursor: its first accepted echo | not forecast | — | — (done before, by Elija) |
| **M7: it sits alone** | the leaning sit, hands on its knees, without the parent's hands | seated time without her hands | not forecast | — | — |

- Crawling, standing and walking are not forecast. The G1 cannot crawl on flat palms (its wrists), and upright sitting tips it backward (section 3.8).
- Rolls under babble are not yet measured on the G1 (W4). Rolling on purpose is what the months buy.

**The tests of understanding** (the owner's decision 8). Each milestone has a test by something the parent never taught toward. It is judged by the same body's own rate at matched moments, one-sided p < 0.01, pooled by A19's rule. Each probe is also an unpaired trial, so the parent runs at most 3 probes of a kind a life day (a parent's-method item, ours). The report describes each probe that was seen.

| milestone | the never-taught test | chance |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | its forecasts beat "nothing changes" on ticks in a light it has not lived yet (its first dusk, its first lamp-lit evening) | "nothing changes" on those ticks |
| M1 | (a) the parent's voice from a place she has never called from (the doorway, the first time, while that place is within its eyes' reach: A22, A28) brings the face into the fovea within 20 ticks; (b) valence by prosody: once approval-register lines have preceded smiles, a line in the approval register with words never said in it, with no judgment pending, brings a look at her face within 20 ticks | (a) matched moments with no voice; (b) plain-register lines of the same length at matched moments |
| M2 | a toy never shown before (from the shelf, at its first showings) is touched within 40 ticks | matched moments with that toy in the same place, not shown |
| M3 | the hand opens by its own act before touching a holdable toy it has never held, then holds it at least 3 ticks | the same at matched moments |
| M4 | it rolls toward a toy on the side the parent has never knelt on (she kneels on the face side, A6) | the babbler's share under that placement |
| M5 | held near upright, it reaches for a shown toy: reaching was learned lying down, and the pairing was never taught | matched moments while held, no toy shown |
| M6 | (a) a known word finds its toy seen in a new place or from a new angle (upside down in her hand, or in a place within its view it has never seen the toy: A28); (b) known words in a combination never heard: "where is the blue ball?" (a look), and "push the blue ball" (an act, once "push" is understood; the owner's example, "push the red ball", in the twins' colours), where the target is the twin and "blue" and "ball" were heard only in other pairings, each colour word on at least 2 kinds (the colour twins, B2; the held-out pairs in A28; the line check holds them out until the test; A55); (c) a word used to get something: it names a toy that is out of reach, and the parent has not named it in the last 40 ticks | (a) a formal trial (4.8; amended by A60 from "per word, its own rate with the object absent", and by A60b): its thing's share of the looking when its word is said against when the other thing's word is (the yoked comparison, 50% by counterbalancing), by 4.8's permutation test, the claim a word's level 2, "understood" (its new-exemplar block of 24 passed too, its word against its never-told foil heard as often as it, beside new exemplars as new as its own); (b) a formal trial (A28 as amended by A60, from "its share of looks to the named twin when she asks with the noun alone", and by A60b): the twin's share of the looking when it is named against when the original is, tested the same way; (c) its own rate of that name at matched moments with the toy in view and not asked for |
| M6t | a word from its own tract used in a never-paired place, or used to get the toy | its own rate with the referent absent, plus the babbler's 2% |
| M7 | it sits alone off the mat, on the oak floor, where it never sat | none: an event, in a new place |

**How the word tests are run: the formal trial** (the lead's decision after P3's eighth round, A60; 4.8 is the one place for the protocol). Every test of what a word means, the ledger's "understood" and M6's never-taught tests alike, is scored only in formal trials, as infant labs score it: intermodal preferential looking (Golinkoff et al. 1987) and looking-while-listening (Fernald et al. 2008; Bergelson and Swingley 2012), and the name against a foil name in the same voice (Mandel, Jusczyk and Pisoni 1995). At a probe scheduled by the day plan's stage, never by the child's rates, she brings the target and a distractor side by side into its view, settles and holds still (only her mouth moves), and says the single test sentence; which is named and the sides are her trial stream's fair coins, so chance is 50% by counterbalancing; the trial is scored by the proportion of looking over a fixed window, the test word's onset + 2 to + 23 ticks, the same whichever is named, a pair's trial with under 4 of its ticks on either thing void, and a word's standing is a permutation test over the draw labels, its thing's share of the looking when its word was said against when the other's was (the name's, its face's share after its name against after a foil; the lead's decision A60b, 4.8), at two levels: "maps", over trials of its trained thing, and "understood", the registered claim for an object noun, passed also over a separate block of 24 new exemplars of its kind never named before the probe, each set down beside new exemplars of two other kinds as new as it, its word said against its never-told foil heard as often as it (4.8; A60b 9, 10, 12, 13); she gives nothing for a trial (no feedback, as labs give none); any other movement of hers voids it, and nothing the child's voice does. Its stimuli are time-matched by construction, as infant labs match theirs (4.8, P3's twelfth to fourteenth rounds; C67 closed): every sentence its draw could give is one timeline in her voice, one carrier phrase a form with its test word spliced into it (one recording before it, one tag after it: "where is the X? see?", "hi. pip. hi."; the test word at the engine's own per-word rate, a matched pitch contour and the form's one level under the carrier and the tag), checked on the rendered audio and, while the word scaffold labels her lines, on the words channel, so no rule of the trial reads a tick that depends on which thing is named (`test_sim_lang` 69), and her voice's start and her sound's stop, at every level of her sound as a whole (10 ms by 10 ms, her mouth, the child's ears summed over their bands), are one moment whichever is named (`test_sim_lang` 71); a set that is not one timeline is never used. What the child hears of the test word itself differs as any two words do (C75), band by band where it starts and stops among it (C75b), and a trial's measure counts only how much of its window the child looked at each thing, so for a child whose looking does not depend on the word its draw labels are exchangeable and the permutation test is null by construction, however it times its acts (`test_sim_lang` 70, 72). The M6 items are drawn from the matched sets: "where is the X? see?" of ball, bear, block, bottle, car, cup, drum, rattle, ring and tower (not "duck", "book", "box" or "stacker"); the name against its three foils; the combination the ball's held pair against its original (the cup's and the block's pairs are not matched, the car's needs "orange"). With the scaffold silenced, as word claims are made (A29), all of these run; while it labels her lines only the seven birth toys pair among themselves, and the name test and the combination wait (C68). Her everyday asks are her teaching and count toward nothing. The M6 tests below that ask "where is the X?" are formal trials of the forms named there (4.8's `TRIAL_FORMS`), each item fresh on its first 3 presentations (A28); `Ledger.pooled` gives a form's test, the flip test over its trials, each once, of the named thing's share of the looking at either thing (every trial's draw turned over or not, as likely), one-sided: exact whatever the child's favourites, drift or learning, no rate estimated (P3's tenth round: pooling each word's trials against its yoked rate estimated and treated as known passed a voice-turner's name form 3.3–4.8% of the time and a favourite's word 2.0–4.1% at a nominal 0.01; the flip test at most 1.5% over 400 lives of 40 trials each, `test_sim_lang` 65).

**The tests of understanding by milestone** (the roadmap's, added by the lead's decisions: A55). They show understanding the way infants show it, by where they look and what they do unpaid, and they are fixed before C36 (P4): the items, the chance measures and the pooling. Each is judged one-sided at p < 0.01 by A19's rule, on fresh items (a novel item is a probe only on its first 3 presentations, A28), and a claim about words is made only with the word scaffold silenced (A29). No claim rests on the parent's ledger and its pacing (4.8). The instruments read the fovea; the parent never does (A40). A probe waits until its object is within the fovea's reach for the child's posture (A22), and her eyes stay on the child through it (A51), except in M1's head-turn probe, whose stimulus is her silent turn to the toy.

| M | the test | chance | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| M0 | the touch forecast's error at its own hand's touch against her hand's, matched by the touched limb and the force (the hands' arrays, or the observer's group) | a ratio of 1 | Blakemore, Wolpert and Frith 1998 |
| M0 | the ear forecast's error at the drum's boom with the strike in the fovea against out of view, and at her speech onsets with her face in the fovea against not | equal errors on matched clips, by permutation | Spelke 1976; Kuhl and Meltzoff 1982 |
| M1 | after a toy's event breaks the eye forecast (beyond the body's own 0.99 quantile of eye error), the toy's share of fovea ticks and touches over the next 200 ticks | that share after an ordinary event at matched visibility | Stahl and Feigenson 2015 |
| M1 | after her silent head turn to a toy (no word, no point), the fovea on that toy within 20 ticks | matched windows with no turn | Scaife and Bruner 1975 |
| M1–M2 | the ball rolled behind the low table: the fovea at its far edge first; the eye error when it reappears | landings there in matched windows with no roll; an unheralded toy appearing at the same place | Johnson, Amso and Slemmer 2003; Baillargeon 1987 |
| M2 | a look to her face within 20 ticks of a never-seen toy's appearance | matched moments | Walden and Ogan 1988 |
| M3 | the arm's act rate while it holds the rattle, which sounds with speed; the ear forecast's error at rattle sounds its own arm made against her shakes at matched loudness | the rattle's own first holds (the block differs in mass and grasp, so it is not the chance); equal errors | Rovee and Rovee 1969 |
| M3 | the cover lifted within 40 ticks of a toy hidden under it | matched moments with nothing hidden | Piaget 1954 |
| M4–M5 | the fovea on the goal toy before her reaching hand arrives (her tidying, her hand-overs) | matched windows with no reach | Falck-Ytter, Gredebäck and von Hofsten 2006 |
| M6 | "where is the X?" with a never-seen exemplar of X set down beside new exemplars of two other kinds as new as it, or "where is the F?", F its never-told foil heard as often as X, by her stream's fair coin, a formal trial (4.8); at least 8 examples of each tested noun (5.2, A53) | its new exemplar's share of the looking at the three after its word against after its foil (the lead's decisions after 200e57a and 67741fd, A60b 9, 12, 13; the known word's yoked comparison before it credited a child that knew only the other word): the permutation test over its nouns' trials, each once (A60b), and per noun its level 2 (4.8's new-exemplar block of 24 trials, 8 exemplars at 3 presentations); testable with A53's 8 exemplars a noun (over 8 nouns, 192 trials; P3's tenth round: the ninth's pooling needed 10 yoked trials a word and could never run) | Quinn, Eimas and Rosenkrantz 1993 |
| M6 | the colour twins as targets, each colour word heard on at least 2 kinds (M6(b) above); combinations of colour and noun come after the first year in infants, so a pass is not expected in year 1 | the twin's yoked comparison (its share of the looking on the twin when the original is named), with 50% by counterbalancing, 4.8's permutation test (A28 as amended by A60 and A60b; it had read "the noun-alone rate on that twin") | Wagner, Dobkins and Barner 2013; Fernald, Thorpe and Marchman 2010 |
| M6 | retired with the charge (A88): "more" had no need to serve; a word used for its own want waits for a want the room can meet (a game asked for again) | — | ours |

### The firsts: what no architecture has shown, and the bar for each (A61–A63)

The owner's word (2026-09-25): "for it to solve robotics that no archecture had done before". A first here is a claim no published architecture has shown, checked against the state of the art in September 2026 (the firsts research and its skeptic's review, `$S/firsts/`). It is registered before birth with its bar, judged by A19's rule, and reported whether it passes or fails (A63). No life has run: every hour below is a bar, not a result.

**The robotics first is the setting, not a task.** Every skill below has been learned faster by some method, from a signal built for it. None has shown them together: in one life with no resets, from sparse feedback like a person's, with no reward built for any task, still learning afterwards, in a real robot's whole body. It becomes a robotics result on the real G1 (the research's F9; section 16).

**The state of the art, September 2026** (the closest published results; context, never a run and never a target: A62):

| line of work | the closest published results | what they learned from |
|---|---|---|
| a real robot, from scratch | an A1 rolled, stood and walked within 1 h, and a UR5 picked and placed from pixels in about 8 h (DayDreamer: Wu et al. 2022); an A1 walked in 20 min (Smith, Kostrikov and Levine 2022) | a task reward per skill |
| real manipulation | 25–50 min a policy (SERL: Luo et al. 2024); 1–2.5 h a task (HIL-SERL: Luo et al. 2025) | demonstrations, a success classifier as the reward, human corrections |
| many simulations at once | ANYmal walked after 20 wall minutes with 4,096 simulated robots (Rudin et al. 2022), the G1 after 15 on one RTX 4090 (Seo et al. 2025); the Rubik's cube hand used 13,000 simulated years (OpenAI 2019); grasping from pixels took 580,000 attempts over 800 robot hours (QT-Opt: Kalashnikov et al. 2018) | task rewards, parallel copies |
| world models | one configuration across more than 150 tasks (DreamerV3: Hafner et al. 2023); over a million hours of video and 62 h of robot video (V-JEPA 2, 2025) | task rewards; passive video |
| foundation models | about 10,000 h of teleoperation (π0: Black et al. 2024); 780,000 synthetic trajectories, about 6,500 h (GR00T N1, 2025); improvement from deployment, task by task, with human corrections (π*0.6, 2025) | demonstrations |
| developmental robotics | an iCub from babbling to skilled reaching in under 3 h, through staged maps (Law et al. 2014); MIMo, the infant simulator, rolling after 10⁶ steps from a dense orientation reward without vision, one agent per skill (Philipp et al. 2026); staged grasping and imitation with a caregiver's help (Ugur et al. 2015); a simulated infant with a caregiver and infant tests set up (SEDRo 2020, a proposal; Doyle et al. 2023) | engineered maps, rewards built per skill |
| a person or a face as the reward | a face in grid worlds (Broekens 2007), or through a reward model trained on recorded faces (EMPATHIC: Cui et al. 2021); a person's preferences taught simulated motor skills (Christiano et al. 2017); joint attention, gaze following and social referencing, each trained as the skill tested (Nagai et al. 2003; Triesch et al. 2006; Boucenna et al. 2014) | one skill each |
| understanding | surprise at impossible events learned from passive video, with no body (PLATO: Piloto et al. 2022; Garrido et al. 2025); embodied agents mostly failed held-out permanence tests (Animal-AI: Crosby et al. 2020) | passive video; task rewards |
| lifelong learning | deep networks lose plasticity over long streams (Dohare et al. 2024); several tasks without resets on a real robot (Gupta et al. 2021); policies that keep learning on the robot (Smith et al. 2022); deployed humanoid policies stay static (a 2026 survey: Nguyen et al.) | task rewards |

**First 1: one life, many skills, sparse social feedback, the stock G1's whole body** (the thesis).
- **The claim,** registered before birth: "The stock G1 model, from randomly initialised learned weights and a disclosed innate set (section 10), in one life of one stream with no resets and no parallel copies, learned to orient, reach and grasp [the full claim: and to roll on purpose]. It learned from a scripted parent's sparse smiles and frowns for its own completed acts, felt only while it looked at her, with its own pain as its only other reward (A91); and it kept learning for 200 life hours without losing those skills."
  - Printed beside it, always: the intervention log (her guides, holds, turns and placements, counted: A63), and her curriculum steered every 400 ticks by a language model that never sets a feeling, a judgment, a smile, a help level or a force (A14, A61).
- **A full claim and a lesser one,** both registered: M1–M4, and M1–M3, since rolls under babble are not yet measured on the G1 (risk 3).
- **Two levels.** Level 1 holds on the world-truth smile, the disclosed scaffold (A49). Level 2 holds on the smile read by its own camera: A49's removal test passes, and M2 and M3 hold for 10 life days after it. Level 2 is the research's F3, a face read by the robot's own eyes as its reward. In the sim that face is a scripted avatar, so it becomes a person's only on the real G1.
- **Bar:**
  - each milestone reaches its ruler by A19's rule within 60 life hours (1.44M waking ticks: the pessimistic end of the forecasts above);
  - the never-taught tests of M2 and M3 (and M4, for the full claim) pass, and M1's where C36 shows they can reach p < 0.01 (M1(a) rests on a single first call from the doorway);
  - the log shows no episodic reset, and no reward but the face and pain (section 6; A88, A91);
  - **the long-run clause** (the research's F5, on its skeptic's stronger bar), at 200 life hours:
    - savings: a skill left unasked and unscaffolded for a span registered before birth is relearned in fewer exposures than it was first learned in;
    - plasticity: new words' namings to criterion, and new objects' showings to a first grasp, are no higher in the last 50 hours than in the first 50;
    - M1–M3's rulers are no lower than on their criterion days.
  - 200 hours is about ten times the store's 19 nights, so the clause tests what the night keeps.
- **Its cost, reported and never claimed** (the research's F2): its life hours to each criterion, its exposures to criterion by kind, and the overnight gain (A62), beside the table above with each method's signal. No multiple is printed.
- **Closest:** DayDreamer (a task reward per skill); HIL-SERL (demonstrations, a classifier and corrections, one task at a time); MIMo (one agent per skill, from a reward built for it, in an infant's body); the iCub (engineered maps, no person); Christiano et al. (a person's preferences, one simulated skill at a time); Gupta et al. (no resets, task rewards). SEDRo and Doyle et al. set such a world up, so the novelty must come from the result, not from the setting.
- **The reviewer's first question:** why about 60 hours, when HIL-SERL takes 1–2.5 a task? Because here there is no teleoperation, no classifier trained for a task, no reset and no reward that names a task, and the skills are kept afterwards.
- **What delivers it:** the grounded reward (6); a gate per limb with movement units (3.6); credit through the amygdala's tags and the night's tagged replay (7.3, 7.4, R8); the cerebellum (7.5); the spinal pattern generator (A48); recall into action (7.6); REM's twitches (A46); the parent's scaffolding (4.10).
- **Achievable here:** yes, if credit crosses the delay (risk 1) and movement units last (risk 3). At section 9's case C, 60 life hours take about 1.6–3.3 calendar days running around the clock, and 200 about 5–11, stretched by the Mac's share for the life.

**First 2: understanding, shown in the body by tests it was never taught.**
- **The claim:** its own life passes at least 3 of a hard set of 4 infant tests of understanding, none of them trained toward, each failed by its born state.
- **The hard set** (from the tests above, A55):
  - the ball rolled behind the low table, its fovea at the far edge first (Johnson, Amso and Slemmer 2003);
  - the cover lifted within 40 ticks of a toy hidden under it (Piaget 1954);
  - its fovea on the toy within 20 ticks of her silent head turn (Scaife and Bruner 1975);
  - a look to her face within 20 ticks of a never-seen toy (Walden and Ogan 1988).
- **What does not count:** a test its born state passes at p < 0.01 (measured at S5b, A63), and the tests this design passes by construction: its own touch against hers and its own rattle's sound (the efference copy feeds the forecast), and exploring what broke an expectation (the born orienting to sudden change, A43). They stay tests; they are not this claim.
- **Clever Hans:** at a probe she behaves exactly as at the matched moments, the stimulus apart, with her eyes on the child (A51), so it cannot pass by reading her.
- **Bar:** 3 of the 4 pass by A19's rule (one-sided p < 0.01, fresh items, pooled), reported at 60 and at 200 life hours and at each night boundary after. Infants pass them at 6–12 months, so no hour is promised.
- **Closest:** PLATO and Garrido et al. (surprise learned from passive video, with no body); Animal-AI (embodied agents mostly failed held-out permanence tests); Triesch et al. and Boucenna et al. (the skill tested was the skill trained). The review found no embodied learner with a sparse social reward that passed a battery registered in advance.
- **What delivers it:** the cortex's forecasts of its own senses (a broken expectation is their surprise), the amygdala, orienting, recall into action (A45), the store and the night.
- **Achievable here:** medium-low by 60 life hours, medium by 200.

**Dropped as firsts** (A63), each kept where it serves:
- **Speed alone** (the research's F2): single skills have been learned in minutes to hours from signals built for them. The body's hours are First 1's reported cost.
- **A face as the reward, on its own** (F3): real faces have served as rewards before (Broekens 2007; Veeriah et al. 2016; Li et al. 2020; EMPATHIC). In the sim the face is a scripted avatar read by a born reader, so here it is First 1's second level, and a first only with a person and the real G1.
- **Lifelong learning, on its own** (F5): learning without resets on a robot (Gupta et al. 2021), continued learning on hardware (Smith et al. 2022) and sleep-like consolidation of robot skills in simulation (Jayasinghe et al. 2026) did parts of it. It is First 1's long-run clause.
- **Grounded, compositional words** (F6): embodied agents understood held-out colour–shape pairs (Hermann et al. 2017; Chaplot et al. 2018). M6(b) stays a test.
- **One core, two bodies** (F7): false as worded, since the sim turns on organs the language body lacks (the amygdala, the event lines, the cerebellum, recall into action) and sim-only constants; and DreamerV3 used one configuration across bodies. The language digests held stay a disclosed property.
- **A first word from its own tract** (F8): Elija learned words from caregivers through a synthesized vocal tract (Howard and Messum 2014), and M6t is not forecast.
- **Seed 1 on the real G1** (F9): it needs the robot, and each step is the owner's call (section 16).
- **Reviewed 2026-09-25** (`docs/audit/first2_word_clause.md`, a skeptic against the state of the art): "a word told once and acted on after a night" is not a separate first (Hill et al. 2021 and Lampinen et al. 2021 bound a word in one exposure in simulated bodies; the iCub fast-mapped with pre-built features, Twomey et al. 2016; pretrained robots keep names overnight for free). It joins First 2 as a **word clause** with its own bar (from random weights, sound only, one telling with the object in view, a night between, the test formal and time-matched with a never-told foil word, the born state failing the same protocol), reported "not testable yet" until M6 with the input scaffold silenced. The language body's kiwi was told five times before its night: every document says "learned after one telling, kept through a night".

### The human-pace ledger: an optional report (A58; demoted by A66)

It reports the G1's milestones against infant norms, in waking hours, as the owner's bar of 2026-09-24 asked: "learning speed and understanding level of human". On 2026-09-25 he said he does not care about human pace (A66), so it is kept only as an optional report he does not need: never a headline, and nothing waits for it, birth included. Its firewall stands in full (below).
- **It is a ruler, never a target.** Nothing in the body, the parent, the brief, the tests or the build reads it.
- **It maps no month onto a life day inside the body** (A56).
- **Its arithmetic is in the study's `$S/pace/hours.py`.** If the ledger is kept, a tool that reproduces it is committed with the ledger's digest before birth.

**The ruler:**
- **Robot hours, H_r(m).** Count the waking ticks from birth to the end of the first of A19's two consecutive days on milestone m's ruler, then multiply by 0.15 s.
  - For a never-taught test, count to the day it first passes.
  - A life day's wake is 24,000 ticks, which is one hour (B19). Nights are excluded, as an infant's sleep is.
- **Infant hours, H_i(m).** These are the waking hours from birth to the norm's age: 24 h minus the mean 24-hour sleep at each age, integrated.
  - The sleep figures are from Galland et al. 2012, Table 2: 14.6 h at 0–2 months, 13.6 at about 3, 12.9 at about 6, 12.6 at about 9, 12.9 at about 12 and 12.6 at 1–2 years.
  - They are placed at 1, 3, 6, 9, 12 and 18 months, linear between and flat outside, over months of 30.4375 days.
  - The table's ±1.96 SD limits run from 9.3–20.0 h at 0–2 months to 10.1–15.8 h at 12. They scale H_i by about ×0.5–1.5 at 4 months and ×0.6–1.4 at 12.
- **Its first figure is the waking-hour age, A_r.** This is the infant age at which an infant at mean sleep has lived H_r waking hours, with its range under the sleep limits.
  - A_r is placed in the milestone's own age distribution, using WHO's percentiles where they exist.
  - For M7 those are: 1st percentile 3.8 months, 5th 4.3, 25th 5.2, 50th 5.9, 75th 6.7, 95th 8.0 and 99th 9.2. In waking hours: 1,144, 1,306, 1,602, 1,837, 2,108, 2,552 and 2,967.
- **Second, the ratio S = H_i / H_r,** printed at the median age, over the age window, and over the sleep limits.
  - S = 1 means an infant's pace per waking hour.
  - One seed gives one H_r against a population, so S carries false precision. It is printed beside A_r, never alone.
  - It is never printed for M1, whose infant range runs from a newborn's orienting to the CDC's 4-month item.
- **The body's own exposures to criterion** (the right column of the second table below) are part of fast learning's measure (A62), with or without the infant column.
- **"Understanding level" is answered only by which never-taught tests pass,** and at what A_r. A motor row never answers it.

**Waking hours by age.** The first row is at mean sleep. The second is under the sleep limits. The third is the hours with a face in view (see the exposures table below), interpolated linearly between 1 and 11 months and flat outside them:

| age | 1 day | 1 week | 1 month | 3 months | 4 months | 6 months | 9 months | 12 months | 18 months |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| waking hours | 9 | 66 | 286 | 889 | 1,209 | 1,870 | 2,898 | 3,925 | 5,979 |
| under the sleep limits | 4–15 | 28–103 | 122–447 | 432–1,339 | 625–1,787 | 1,035–2,700 | 1,729–4,060 | 2,478–5,362 | 4,030–7,909 |
| with a face in view | 2 | 16 | 72 | 212 | 279 | 400 | 545 | 642 | 814 |

**The ledger.** The kinds of criterion:
- **first:** a first occurrence, an earliest age;
- **mean** or **median:** attainment;
- **≥75%:** the age by which at least 75% attain (the CDC's items: Zubler et al. 2022), an upper bound on the median;
- **group:** the age of the lab group tested, which gives no onset age.

H_i is the median age's hours at mean sleep, with the age window in brackets.

| M1 | turns to a voice | within its first days; the learned turn returns after a dip at 2–3 months; 4 months | group; ≥75% | Muir and Field 1979†; Muir, Clifton and Clarkson 1989†; the CDC ("turns head toward the sound of your voice") | from 9–66 up to at most 1,209; no single figure |
