- **Added in the build (ours):** a word's chance is its yoked rate where higher than 50% (a favourite toy looked at first whatever is said would otherwise have its word understood), and since P3's tenth round the two are tested on one stretch of its last 20 trials, named or yoked, as two one-sided binomial tests against 1/2 (4.8's (i) and (ii)): the ninth round's rule set the last 10 trials against a yoked or foil rate counted over all time and treated as known, and its verifier found a voice-turner's name understood in 15 of 40 lives within 100 name tests, 94% within 120 when its turning rose, a shifting favourite's word far more often than a fair coin, and section 12's pooled claims past their 0.01; now any child whose look does not depend on the word meets (ii) as a fair coin exactly; fresh items only on their first 3 presentations (A28), a dropped probe's display counted as one; her judgment after a trial as after any ask (A28); a trial's sentence is never said on request, Claude's rows included (A14); an act her motion's report leaves out is asked of its own status, so an ended act whose one report was never read no longer stalls every trial; since P3's eleventh round, each trial on one timeline for every sentence its draw could give (its window from the latest onset, her voice held to the latest end) and nothing the child's voice voids it (no stop, frown or judgment of its turns until the trial is decided): its tenth verifier found two void rules following the named sentence's own length in her voice, so a child that knew no word, sounding at a fixed time, had one word's trials voided and the other's understood. Since P3's twelfth round (the lead's decision on C67), its stimuli time-matched by construction, as infant labs match theirs: every sentence its draw could give one identical timeline in her voice (the test word at the engine's own per-word rate and a matched pitch contour), checked on the rendered audio and, while the scaffold labels her lines, on the words channel; a set that is not one timeline is never used (its eleventh verifier found that a hit timed from her sound's stop, the sentences ending on different ticks, voided one word's trials and let the other's be met). Since P3's thirteenth round, every trial scored by intention to treat (a none or a void from its onset's tick on against it), every sentence of a form on one carrier recording before its test word, and the name test's foil held to no turn past its window by her sentence's span (its twelfth verifier found the time match held on the clip's tick grid only: a child timing a look or a hit from a moment of her sound as it perceives it, 10 ms by 10 ms, at other levels or through its own ears, had one word's trials met and the other's none or void, counted for nothing, "ball" understood in every life). Since P3's fourteenth round, every sentence of a form one carrier phrase, its test word between one recording before it and one tag after it at one level under both, so her voice's start and her sound's stop are one moment at every level, however the child perceives them, and the onset's reading taken the tick before the onset's (its thirteenth verifier found that with one recording a word, where a sentence stopped was the same against every other word, so a child with a favourite and two looks, one timed from her sound's stop, had the order of its looks follow the word, "drum" understood in 12 of 12 lives whatever its distractor).
- **What it costs, disclosed:** trials are fewer than asks, so "understood" comes more slowly and the word pace's floor carries more of the growth (4.8); a word needs at least 5 trials naming it and about as many yoked (a perfect knower, about 10 in all); the ledger's window of 20, tested after every trial and kept once reached, is reached by a child whose looks do not depend on the word at most as often as a fair coin's trials reach (ii): 23% over 60 trials, 40% over 120, 63% over 240 (C64); a child that knows the other word and favours this one's thing when it is not said passes as knowing this one, as the lab's yoked comparison would (4.8); since P3's thirteenth round a knower's trial with no look in its window, its pain, distress or hit in it, or a void in it from anything of hers (her body moving, a tick the world did not read), counts against it, where a lab would drop it (intention to treat, C68); since its fourteenth her trial sentences carry a tag, "see?" or "hi.", and a test word 0.4 to 2.9 dB under its natural level (the level ceiling), and the test word's own sound is left as the words differ (C75).

**A60b. Understanding scored by the proportion of looking, tested by permutation over the draw labels** (the lead's decision on P3's fourteenth verifier's finding, P3's fifteenth round; sections 4.3, 4.6, 4.8, 5.2, 10, 12, A19, A28, A53, A60; C64, C75b)
- **Decided (the lead's; structural: it replaces timed-event scoring, it does not patch it):** understanding is scored as infant labs score it, by the proportion of looking over a fixed long window, not by whether an event lands at a moment.
  1. The display and the stimuli stay as P3's fourteenth round built them: two things placed by one fixed choreography, symmetric about the midline; she names one from the fixed carrier ("where is the X? see?"), target and distractor counterbalanced, she stays still, her eyes on the child, no point.
  2. The window: from the test word's onset tick + 2 (about 300 ms, the earliest a word-guided shift can begin: Fernald, Zangl, Portillo and Marchman 2008, "Looking while listening", in Sekerina, Fernández and Clahsen eds.) to its onset + 24 (about 3.6 s): the ticks onset + 2 through onset + 23, 22 ticks (`consts.TRIAL_LOOK`). The same whichever item is named; nothing in it is timed from the word's sound.
  3. The measure per trial: over the window's ticks, T the ticks the child's gaze (her reading of its head line, A40, as the conduct reads it, `Percept.child_target`) is on the named thing, D on the other; p = T / (T + D). A trial with T + D < 4 is void, not scored, counted and disclosed (`TRIAL_LOOK_MIN`). Nothing else about the ticks matters: not when they fall, not their order.
  4. Per word W over its registered trials: the mean p on trials where W is named against the mean share to W's thing on trials where the other word is named (the same thing, the foil condition); "understood" when the difference exceeds zero at one-sided p < 0.01 by a permutation test over the draw labels (which item was named), with at least the trials that make p < 0.01 reachable. The permutation test is the invariance property: for any child whose behaviour does not depend on which word was said, the labels are exchangeable and the difference is null by construction (Golinkoff, Hirsh-Pasek, Cauley and Gordon 1987, intermodal preferential looking; Bergelson and Swingley 2012, PNAS 109:3253–3258, proportion of looking to the target over a post-onset window; Fernald et al. 2008).
  5. The name test (its name against a same-voice foil name, Mandel, Jusczyk and Pisoni 1995): the same measure, the region her face: its share of the window's ticks on her face, name against foil, the permutation test.
  6. Her everyday asks stay her teaching and never count; only trials count.
  7. **No feedback in test trials** (the lead's, after 535e32b): her smile after a trial is removed; a trial is silent, she stays still through the window and after it and gives nothing for it, as infant labs give no feedback in test trials (Fernald et al. 2008; Golinkoff et al. 1987). Her smiles and her teaching stay in the everyday asks, which never count. The reason: a rewarded trial is trainable by construction (the skeptic's finding, and the learners measured on 535e32b below).
  8. **Two levels of "understood"** (the lead's, after 535e32b): the per-word test as built is level 1, "maps" (the word to the trained thing). The registered claim (section 12's tests) needs level 2, "understood": the same permutation test passed also with new exemplars of the named kind (A53's calendar: at least 8 a tested noun since decision 12) against at least two distractors, each never named before the probe, in a separate registered block, with the same window, void rule and statistic, the same flip invariance, and its alpha spent as at level 1. A child keyed by sound to one particular object passes level 1 and fails level 2; a knower of the kind passes both (Waxman and Booth 2001; the category-label comprehension literature). The lead approved the build's two choices: the 0.01 spent over the repeated tests (the first at 0.005; C64 closed), below.
  9. **Level 2's control is a never-told foil word, not another known word** (the lead's, after 200e57a; the skeptic's design in `docs/audit/first2_word_clause.md`, on main at 5e86398): the exemplar block compares trials where W is said against trials where a foil word F is said, F never told, matched to W in syllables and stress, in the same voice and register, drawn by her trial stream, with at least two distractor objects present besides the exemplar. The reason: with a known word as the control, a child that knows only the other word passes W by exclusion (the build's open item on 200e57a), and mutual exclusivity is a strategy, not understanding of W.
  10. **At least 4 exemplars per tested noun** (the lead's, after 200e57a): A53's "at least 3" amended to "at least 4", so that level 2's 12 registered trials at A28's 3 presentations each are reachable; the world's open item (W5b inherits it). Superseded by decision 12.
  11. **The name keeps one level, disclosed as the paradigm's limit** (the lead's, after 200e57a): recognizing one's own name at 4.5 months is recognizing its sound pattern (Mandel, Jusczyk and Pisoni 1995), so a child keyed to a band of its name's sound is not distinguishable from a recognizer by any looking test, and need not be. The name has no new exemplars to test a second level with; its test is its sound's recognition, which is what the child keyed on its 2667 Hz band does (C75b).
  12. **Power: level 2's block is 24 registered trials** (the lead's, after 67741fd): 8 exemplars at 3 fresh presentations each, or the equivalent that keeps every trial's exemplar unnamed and fresh; the room supplies at least 8 exemplars per tested noun (A53 and 5.2 amended from "at least 4" to "at least 8", the world's W5b open item, cheap in a sim). Any void spends a presentation, so the conduct draws a further exemplar when needed.
  13. **Exclusion by familiarity: the foils matched in exposure** (the lead's, after 67741fd): before a noun's level-2 block its foil has been heard by the child about as many times as the noun (her non-teaching chatter carries the foils, never with a referent in view, never as a label, at the noun's running rate), so a child that turns away from nameable things only at a word heard often enough meets the same familiarity at the foil and is null; disclosed as ours (the standard studies use novel foils; ours are matched-exposure nonwords); each foil's exposure count logged beside the noun's.
  14. **Matched newness** (the lead's, after 67741fd): the two distractors beside the exemplar are new exemplars of two other kinds, as new as the target (never named, within their fresh presentations), drawn so that each noun's exemplar pool serves both roles without a presentation ever being named or exceeding freshness; enforced in the conduct.
  15. **Inventory: a registered short list** (the lead's, after 176da4e): level 2 is run only for a registered short list of tested nouns fixed before birth, at most 6 (the nouns First 2's tests and the word clause need), and the room provides at least 24 new exemplars per registered noun (A53 and 5.2: "at least 24 for each registered noun; at least 4 for every other tested noun"), so every registered block can run with matched newness and a knower's next test at 48 trials is reachable (16 own exemplars and the others'); the conduct refuses a level-2 block for a noun not on the list.
  16. **Talk density** (the lead's, after 176da4e): her talk rate with the chatter on is measured over the zoo's lives against the design's bounds (4.6: 1,500–2,500 words a life day, at least 40% of play ticks silent); if the chatter pushes it over, the foil lines replace some of her existing filler rather than adding to it, disclosed. The familiarity-by-context limit is accepted as disclosed (1 of 12 lives is within the nominal rate), and the words-channel note as written.
- **Why.** Thirteen rounds chased leaks in the timed-event scoring: a look or act counted when it landed in a window timed from the test word, so any child whose act was timed from some feature of the sound passed "understood" for one word without knowing any. The fourteenth verifier (f5ed8cd) found that outside the test word's slot the stimuli are identical sample for sample and her voice's start and her sound's stop are one moment for her sound as a whole, but band by band at the child's own ears (`body/sim/ears.Ears`' 40 bands) they fall inside the slot and differ by word (53–64 of 80 band-ear channels for "where is the X? see?"), so children with a favourite and one look timed from one band's stop reached "understood" in 12 of 12 lives (drum, bear, cup, car; "pip" by a gated turn), and 8 to 12 of 12 when they learned their delay from her smiles.
- **Added in the build (ours), each disclosed:**
  - *The life's 0.01, spent over its tests* (C64's "alpha spent over the looks", C64 closed): a life re-tests a word as its trials come, and a test at 0.01 taken after every trial would pass a child that does not know the word in more than 1 life in 100. So a word is tested at its 12th registered trial at 0.005, at its 24th at 0.0025, its 48th at 0.00125, doubling, each over all its registered trials so far, only where a perfect separation could reach that level (1 / C(n, k) under it; at 12 trials when 3 to 9 of them named it, 96% of lives), the levels summing to 0.01: a child whose looking does not depend on the word reaches "understood" in at most 1 life in 100, however long it lives (a union over the tests, each exact) (`consts.UNDERSTOOD_P`, `UNDERSTOOD_FIRST`, `ledger.test_level`).
  - *The permutation p, exact*: every relabeling counted, two halves of the trials each enumerated (meet in the middle), up to 36 trials (`PERM_EXACT_N`); past them 20,000 relabelings, or 20 over the test's level, drawn from one fixed stream, p = (1 + those at least as far) / (draws + 1), valid at any number of draws (Phipson and Smyth 2010) (`PERM_DRAWS`, `PERM_SEED`; `ledger.perm_test`, held to brute force in `test_sim_lang` 12).
  - *Section 12's test of a form*: over its scored trials, each once, the named thing's share of the looking less the other's, x = (T − D) / (T + D), their sum by the flip test over the draw labels (every trial's draw turned over or not, all 2^n as likely; `ledger.flip_test`), one-sided at 0.01; the name form by its permutation test.
  - *The other voids*: her attention log showing anything but her mouth moving from the sentence to the window's end (a tick not read among it), the child's pain, distress or hit, the world stopping her sentence, and a sentence not its stimulus's timeline void a trial as before, now not scored and counted (`Ledger.voided`, each word's `voids`); nothing the child's voice does voids one. For a child whose looking does not depend on the word, whether its trial is void is the same whichever was drawn, so dropping it leaves its labels exchangeable. Intention to treat (P3's thirteenth round) and the onset's reading of where it already looked (its fourteenth) are retired with the first look they were built for.
  - *The name's measure*: F / 22 (F its window's ticks on her face), every name trial scored (no looking voids one), the old span a foil's "no turn" was held to past the window (`TRIAL_EAR_TICKS`) retired.
  - *No feedback, as built* (decision 7): `Conduct._trial_end` records the trial and does nothing more: no judgment, no confirm, no reply owed for it; her face holds its neutral set (no mark in her attention log) through the window and after it. On 535e32b her smile and confirm had followed a trial whose looking went more to the target (T > D), and her smile a turn to her face after its name.
  - *Two levels, as built* (decision 8): an "exemplar" trial registers at level 2 (its score's fifth field; `Ledger.trial_outcome` puts it in the word's `seq2` with the word said, on 200e57a with the other thing's word), each of its things a new exemplar only while fresh (A28's first 3 presentations) and never among her lines' referents before the probe (`Ledger.was_named`, from each line's refs; an older save's read again from its file, else every thing counted as named, fail-closed); a "place" trial registers at level 1 (`seq`). Each block is tested at its own 12th, 24th, 48th, ... registered trial at 0.005, 0.0025, ... (0.01 a life each; on 200e57a the second only once its trials stood against at least 2 other words, a rule decision 9 retired); `Ledger.maps` is level 1, `Ledger.understood` level 2 for an object noun (both blocks passed) and level 1 for the name, the colour words and a combination's key, whose "understood" is their one test. The combination's gate (A28) and her pacing read "understood". Level 2 needs 12 registered new-exemplar trials a noun: 4 exemplars at 3 presentations each, A53's number since decision 10 (with 3 it is never testable at its first level, 0.005).
  - *The foil control, as built* (decision 9; on 67741fd from three foils a syllable count, drawn, and things P4 named; since decisions 12–14 as follows): `Conduct._exemplar_begin` draws from her trial stream, three draws as for every trial, its word or its foil (a fair coin) and the three things' order (each of the 6 as likely; the present act's target 'left|centre|right'); the third draw is unused (one foil a noun: `consts.NOUN_FOILS`, ball zeb, bear fep, block gub, bottle tuma, car tam, cup tuv, drum koob, rattle modi, ring kem, tower zibo, each noun's the first of its written syllables in the measured order whose first and last sounds differ from the noun's, c, k and q one sound, each foil once; ours). The foils are words she never tells: none among her 128 words, none within edit distance 1 of them, the name or its foils, the line check refusing each ("where is the F? see?", `templates.foil_line`, the only lines besides the name's foils that are not of her words, never Claude's); one found among her words drops the probe, whichever is drawn (fail-closed); her word's sentence alone is held to the line check, whichever is drawn. Every sentence the draw could give, its word's and each foil's of its syllables, is made on the form's one timeline and carrier phrase as before (`_trial_stimuli`, `stimuli.same`): the foils measured in her voice before birth (`tools/sim_voice_check.py --trial-foils` into `trial_lines.json`'s "foils", as many of each syllable count as the form tests nouns of it, none within edit distance 1 of another: each at the step of the engine's per-word rate nearest its natural one that puts it on the form's timeline, its pitch contour within `TRIAL_F0` of the form's, spliced at the form's one loudest 10 ms, the ceiling's margins 1.26–1.53 dB at the clip and 1.04–1.58 dB at the child's ears; "dax", "nef", "gaf" and "dap" left out, no step of the rate putting them on the timeline, "fam" within edit distance 1 of "tam"). The window's ticks are counted on the new exemplar (T) and on either thing beside it (D), q = T / (T + D), void under 4 on the three; the ledger's level-2 rows are its new exemplar's ticks and the others' whichever is said (the score entry's columns, (1, 0), say its thing is the target), with the word said (`seq2`, each test's foils in its record); section 12's exemplar form is tested by the permutation test, its words against their foils, as the name's is. For a child whose looking does not depend on the word said, a trial and the same trial with the other drawn (its word for a foil, the same foil and order) end alike, its ticks on each thing the same (`test_sim_lang` 73). While the word scaffold labels her lines, a word's token and a foil's letters are never one timeline on the words channel, so exemplar probes are dropped then, as the name's are: level 2 is testable once the scaffold is silenced (A29), which section 12's word claims need anyway. The ledger's scoring key `look-share-4` since decisions 12–14: a save of `look-share-2` or `look-share-3` keeps its level 1 and starts its new-exemplar blocks again, no object noun's "understood" kept (its block stood against a known word, or against a foil beside familiar things and not heard as often); an exemplar trial saved mid-trial before this is void, or dropped before its sentence; an older save still starts its whole record again.
  - *The block, the exposure and the newness, as built* (decisions 12–14). **The block**: `consts.KIND_FIRST` = 24, a new-exemplar block's tests at its 24th, 48th, ... registered trial at 0.005, 0.0025, ... (`ledger.test_level(n, first)`; 0.01 a life, as level 1's); P4 asks for exemplar trials of a noun, `Conduct.probe("exemplar", noun=W, new=the room's new exemplars)`, until its block holds 24. **The newness**: `Conduct._exemplar_set` draws each trial's three things itself, never reading the child or her stream: among the room's new exemplars she sees, fresh (their presentations in trials and outside them under 3, A28) and never among her lines' referents, its noun's with the most presentations first, beside one each of the two other kinds with the most such exemplars presented exactly as often (ties by name, then id): so a set drawn together stays together for its 3 presentations, nothing shown is newer to the child than another, none named, and each noun's pool serves as targets and as others alike; a void's presentation is spent (A28), and the next set is drawn when one is needed; with no such set the probe is dropped (the room's, W5b). **The exposure**: the conduct's `level2`, the nouns whose level 2 is registered (section 12's items, fixed before birth; none: no exemplar probe runs), each with its foil; her chatter (`Conduct._foil_chatter`, her priorities' ninth, below every teaching line) says "oh. F." (`templates.foil_chatter`: the only line besides the trials' foils not of her words; no label, no act, no object named, never Claude's) for the registered noun whose foil she has said least behind it (`Ledger.exposure`: each word of her lines whose sound ended), only while she reads the child's head line on no thing and its hands holding none, at most one a `FOIL_CHATTER_GAP` = 40 ticks (her idle slot since decision 16; 20 before) and each line at most once a `SAME_LINE`, so its foil keeps pace with its noun's running count from birth as the slot allows; an exemplar probe runs only while the foil's count is at least the noun's less 10% of it or 2 (`FOIL_EXPOSURE`, ours; one-sided: a foil heard more often than its noun is the stricter control), and each trial's record logs both counts (its `exposure`), as each level-2 test does.
  - *An older save*: its records of first looks start again (the ledger's scoring key `look-share`), and a trial saved mid-window before this rule is void.
- **Measured** (`test_sim_lang` 12, 59–65, 68–72): the permutation test at one fixed test (0.01) rejected 0.3–1.3% of 1,500 lives of each of 15 kinds of child whose looking does not depend on the word (voice-turners steady, rising, falling or stepping, or turning again once smiled at; favourites steady, shifting or rising; one that repeats what her smile rewarded; one that looks where the last word sent it), each within its sampling error of 1%, and a life's tests at 12, 24 and 48 trials 0–1.3% of 300 lives each (their levels 0.875%, the same); through her conduct, the zoo, 12 lives each of 24 probes (the favourite against each other toy in turn; the name 24), in the suite's timed voice and her real voice: favourites that look only at their toy, children with a favourite and a second look timed from her voice's start or her sound's stop at 11 levels and measures (10 ms frames at −44 to −18 dB, her mouth at −30 to −18 dB, its own ears 0.7 m from her at 21, 33 and 45 dB SPL), the thirteenth verifier's three children, a child that learns its delay from her smiles and two gated name children, 39 kinds: "understood" in 0 of 468 lives; every trial of theirs turned over at its draw the same (its window's ticks on each thing), a whole life with every draw turned over the same looking trial by trial, each label turned over; the eleventh and twelfth verifiers' children and every child timed from the carrier's and the ears' every level and measure likewise (26,040 children and trials of one act each on the clip's tick grid; 3,360 of the thirteenth verifier's kind, 720 gated name children and 6,336 of one act each as the child perceives her sound; 7,808 trials each way of looks and events at every tick after the onset); knowers detected: a knower 12 of 12, one right 80% of the time 11 of 12 in 48 probes, one that learns the word at its 12th trial 12 of 12 in 48, a knower of its name 12 of 12, in either voice; zoo lives the same ledger chains and rows in two processes, threaded with hash seed 1 and on one thread with hash seed 4242. Beyond the suite (the build's scratch measure, the timed voice, 200 lives of 48 probes each, a word's tests at 12, 24 and 48 trials, their levels 0.875%): children whose looking varies from trial to trial but never with the word, a favourite of the cup at 0.7 and at 0.5 (two looks of 3–14 ticks at random ticks of the window), the cup child learning its delay from her smiles, and a voice-turner at 0.6 in the name test: "understood" in 0, 2, 1 and 1 of 200 lives, 4 of 800 in all, on 535e32b; with no feedback (decision 7), mapping (level 1) in 0, 2, 2 and 1 of 200, 5 of 800 (their levels allow 7).
- **Measured for decisions 7 and 8** (`test_sim_lang` 65, 72, 73; P3's fifteenth round's second commit): with no feedback, the children keyed on one band of the child's own ear that redraw their delay whenever a trial with their favourite brings no smile (the drum's 107 Hz and 167 Hz bands, the bear's 1684 Hz, the cup's 136 Hz, the car's 323 Hz start) map in 0 of 12 lives each, where on 535e32b her smiles had taken them to 0, 8, 7, 1 and 0; the zoo's learner of its delay (her last 10 ms above −36 dB) 0 of 12 in either voice; no judgment and no confirm after any of 84 lives' trials; at level 2, through her conduct, 12 lives of 24 place probes and 12 of new exemplars each: a knower of the kind maps in 12 and is understood in 12 of 12 in either voice; a knower of its trained things alone maps in 12 and is understood in 0; the favourite of the trained thing maps in 0 and is understood in 0; the children gated on one band of the ear to the drum, the bear, the cup and the car map in 12 of 12 each and are understood in 0 of 12 each; an exemplar she named before its probe leaves its block empty; a block against one other word is not testable; a favourite's exemplar trials alike with the draw turned over; in the ledger alone, a child keyed to its trained thing, at chance on new exemplars, maps in 300 of 300 lives and is understood in 1 (level 2's tests at 12, 24 and 48, 0.875%), a knower of the kind understood in 100 of 100.
- **Measured for decisions 9–11** (`test_sim_lang` 12, 65, 73; P3's fifteenth round's third commit), through her conduct, 12 lives each of 24 place probes and 12 exemplar probes (A53's 4 exemplars: 12 registered trials) or 24 (8 exemplars: 24 trials), the new exemplar set down beside two things of two other words, its word or a foil by her stream's coin, the scaffold silenced before the block, in the suite's timed voice and her real voice alike: a knower of the kind whose looks at a foil spread over the three things, understood in 12 of 12 at 12 trials; a knower of the kind whose look at a foil goes to one of the three, understood in 0 of 12 at 12 trials and 10 of 12 at 24; a knower of its trained things alone, 0 of 12 at 12 and at 24 (it maps in 12); its favourite the trained thing, 0 (its exemplar trials all void) and its favourite the newest thing shown, 0 (neither maps); the child keyed by its ear's 167 Hz band to the drum (her real voice), maps in 12 and understood in 0 at 12 and at 24 trials; **a child that knows only the other words, turning from what it can name at any word it does not know, maps in 12 of 12 (level 1's disclosed limit) and is understood in 0 of 12, at 12 and at 24 trials**, where on 200e57a's code, its level 2 against the distractor's known word, the same child was understood in 12 of 12 (the build's scratch measure, the timed voice); in the ledger alone, such a child understood in 0 of 300 lives (level 2's tests at 12, 24 and 48, their levels 0.875%), and the M6 exemplar test, over 8 nouns' 96 trials by the permutation test, finds a knower (p = 5.0 × 10⁻⁵, the floor of its 20,000 relabelings) and neither a lover of the new exemplar (p = 0.65) nor such a child (p = 0.06). No smile or confirm after any trial of 384 lives. A non-knower's exemplar trials turned over at the draw: 6 of 6 the same in either voice. Dropped, never said: a probe whose things are of two words, one whose foil is a word of hers, and one made while the scaffold labels her lines (its word's token and a foil's letters differ on the words channel). What the foil does not close, measured the same way (below).
- **Measured for decisions 12–14** (`test_sim_lang` 12, 65, 72, 73; P3's fifteenth round's fourth commit), through her conduct, 12 lives each of 24 place probes and exemplar probes to a block of 24 registered trials, the noun registered from birth, the scaffold silenced before the block, the room's new exemplars 10 of the noun's kind and 10 of each of two other kinds (drawn by the conduct), in the suite's timed voice and her real voice alike: a knower of the kind understood in 9 of 12 lives when its look at a foil goes to one of the three things, 12 of 12 when it spreads over them, and 8 of 12 when it looks at the newest thing at a novel name; a knower of its trained things alone 0 (it maps in 12); its favourite the trained thing 0 (its exemplar trials all void, the room's sets spent) and the newest thing shown 0 (neither maps); the child keyed by its ear's 167 Hz band to the drum 0 (it maps in 12; 186 registered of its trials, 174 void); the child that knows only the other words, excluding at any word it does not know, 0 (maps 12); **the frequency-gated excluder (only at a word heard 6 times), 0 of 12, where on 67741fd 10 of 12**; **the knower of its trained things that looks at the newest thing when its thing is absent, 0 of 12, where on 67741fd 10 of 12**; disclosed, an excluder gated on the trial sentence's familiarity (only at a word heard 6 times in her trial sentences), 1 of 12. Her chatter said 3,837 foil lines over the 252 lives, every one while the child attended nothing and held nothing, with no act and no object named; at each block's first trial its noun had been heard 9–15 times and its foil 9–15. No smile or confirm followed any trial; a non-knower's exemplar trials turned over at the draw ended alike, 6 of 6 in either voice; an exemplar she named before the block was never drawn; dropped, never said: a probe with no set of new exemplars as new as each other in the room, one whose foil is a word of hers, one whose foil was heard 0 times against its noun's 30, one of a noun not registered, and one under the scaffold. In the ledger alone the block is tested first at its 24th trial (p = 1/C(24, 12) for a perfect knower with 12 naming it), a save of 67741fd's ledger keeps its level 1 and starts its blocks again, and the M6 exemplar test over 8 nouns' 192 trials finds a knower (p = 5.0 × 10⁻⁵, its relabelings' floor) and neither a lover of the new exemplar (p = 0.04) nor a child that knows only the other words (p = 0.21); a child keyed to its trained thing, at chance on new exemplars, maps in 300 of 300 ledger lives and is understood in 1 (tests at 24 and 48, their levels 0.75%), such a child that knows only the other words in 0 of 300, a knower of the kind in 100 of 100.
- **Built and measured for decisions 15–16** (`test_sim_lang` 73; P3's fifteenth round's fifth commit). **The short list**: `consts.LEVEL2_MAX` = 6; `Conduct(level2=...)` refuses a seventh noun, and an exemplar probe of a noun not on the list is refused (dropped, never said); the list is fixed at her construction and kept in her save. **The draw carries every registered block**: `Conduct._exemplar_set` keeps back, from a new set's others, each registered noun's own new exemplars still needed for its unfinished block (its 24 registered trials, three a new exemplar), taking them from the kinds with the most to spare. The room at A53's numbers (6 registered nouns at 24 new exemplars, the 4 other tested nouns at 4) drawn through it block after block: every registered block reached 24 trials with its things matched in newness, 16 new exemplars left unpresented; the draw without that keeping (67741fd's), in the build's scratch simulation of the same room, left the sixth block at 15. **Disclosed, the second test at 48**: a block's next 24 trials spend 24 more new exemplars (8 of its noun's, 16 others'), and with all six blocks run the room's 16 spare carry none; 48 new exemplars a registered noun (or fewer nouns registered: 5 at 24 leave 40 spare, one second test) carry every registered noun's second test (the scratch simulation: 48 a noun, every block to 48). **Talk density**: over the zoo's lives (test 73's children, 120 lives in each voice) her talk ran 1,194 words a life day, 11% of them her foil lines, with 84% (her real voice) to 88% (the timed voice) of the ticks free of her voice: within 4.6's upper bound and its silence bound (under its lower bound: those lives are blocks of trials, not a day's play). Over a scripted day of 24,000 ticks (the suite's `script()`: the child attending a toy two thirds of the time, the cup held a third), her own talk ran 2,298–2,519 words, 69–73% of ticks free of her voice; with the chatter on and nothing else in her idle slot, its foil lines added 284–316 words (142–158 lines), over the bound; so the foil lines now take her **idle slot** (4.10: at most one idle line a 40 ticks, `FOIL_CHATTER_GAP`), which P4's idle lines share (`Conduct.idle_free`, `note_idle`), and with a stand-in for P4's idle lines filling the slot her day ran 2,603–2,809 words with the chatter against 2,754–2,954 without it, 65–68% of ticks free of her voice against 63–67%: the foil lines replace her filler rather than add to it. Disclosed: that scripted day is over 4.6's 2,500 words with or without the chatter (her follow-in naming of a child that attends toys that often), 4.6's density is to be measured on P4's day (`tools/sim_parent_rates.py`, open); and in the idle slot the foil falls behind a noun she names more often than the slot allows (about 150 lines a day while that child attends nothing): at the day's end cup had been heard 332 times against its foil's 143, ball 197 against 15, so such a noun's block waits at the exposure gate until its foil catches up, which a noun named that often may not allow (the lead's: a foil line carrying more hearings in the same words, a registered noun named less often, or a looser match).
- **What it costs, disclosed:** a knower's trial with under 4 ticks on either thing, or with her body moving, is dropped, as labs drop theirs; its window's first 2 ticks, and any after its 23rd, count for nothing; a word needs 12 registered trials before its first test (a knower with 6 of 12 naming it passes there at p = 1/924); a child that knows only the other word looks at the other thing when that is named and at either when this one is, so this word's difference is positive and it is credited at level 1, "maps", in every life measured (the lab's yoked comparison has the same limit; the old rule's (i), which held it, went with the first look), and not at level 2 since decision 9; since decision 7 no child can learn a trial from a trial (she gives nothing for one), so a child whose looking does not depend on the word said is inside the permutation test's exact null; what her conduct reads of past trials is the ledger's "understood" alone (her pacing, the combination's gate, the call's smile), which such a child reaches in at most 1 life in 100; a word's "understood" (level 2) comes later than "maps" (level 1), after a block of 24 of its kind's new-exemplar trials, its word against its foil, with the scaffold silenced (A29); her chatter carries each registered noun's foil as often as she says the noun, a nonword said with no referent and no teaching, a line of talk a teaching line would otherwise not have (ours): with every tested noun registered her talk grows by those nouns' share of it, two words a foil line, so 4.6's density (about 1,500–2,500 words a life day, at least 40% of play ticks free of her voice) is to be measured again with it (`tools/sim_parent_rates.py`; open); and what stays of C75, C75b: a child whose looking follows the test word's own sound, band by band at its ears, is not a child whose behaviour does not depend on the word, and no measure of its looking tells it from a knower of that word in the trials it is shown: at level 1 it maps; at level 2, keyed to one particular thing, it fails; the name, which keeps one level (decision 11), it passes (a child keyed on the name's 2667 Hz band, 12 of 12 lives: C75b), the paradigm's limit, not a leak: to recognize one's name is to recognize its sound pattern. **What level 2's foil left open on 67741fd, and what stays** (`test_sim_lang` 73, 12 lives each, either voice): on 67741fd (a block of 12, its foil not heard as often as its noun, beside familiar things) a knower whose look at a foil goes to one of the three was understood in 0 of 12 lives at 12 trials, the frequency-gated excluder in 10 of 12 and the knower of its trained things that looks at the newest thing in 10 of 12; decisions 12–14 close each (below). What stays: (i) *a knower's power*: a foil's control is chance among the three things, so a knower whose look at a foil goes to one of them is understood at 24 trials in 9 of 12 lives, one that looks at the newest thing at a novel name in 8 of 12, where one that spreads its looks is in 12 of 12; the rest reach their next test at 48, which A53's 8 exemplars a noun cannot reach (16 would). (ii) *Familiarity by context, not count*: the foil is heard as often as its noun, never where its noun is heard (in her teaching, with a referent in view, and in level 1's trial sentences); a child that excludes only at a word heard 6 times in her trial sentences (its noun there throughout level 1, its foil only in the block) is understood in 1 of 12 lives, and one gated on her everyday questions or on having seen a thing named would meet no foil there at all; matching that would take the foil in her teaching, which would make it a word. (iii) *The words channel*: while the scaffold labelled her lines a birth noun came as its token and its foil as letters, so their exposure is matched in her voice, not on the channel (A29). (iv) *The room's inventory*: matched newness spends two other kinds' new exemplars each trial, so a block of 24 spends 24 exemplars' fresh presentations, 8 of its noun's and 16 of others'; since decision 15 level 2 runs for a registered short list of at most 6 nouns, 24 new exemplars of each, which carries every registered block of 24 (the draw keeping each unfinished block's own back) but no second test at 48 once all six have run (below, decisions 15–16: 48 a registered noun would).

A61–A66 were decided by the lead for the owner on the morning of 2026-09-25, on his word "only local no cloud. i dont care about huuman pace i just want fast learninhg and for it to solve robotics that no archecture had done before", from the firsts research and its skeptic's review (`$S/firsts/`). His standing rule is to decide rather than ask; the lead decided and reports, and the owner may overrule any of them. B21 is answered by his own word.

**A61. Local only: no cloud, ever** (the owner's word; B21 answered; sections 0, 1, 9)
- **Decided (his):** seed 1 is born and lives on this Mac. No cloud or rented machine, no paid test and no spending, ever. C63 is closed, and section 9's rented-machine cases, their conditions and their prices are dropped; they stay in git history (7bdc141).
- **The one input from off this Mac, read by the lead:** Claude's steering row every 400 ticks (4.5, A14, A59). It carries the owner's word of 2026-09-17 for a teacher ("we need a human teacher; you have to get Opus to become that", said of the language body's parent), and it is served on his plan, never the metered API, so it costs no money. Every row is logged by tick, so the life replays on this Mac with no network. The lead reads "no cloud" as no cloud machine and no spending, and keeps the steering.
  - If the owner means no call off this Mac at all, the fast layer runs alone (4.5 allows it), its choices of toys, episodes, tasks and new words made by P4's day plan, and nothing in the body changes.
- **Disclosed beside every first** (A63): the steering chooses her curriculum (the focus toys, the episodes, the scaffolding tasks, the day's new word), and never a feeling, a judgment, a smile, a help level or a force (A14).
- **What it costs:** calendar days only. In lockstep the machine sets wall time and nothing the body senses or does (A59).

**A62. Fast learning, defined** (the owner's word, "i just want fast learninhg"; sections 0, 1, 9, 12)
- **Decided:** fast learning is two things, reported apart.
  1. **Sample efficiency, the headline:** the experience the body needs, in its own units.
     - Its life hours to each milestone's criterion: the waking ticks from birth to the first of A19's two days, × 0.15 s. A life day's wake is one hour.
     - Its exposures to criterion, by kind: the smiles it felt and the asks it met before criterion; her guides, holds, turns and placements (the intervention log, A63); the namings of a word before its test of understanding passes (a variation set counts once, 4.5); the holds before the rattle's test passes.
     - The overnight gain: each skill's ruler in a day's first floor-play block against the day before's last, with nothing practised between but the night's replay and twitches.
  2. **The fastest honest local run:** the wall time a life hour takes on this Mac, from the exact levers (A64), the render as decided before birth (A65) and the machine's share, with nothing changed that the body senses or does.
- **Context, never a run and never a target:** the closest methods' published sample counts, each with the signal it learned from (section 12's firsts). No multiple ("N times faster than X") is printed, since each of those methods learned one skill from a signal built for it.
- **What can move it:** only architecture decided before birth: credit across the delay, the night's replay per waking hour, recall into action. Never the parent's pace, a looser ruler or an easier world. A faster wall clock brings the firsts' life hours sooner on the calendar, and changes none of them.
- **Why:** the body's hours do not depend on the machine, and they are the units other methods' sample counts are counted in. Wall time does depend on the machine, so it is reported beside them, never as learning.

**A63. The firsts, registered before birth** (the owner's word, "for it to solve robotics that no archecture had done before"; section 12)
- **Decided:** two firsts are registered (section 12).
  - **First 1:** one life, many skills, sparse social feedback, the stock G1's whole body, in a full claim (M1–M4) and a lesser one (M1–M3), at two levels, with a long-run clause and its cost reported.
  - **First 2:** understanding shown by a hard set of four tests it was never taught, claimed at 3 of 4.
- **The three safeguards the laws allow** (one seed and no comparison leave only "it can be done" claims):
  - each claim's wording, bar, rulers, items and chance levels frozen with a digest before birth;
  - each test's chance measured on seed 1's born state at every learning rate 0 (S5b); a test it passes at p < 0.01 leaves the claim;
  - the exact replay released: the commit, seed 1's born state and the logs, so anyone can replay the life to any tick.
- **The intervention log:** every act of hers that moves its body or its world on its behalf (guides, holds, turns onto its back, the pull-to-sit, props, catches, toys placed on the ladders, tidies, the morning basket), counted per life day and printed beside each first. "No reset" means no episodic reset: the world never restarts, and her interventions are counted, not hidden.
- **Dropped as firsts** (section 12 gives each reason): speed alone (the research's F2, now First 1's reported cost); a face as the reward on its own (F3, now First 1's second level); lifelong learning on its own (F5, now its long-run clause); grounded compositional words (F6); one core, two bodies (F7); a first word from its own tract (F8). Seed 1 on the real G1 (F9) is the endgame and the owner's call (section 16).
- **Why:** a first holds only if it is worded for what the life can show. A scripted parent whose smiles follow a worth table and whose hands guide its limbs is not "a person as the only reward". A test that a reflex or the efference copy passes by construction is not understanding. Speed against methods that each learned one skill from a signal built for it is not a like-for-like figure.
- **No claim leaves the project** before the life has passed its registered bar and a skeptic has read the claim against the log (risk 25).

**A64. The local speed levers, with their build steps** (the owner's word; amends A59's schedule; sections 9, 11)
- **Decided:** all five exact levers of section 9, each built by the session that owns its code:
  - **R10** (core, day 15): the critics' rank-one updates on a second performance core, joined before each solve and each save (14–26 ms);
  - **P7** (parent, day 13): her ear off the tick, joined at her reply tick (about 5 ms);
  - **W7** (world, day 13): the ears, the tract, touch and the observer computed while the GPU draws (4–8 ms), and the world's per-step Python compiled, bit-equal to the Python it replaces (2–4 ms);
  - **R8** (core, day 12): the live night's physics and the replay on separate cores, merged in a fixed order (the night 12–25 wall minutes, not 18–32).
- **Under the digest rules** (A59): every join at a fixed sim tick, blocking if the work is late; the BLAS thread count pinned per operation; each lever kept only if the eight language digests, the `sim` profile and SimWorld's exact replay are unchanged.
- **Birth waits for none.** An exact lever leaves the body bit-identical, so one not ready by S5b joins at a night boundary after birth, once its three rules hold on a copy. It is not a change of body (A20).
- **The machine's share.** The largest local factor measured is other work on this Mac: at a load of 10–15 the eyes' render ran about 4× slower (section 9). While the life runs, it has the Mac's first claim after the owner's own use: other sessions' sims, renders and verification copies run at `nice -n 19`, one at a time; the room camera renders only while someone watches; and the load is logged each life day (C72).
- **The steering's lag.** Claude's round trip caps the pace at 60/R × real time (A59). If P5 measures a round trip longer than a window's wall time at the pace the levers reach (26 s at 2.3×), each row is applied two windows after its digest (the digest's tick + 800), fixed before birth (C71). It is a constant of the parent's method: it changes when a row lands, never what the body senses or does, and it is never set by the child's rates.
- **Not reopened:** reduced precision, a longer step or a looser solver, GPU physics, parallel lives, and the lesson lag or the late solve (A59). Fast learning is counted in life hours (A62): a faster wall clock bought by changing the body buys nothing the bar counts, and risks the only seed.

**A65. B3 answered: the lighter meshes for its eyes; the colour camera's own view and the sun's shadow kept** (B3, answered by the lead for the owner; sections 3.4, 5.1, 9, 10)
- **The rule:** a render shortcut is taken only if what the body senses stays the same within its own sensor's noise and a pixel of its native image. The render is the world's, which reality replaces on the robot (A36's line); this keeps the owner's complete reality (decision 3) at the eyes' own resolution, and every sensor where the robot has it.
- **(a) Yes: a lighter copy of its own visual meshes, for its eyes only.**
  - Each of the G1's visual meshes is decimated (quadric edge collapse: Garland and Heckbert 1997) and loaded world-side for the three views alone. The G1's file stays byte-identical, and the physics, the collision geoms, the room camera and the films keep the full meshes.
  - The coarsest copy that passes the rule, at the poses where its own body is nearest its eyes (a hand before its face, an arm across its view), is used. It is measured at W3's reopening and set before birth, never after (C69).
  - It saves about 8–18 ms over the two grey eyes, since half their render was its own meshes (hiding them, a test only: section 9), and about half as much again in the colour view: about 12–27 ms in all, an estimate.
- **(c) No: the colour camera keeps its own view at its own place.** The evidence:
  - Its RGB sensor's centre-line is 15 mm from the left imager's (RealSense's documentation, read 2026-09-25; the datasheet is still read at C45).
  - Cut from the left eye's render, its colour would sit exactly on the left eye's grey. On the robot it is displaced by the parallax: 2.9° at 0.3 m and 5.7° at 0.15 m, where its hands and the toys it reaches for are. That is 1.1 and 2.2 of the colour window's 2.6° cells, or 9–17 of the fovea's pixels: far past the rule.
  - By A36's line, a sensor seen from another place makes the real G1 a change of body, as B5 says of the microphones.
  - Reprojecting the left eye's colour through its depth to the camera's place would keep the parallax, but not the band a near hand uncovers against the room (about 2.4° beside a hand at 0.3 m and 3.9° at 0.2 m, with a wall 2 m behind), so it fails too.
  - Its saving, about 10–15 ms (an estimate), is smaller after (a), which lightens the colour view's own draw.
- **(b) No: the sun's shadow stays** (the owner's decision 3). A shadow changes the light far past the rule.
- **What it changes:** with the exact levers and (a) the tick is about 65–135 ms, 1.1–2.3× real time (section 9's case C, an estimate that C12 and C48 measure). No 150 ms bar binds a lockstep life, since a slower tick costs wall time only, so the birth checklist's tick rule becomes a measurement.
- **Why the lead answers it:** the design asked B3 only if the tick passed 150 ms, a bar the owner's word removes. Answered by this rule, the answer changes nothing the body could sense beyond its own noise, and keeps every sensor where the robot has it.

**A66. The human-pace ledger, an optional report** (the owner's word, "i dont care about huuman pace"; amends A58; sections 0, 1, 12)
- **Decided:** the ledger stays in section 12 as an optional report the owner does not need. It is never a headline, and nothing waits for it: birth does not, and C62's reading of its recalled rows is done only if it is kept.
- **The firewall stands in full,** printed or not. If it is reported at all, it is the ledger frozen with its digest before birth, every row, passes and failures alike. Nothing in the body, the parent, the brief, the tests or the build reads it, and the parent's pace is never moved toward a norm. A ledger not frozen before birth is never reported.
- **In the headlines instead:** fast learning in the body's own units (A62), and the firsts (A63).
- **Kept from it:** the body's own exposures to criterion (its second table's right column) join A62's measure; the infant column is optional with the rest.
- **Why:** the owner's word. A ruler he does not want should not shape the reports, and the firewall costs nothing to keep.

A67–A70 were decided by the lead later on 2026-09-25, on the core's builds: A67 on the mossy list R6c left to it, the other three on the C54 builder's report after 903cb75 and e351813. They are the body's constants and design, so ours, each fixed before birth; the follow-up building A68–A70 on `sim-core` is under way.

**A67. The G1's mossy fibres: the body's own signals** (the lead's decision; sections 7.5, 10; A44, C50; built in e351813)
- **Decided:** the cerebellum is on at birth for the G1 (`SIM_CFG`'s `cereb` 1). Its inputs are the body's own signals only, nothing seen or heard, in four kinds, each number named in order (`SimAnatomy.mossy`) and declared by its middle and half-range (`SimAnatomy.cerebellar`, `body/sim/anatomy.py`):
  1. **the efference copy of the effectors' acts**, the tick's own act held through its 15 sub-steps (the pontine route of the motor command: Ito 1984; Apps and Garwicz 2005, Nat Rev Neurosci 6:297–311): each joint's setting of the tract, the gaze and the limbs, 0–4 about the hold (middle 2, half-range 2), the gate's own act only, never the cord's patterns or a reflex's forced act: 56 (the token output's lines out, A68);
  2. **the joints' positions and velocities** (the spinocerebellar proprioception: Bosco and Poppele 2001, Physiol Rev 81:539–568): each of the 43 joints' angle over its range in the model file (`RANGES`, checked joint by joint against the file), then each one's velocity: 86;
  3. **the inertial units** (the vestibular route: Apps and Garwicz 2005): the torso's and the pelvis's accelerometer and gyro: 12;
  4. **touch and contact** (the cutaneous spinocerebellar input): the Dex3's 16 zones' log(1 + F / 1 N), the observer's outside torque on each of the 43 joints over its limit, and the base's outside wrench over the body's weight, in the touch channel's own units (middle 0, half-range 1), without the onsets: 65.
- **As built:** 298 numbers, the efference copy 135 of them, since the builder read "every effector's acts" literally and gave the token output one line per symbol too (79, each with middle and half-range 0.5, silent unless its symbol was said). A68 takes those lines out: 219.
- **The closest declared sense:** the anatomy declares no orientation (its vestibular channel carries the accelerometer and the gyro alone, 3.4), so the accelerometer's specific force, which way is down, stands for the inertial units' orientation, as the otoliths' own signal does. Nothing is estimated for it; an estimated attitude would be a new sensor model for S5a.
- **Ours, disclosed:** the half-ranges the model file does not give: `MOSSY_SPEED` = 0.27 / 0.15 = 1.8 rad/s for the velocities and the gyros (the big step's pace, the fastest the body's own act moves a joint's target; the file declares no joint velocity) and `MOSSY_G` = 9.81 m/s² for the accelerometers (one g, so which way is down spans the fibre's range). The inertial units' declared cutoffs (34.9 rad/s, 157 m/s²) are their sensors' full scales, under which a turn or a tilt would barely move a fibre. The touch channel's unit half-ranges stand until the Dex3-1 arrays' range is read (C44).
- **The organ unchanged** (`CEREB`, 7.5): 4,096 granule units of 4 fibres, 10% active; 29 readouts (the waist, the arms and the legs; the Dex3's joints none); the flocculus on the gaze window's yaw and pitch.
- **Why:** R6c left the humanoid's list to the lead. Each kind is a route the sources name for the body's commands and state, which is what its two teachers' errors come from (A44). The first list's estimated motor torques brought the organ's own torque back into its input and drove the pure law into oscillation (7.5); the G1's list carries the observer's outside torques instead.
- **Measured** (e351813, C38's setup): 3.68 ms a tick (245 µs a sub-step), every one of the 45,000 sub-steps taught; the stub's building of the numbers 1.91 ms, the world's share; the movement units and the pattern generator's cycles the same to the digit, since nothing of the tick reads the cerebellum.
- **Open:** the world's hook on the G1 (C73); the constants from their sources (C50).

**A68. The token output leaves the cerebellum's efference copy** (the lead's decision, on A67's build; sections 7.5, 10; built in 1ecd68d: 219 mossy numbers in the order tract 10, gaze 3, waist 3, arms 14, hands 14, legs 12, then the 43 angles, the 43 velocities, the torso's and pelvis's accelerometer and gyro 12, the 16 Dex3 zones, contact on the 43 joints, the base's force and torque 6; motor 11 checks the order number by number; the law 1.80 ms a tick at load 2)
- **Decided:** the silent token output's 79 lines leave the mossy input. The efference copy is the motor commands alone: the tract's 10 articulators, the gaze's 3 and the limbs' 43 joints, 56 numbers, and the mossy input is 219 (56 + 86 + 12 + 65).
- **Why:** the cerebellum smooths joints. Its readouts are torques at the waist's, the arms' and the legs' joints and its flocculus a correction to the window's counter-shift; the token output has no joint and no body sense for it to predict.
- **What follows:** removing the token output (4.9) leaves the cerebellum's input as it was. The expansion stays 4,096 units, about 19 times the 219.

**A69. The pattern generator's extension at 2A/3: no net excursion** (the lead's decision, on C54's build; amends A48; sections 3.7, 10; built in 6d20f67: the extension step 0.0172 rad against the flexion's 0.0259; net excursion per movement ≤ 3.5e-18 rad with nothing cancelled (motor 6 checks 63 joint-movements); units, cycles and fractions unchanged to the digit)
- **Found** (the C54 builder): 2 flexion ticks at +A and 3 extension ticks at −A move the targets 2A toward flexion and 3A back, a net A toward extension every cycle.
- **Decided:** the extension steps at 2A/3 a tick, so each cycle's net excursion is zero: a kick is a flexion and a return.
- **Why:** the sources give the phases' durations (Thelen and Fisher 1982, 1983), not their excursions, and a generator with no posture term (A48) must not carry a limb toward extension cycle by cycle.

**A70. Every state a save left out, saved; exact replay across a save and a night** (the lead's decision, on the C54 builder's report; sections 3.7, 11; A18; built in 103bf06)
- **Found** (the C54 builder): the born cry's breath clock (`cry_t`) is not saved, so a save in mid-cry restarts the breath group.
- **Decided:** the cry's breath clock and every other R6h state that a save left out are saved, and a test replays a life across a save and load and across a night against the life that went on, bit for bit. Exact replay is the law.
- **Found for this sync** (the code at e351813; the follow-up names its own list): the save keeps each motor effector's `act_inv` confusion, kappas and reliability, the performance error's means, its own fatigue, the kappa correction's running sums and `act_inv`'s pending pairs. It leaves out:
  - the movement unit's held settings and the cry's clock, which the night resets before its save (A18), so only a save inside a day loses them; R6's own working state (a chunk under way, the act last tick, the forward half's foresight) is left out and reset the same way;
  - the cord's counts, an instrument's, which run on across nights;
  - orienting's memory of whether the face fired on the tick before, which decides whether a face "appeared": it runs on across the night and is lost at a load, so after a reload a morning's first face could count as appeared where the life that went on would not count it.
  - The pattern generator needs nothing: its rhythm is found again from birth.

**A72. The Dex3's servo gain** (the lead's decision at S5a, 2026-09-25; sections 3.3, A39)
- **Decided:** the legs', the waist's and the arms' gains are Unitree's published ones (unitree_sdk2's g1_ankle_swing_example: hip 60, knee 100, ankle 40; waist yaw 60, roll and pitch 40; arms 40; kd 1, the knee's 2). The Dex3's fingers take kp = the joint's torque limit / STEP_BIG (0.27 rad) and kd 0.1, so a big closing step spends the finger's full torque.
- **Why.** Unitree's own Dex3 example grips at kp 1.5, kd 0.1. Under the body's step law (a target the measured angle plus a step) that gain could not move a finger against the model's own friction loss (0.3 N·m) on most steps, so the hand could not close on a toy. The gain is set by the law's own step and the joint's own limit, never by a rate. unitree_rl_gym's locomotion gains (legs 100–150) are a walking policy's, not the servo's, and are not used.

**A73. The joints' pain is the gear's load** (the lead's reading of A37 at S5a, 2026-09-25; sections 3.4, 3.7, 6)
- **Decided:** a joint hurts on a tick when the load its gear carries, the motor's torque less its rotor's inertia times the joint's acceleration (MuJoCo's `qfrc_actuator − dof_armature × qacc`), as its tick's largest 10 ms mean, passes the joint's declared torque limit. The base keeps F_pain (3 × the weight). The observer (A37) still gives touch its outside torques.
- **Why.** A37 names the robot's damage line: "a load from outside larger than a joint's motor can hold back-drives its gear". The gear's load is that line itself. The observer's outside torque also counts the mat carrying the body's own weight: lying still on its back, the waist and shoulders read past their limits on 70–80% of babble ticks, a body in pain for lying down.
- **Measured on the born loop** (S5a: the core at every learning rate 0 in the G1 world with the parent's lane, d 128, 200 ticks; C43): pain on 25% of ticks, the born cry on 22%. Nearly every step over a limit has a contact on that joint's links: the right wrist and fingers on the mat (most), the hand against its own hip, torso or other shoulder, a thumb against its own index finger. The strong shoulder and elbow drive the hand into things, and the wrist's 5 N·m and the fingers' 1.4 N·m motors take loads past their limits. None was at a range's end.
- **Kept, not tuned.** This is the robot's own physics: a G1 flailing on its back overloads its hands. Pain is how a body learns not to, and the rate is written down, never fitted (the owner's law). What it costs is disclosed under A75.

**A74. The born table keeps the article "a"** (the lead's reading of the two branches' tables at S5a)
- The parent's 50 birth words include "a" (the lexicon keeps its word row and its letter row apart); the core's born table refused a one-letter word, since its tokenizer keys each row by its text. Decided: a one-letter birth word keeps its own row, keyed "a_" (`anatomy.word_key`). The sim profile's placeholder words are unaffected, and its pin holds.

**A75. The parent's lane** (the lead's build at S5a, 2026-09-25; sections 4.3–4.10, A1–A3, A29, A40, A49; `body/sim/lane.py`)
- **Built:** each day tick, after the physics and before the ears, her percept is filled from what a person in her place could see or hear, her conduct ticks on it with the child's tract and token, her line starts and sounds from her mouth into the child's ears, the words channel labels what was said while she is audible and the scaffold is on, her feelings set her face, and the born reading of her face gives the face channel (A1, A2: while the face test passes two ticks, held 30 out of view; A49's scaffold). Her lexicon maps one to one onto the anatomy's born table. It costs about 1 ms a tick and is saved whole, a line under way included (exact replay across a save).
- **Her percept of pain is outward only** (A40's rule: nothing of the child's inside reaches her). She hears its born cry and sees a blow to its body past F_pain. She never reads its joints' pain flags. Her other events: a toy's fall, a toy come into its hand by itself (none of hers handed over in the last 40 ticks, A2), a toy lost, a toy given to her, a roll, sitting, lying face down 100 ticks (A13's distress), and a low charge. Not yet made: the movements her copying reads (A52), and being hit, which A25c makes impossible.
- **What it costs, disclosed:** at the born loop's measured rate (A73) the child cries on about a fifth of ticks. Her concern at a cry lasts about 4 s and no smile answers it (A13), so at birth nearly every judgment of hers is suppressed. In the plumbing run she judged the child's echoed "mama" worth a smile twice and showed neither. Early learning is pain's until the body stops overloading its hands. This is the consequence of the robot's own physics and of A13, kept and reported.
- **Seen in the plumbing run:** at every learning rate 0, the child's silent token output echoes her words through its fast store ("mama", "oh", "here", "is"), and her ear hears them as words: the store's recall is live from birth.

**A76. The bottle on its dock** (the lead's build at S5a, 2026-09-25; sections 5.3, A17)
- **Built:** a toy-sized bottle (56 mm across, 12 cm with its teat, 0.12 kg with milk; ours) whose three shapes are named `bottle*`, so each charges the child while it touches a palm, whoever holds it. It stands on the charge pad, its dock.
- **The dock's place, searched, not chosen:** the nearest place beside the right hand that the lying arm reaches with 5 cm to spare, at least 15 cm from every other toy, where her planner finds a spot to kneel to it. Found 0.26 m from the right hand and 0.35 m from its shoulder (the arm reaches 0.55). Beside the left hand it stood where she kneels to attend the child (the side it faces when flat), and her hands could not reach their holds past it: two of her tests failed, and pass with it on the right.
- **What a feed does now:** her feed line, then her body walks to the dock, picks the bottle up, kneels beside the child, swaps it to the near hand and brings it into the palm. A closed fist has no palm to give to (C41), so she refuses and her day plan offers again after 40 ticks. A babbling child opens its hands; a still one does not, and goes hungry.
- **Left open:** "all done." ends a feed only once "all" and "done" are her words: they are growth words whose moment is the day plan's to show, not yet built.

**A77. Her day, the episode layer (P4's first build)** (the lead's build at S5a, 2026-09-25; sections 4.7, 4.10, A17; `body/sim/lang/dayplan.py`)
- **Built:** the day laid out at dawn from her own stream (spawn key 7): the wake (greeting once she kneels beside it, then the call); 4.7's blocks, their lengths drawn and scaled to fill 300 to 23,000 exactly (floor play opens the day; the absences never first, last or two running); the winding down; goodnight. The meal preempts everything but the conduct's urgent lines when the charge light is low, with the bedtime feed under 0.6 (A17). Floor play offers every 150–300 ticks (showing a focus toy, peekaboo, an ask of her teaching, the call); show time asks her growth words in order when the room and the moment can show them; away is a goodbye, calls from the hall every 600 ticks, and a return after the block or after 3 of its vocal turns in 40 ticks, never with the charge under 0.5, within 100 ticks of its cry or in distress. Her attention is .4 at her own tasks. A shorter day scales every length.
- **Measured** (`test_sim_lane` 7–9): 200 days laid out as 4.7 says; a 2,400-tick day with a still child runs from "hi pip." to "night night pip." with goodbyes and returns, and replays exactly from a save in the middle; the meal offers the bottle, retries no sooner than 40 ticks, and ends when full.
- **Not yet:** the formal trials by stage and the tests of understanding by milestone (A55, C36), hand-overs and the ball rolled to its hands, the moment words ("all", "done", "wow", "thanks"), and the scaffolding ladders (closed at birth, A25c).

**A78. The eyes at the anatomy's sizes (W3r, the lead's build at S5a, 2026-09-25; sections 3.4, A38, A42; `body/sim/eyes.py`)**
- **Built:** the D435's three views rendered into one buffer with one read-back: the two grey imagers at 336 × 192 (88.3° × 58°, 3 px a degree) and the colour camera at 238 × 134 (69.4° × 42.5°), 15 mm beside the left imager. Grey is the render's luma (Rec. 601's weights, ours until the OV9282's response is read, C45). eye_p (172): each grey eye's periphery, 56 × 32, as 7 × 4 cells × luminance ON and OFF, and the colour camera's 5 × 3 cells × red–green and blue–yellow ON and OFF. eye_f (1,536): each grey eye's 64 × 64 fovea through the born bank (centre-surround ON and OFF, centre sd 1 px and surround 3 px; oriented energy at 4 orientations and periods of 3 and 6 px, Gabor envelopes 0.56 of a period; all ours, disclosed), pooled in 8 × 8 px cells, and the colour window, the same 21° at the left eye's gaze direction in the colour image, 8 × 8 cells × the two opponent axes ON and OFF. A1's face test keeps its reach: 80 px at 3 px a degree.
- **Multisampling off** (the lead's decision): a render is a function of the state alone, byte for byte (checked), so the replay's exactness does not rest on the GPU's sample pattern.
- **Measured:** the codes equal their definitions computed by hand (`test_sim_eyes` 4); the three views cost 57 ms a tick with the sun's shadow (24 ms without) and the codes 5 ms; the core with her lane and the eyes ran 177 ms a tick at d 128 while another suite ran.
- **Not yet:** the camera model (A38: exposure, noise, motion blur, rolling rows, gamma; C45's constants), the eyes' lighter mesh copy (A65, C69), the visual onset cue (A43, C49: onset_periph reads nothing), and the eye check on the new code (C3, C48; `tools/sim_eye_check.py` still reads the first build's code). face_fovea and face_periph read nothing (C39 option a); the born template is an instrument.

**A79. The motors as the real ones: rotor inertia and friction** (the lead's decision at S5a, 2026-09-25; A39, C46; `g1scene.MOTORS`)
- **Found:** the stock file (Menagerie's `g1_with_hands.xml`) gives every joint one default, armature 0.01 and friction loss 0.3, marked by its makers as needing tuning. The same repository's MJX G1 (`g1_mjx.xml`), the variant MuJoCo Playground transferred to the real robot (Zakka et al. 2025), carries each motor's reflected rotor inertia: 0.0251 at the knees and hip rolls, 0.0102 at the hip pitches and yaws and the waist's yaw, 0.0072 at the ankles and the waist's roll and pitch, 0.0036 at the shoulders, elbows and wrist rolls, 0.0043 at the wrist pitches and yaws, with friction loss 0.1.
- **Decided:** the 29 body joints take those values, set on the compiled model, so the stock file stays byte for byte. The Dex3's joints: A80.
- **Why.** The actuators are the main gap between a legged robot's simulation and its reality (Hwangbo et al. 2019). A rotor inertia too large makes every impact load the gear more: under the flat default the born loop's wrists and fingers were in pain on a quarter of its ticks (A73).
- **Measured:** with A79 and the observer (A81), pain fell from 25% to 11.5% of the born loop's ticks and the cry from 22% to 10.5% (d 128, 200 ticks at learning rate 0), nearly all of it now on the Dex3's fingers.

**A80. The Dex3-1 as Unitree specifies it** (the lead's decision at S5a, 2026-09-25; A37, A39, A72, C44, C46; `g1scene.DEX3`)
- **Read** (Unitree's Dex3-1 page, 2026-09-25): seven joints a hand, six on the F-1515-108 micro brushless joint (1:108; ideal 0.76 N·m; 0.49 N·m turning its own way; 1.37 N·m held against a load turning it back; 23 rad/s) and the thumb's rotation on the geared F-1515-214 (1:214; 1.498; 0.86; 3.1; 11 rad/s); 33 pressure sensors in 9 pads, each 10 g to 2,500 g (C44).
- **Found:** the stock file gives the six 1.4 N·m, their holding figure, as their driving strength (about three times the real), and every joint armature 0.01.
- **Decided:** each motor's ideal torque is its force range; its gear's friction, the ideal less the driving figure (0.27 and 0.638 N·m), is the joint's friction loss, so a finger drives with Unitree's 0.49 (0.86); the holding figure is the joint's pain line, the gear's load it takes before it back-drives (A37, A73). The rotor's inertia is not published: ours, from the motor's size (a 1515 inner rotor about 7 mm across and 12 mm long: 2.1e-8 kg·m², half again for the gearbox's first stage), reflected through its reduction: 3.7e-4 and 1.4e-3 kg·m², until the datasheet gives it (C46). The speed limits wait for A39's torque–speed envelope.
- **Measured:** with A79–A81, pain on the born loop fell to 6.7% of ticks and the cry to 5% (300 ticks at learning rate 0), all of it now at the wrists, none on the fingers; the child saw her smile at birth (its face reading at 1.0).

**A82. The palmar grasp closes in big steps** (the lead's decision at S5a, 2026-09-25; section 3.7, A11, A80; `reflexes.closing_act`)
- **Found:** under the Dex3's real motors a small step's servo torque (0.25 N·m) is below the finger gear's friction (0.27 N·m); the target re-anchors at the measured angle every tick, so the error never grows, and the grasp could never close the hand.
- **Decided:** the grasp steps each closing joint one big step a tick (its torque drives the finger at Unitree's 0.49 N·m). The newborn's grasp is strong, bearing its weight for moments in the traction response (recalled: Twitchell 1965). The small step was ours; its size and the step law are unchanged elsewhere, and a finger's own small steps stall against its gear's friction, as the real hand's would.
- **What follows:** a hand resting with its palm on the mat closes into a fist and presses its own palm (41 N at rest in both hands; C41), which the palm's own-hand force counts; letting go stays the hand's own act (A11).

**A81. The observer built as A37 wrote it** (the lead's correction at S5a, 2026-09-25; A37, A73, C43; `body/sim/observer.py`)
- **Found:** the first S5a build handed the body the world's exact contact torques (the physics' contact forces through its Jacobians) as "the observer's": a sense no robot has. Touch's 98 outside-torque numbers, the base's pain and the cerebellum's mossy fibres read them. A cheat, now removed.
- **Built:** the generalized-momentum observer (De Luca and Mattone 2005; Haddadin, De Luca and Albu-Schaeffer 2017) on the robot's own sensed signals alone: the encoders' angles and velocities, the motors' torques from current, the pelvis unit's accelerometer and gyro (with their declared noise) and its attitude, with the robot's own model file. Every 10 ms, the joints' outside torques through a first-order lag (gain 50 per s); the base's outside wrench by Newton–Euler on the base rows, the joints' accelerations from their velocities' change. What the model does not carry reads as outside torque, as on a real robot: friction while a joint moves, a joint at its stop. It starts at birth from the static estimate of the born state. The gear's load for pain (A73) is the sensed torque less the rotor's inertia times the encoder's velocity change.
- **Measured against the truth** (an instrument, C43; the born loop's babble, 1,500 samples): correlation 0.925; the joints' median error 2.3% of their limits, the worst the fingers (their friction, 0.21 of their limits); the base's force unbiased (326 N against 326 N true), its single samples within 38 N at the median; 0.08 ms a sample.
- **Where world truth still reaches the body, disclosed:** the words channel (A29) and the smile's level (A49), both scaffolds with removal tests; the pelvis's attitude, the encoders, the motors' torques and the images are ideal until their sensors' noise is read (C45, C46).

**A83. The room's sounds from its physics (W5's first part; the lead's build at S5a, 2026-09-25; section 5.2; `body/sim/sounds.py`)**
- **Built:** every physics step, each contact pair new since the step before that closes faster than 5 cm/s (read from the bodies' spatial velocities at the contact point, in the state the contact was found in) is an impact: a toy against anything sounds as that toy, the G1's own link against the room as its housing's knock on foam, her foot on the floor as her step. Each toy's sound is made once from 5.2's description, as damped modes and a click of noise (modal synthesis: van den Doel, Kry and Pai 2001): the ball's soft rubber bounce, the block's hollow knock, the cup's clink, the drum's boom. The rattle's beads, the car's wheels, the bear's bell, the ring's crinkle and the bottle's slosh sound while they move, louder with speed. Loudness grows linearly with the impact's speed (0.06 Pa peak at 1 m at 1 m/s, clipped at 4 times: ours). Each source is placed at its event for the two ears, by day; its state is saved with the world.
- **Measured:** the ball dropped 0.4 m sounds at its landing at the fall's own speed (2.81 m/s against 2.80); under babble the child's own limbs knock the mat on most ticks, gently, up to 74 dB peak at a slap; a still room is silent. 2.6 ms a tick.
- **Not yet:** the room's echo (B9), a toy's squeak only when squeezed (the duck squeaks on any strike), her voice's and footsteps' levels checked against sources, and the sounds' constants read from recordings of the real toys.

**A84. The visual onset cue built** (A43 as decided; the lead's build at S5a, 2026-09-25; C49; `eyes.Eyes._onset`)
- **Built:** once a tick, each grey periphery's change since the last tick, over the scene's mean luminance (the level the retina adapts to), less the median change over both eyes (the whole image moving with the head); a place counts only where nothing near it (2 periphery px, about 4°) passed the threshold the tick before, so an appearance fires and a motion under way does not keep firing; times one less the place's habituation (each onset halves the next there, recovering over 10 s: Sokolov 1963); none while the trunk turns faster than 0.5 rad/s (the torso unit's sensed gyro). The strongest place past 0.2 (a newborn's contrast threshold at its best, 10–20%: Banks and Salapatek 1978, recalled) fires, its direction from the fovea's centre given: onset_periph, orienting's third born cue and the event lines "a visual onset on the left / right". Its memory is saved with the world.
- **Measured:** a ball appearing 0.6 m out fires toward it, at its outline; the room's own rate with the child lying still is 7 of 20 ticks, because nothing in it is ever quite still (the legs sinking at rest under Unitree's gains, the duck rocking on its round bottom, the parent moving; C49 writes these down, never tunes them). Its constants are ours or recalled until C49 reads them from their sources.

**A85. The eye check on W3r's code, measured** (C3, C48; the lead at S5a, 2026-09-25; `tools/sim_eye_check.py`)
- **Measured** (the ten toys and her face, 0.3–1.2 m, the gaze aimed with 1.5° of error, midday light): with 40 training views a class the nonlinear readout on eye_f reads 0.59 (the sun's shadow) and 0.61 (none), 0.64 and 0.67 through the core's own projection (d 512 and its LayerNorm); with 80 a class, 0.72 and 0.74, and 0.74 and 0.76 through the core's projection, still rising with the views (0.60, 0.71, 0.74 at 27, 53, 80). The first build's colour cells read 0.865 at 40.
- **Where the identity is:** the colour window alone reads 0.81; the grey foveae through the born bank alone 0.29 (centre-surround 0.21, oriented energy 0.29). In the dim room a small toy far away covers few of the window's pixels and its grey edges vary with distance and light, while its colour does not. Contrast relative to the fovea's mean (the retina's adaptation) and divisive normalization of the cells' energies (Heeger 1992) were tried offline on the same views: the grey alone rose to 0.34 at best and the whole code stayed at 0.65–0.67. A42's bank is kept as built.
