- **Verdict:** C3's bar (0.75) is reached through the core's projection without the sun's shadow and just missed with it, at 80 views a class and rising; the body sees far more than 80 views of each toy. Written down; the bank is not tuned to the check.

**A86. She kneels clear of the child's trunk, not its limbs** (the lead's decision at S5b, 2026-09-25; A4, A25c, A6)
- **Found at S5b:** the first meal failed. The charge fell under 0.35 at tick 12,772 and in the next 5,000 ticks the child was never fed; it cried on 35% of those ticks. Her bottle offers were refused: "no spot to kneel beside the child: every side is blocked", then "the child keeps moving where she would kneel". Her planner kept every kneeling frame 3 cm from all of the child's shapes, and a babbling child's limbs sweep every spot beside it.
- **Decided:** since A25c the child's limbs pass through her body without force. Her kneeling (the spot, kneeling down, shuffling in, the turn on her knees, getting up) is planned 3 cm clear of all of the child first, as A4 has it; only when no such way exists, a second pass plans it 3 cm clear of its trunk (its pelvis, waist and torso), as a thrashing baby's arm bumps a kneeling parent's knee and she stays. So wherever a clear spot exists nothing she did changes (her acts' tests unchanged); beside a babbling child she kneels anyway. While she kneels where only its trunk was clear, her per-tick standoff (A25b) measures its trunk only too: measured against its arms, it had pushed her base back up to 0.4 m and then reset at once as she walked off, a jump her motion refused. The choice is saved with her. Her hands' holds and touches are unchanged.
- **Measured:** a babbling child with its charge at 0.30 was fed on 27 ticks in 800 and its charge rose to 0.53 (before: 3 fed ticks, all by its own hand bumping the bottle on its dock).

**A87. S5b's first day, measured** (the lead, 2026-09-25; `tools/sim_life.py`, d 512, the real voice, every learning rate 0; stopped at tick 18,001 for A86)
- **The tick:** median 252 ms, mean 289, 95th percentile 398 (the eyes' three views 89 ms, the world's apply 109 ms, the core the rest), with other work on the machine; C12's heat-soaked measure is still to take on a quiet machine.
- **The body:** pain on 1.6% of ticks and the cry on 1.4% before the hunger (A73's rate with the real motors, A79–A80); the cry on 35% of hungry ticks (the born cry on a low charge, A47); the visual onset on 63% of ticks (C77).
- **Her talk:** 3,680 words in 18,001 ticks, about 4,900 a life day, twice 4.6's 1,500–2,500: most are replies to the child's token output, which at birth emits a symbol on about a third of ticks and echoes her own words through its fast store ("is", "oh", "mama"). Her method (the parent's rate) is to be set against this before birth, never by the child's learning.
- **Her smile:** 39 approvals spread across the day, but the child's face reading was positive on 199 ticks, all between ticks 2,054 and 3,202: at every learning rate 0 its gaze wanders and, with no born face detector (C39 a), nothing but her voice's side turns it toward her face. The reward is sparse at birth, as First 1's claim assumes.
- **Her day:** the wake, floor play, show time, motor time and her absences ran as laid out.

**A88. One reward: her face. Pain is cortisol. No charge, no bottle, no charger; the objects are for teaching** (the owner's decision, 2026-09-26; built by the lead the same day)
- **The owner's words:** "i want g1 to only get dopime from parent in sim no charging or anything? when parent smiiles with face it gets it when ot frowns it gets cortisol. we need parent to become the teacher of reality." Then: "also add pain is cortisol tp plan. no bottle no charger. objects are for teaching." The reasoning and the plan: `docs/audit/teacher_of_reality.md`.
- **The rule:** dopamine, up or down, comes only from her face (a smile's rise felt positive, a frown's negative, as 6.1 says). Cortisol comes from her frown (the dip's stress, the amygdala's face− tag) and from its own pain.
- **The core:** `RewardSource.dopamine` (body/core/anatomy.py), on by default. A source with it off is cortisol: its term never enters the reward, the critics or the actor; its size raises the body's stress at `stress_gain` (0.5; as a dopamine dip does, body/core/mouth.py) in `_sense` (body/core/senses.py); the term stays in the tick's terms, so the amygdala's heads and tag and the cord's cry still read it. Source 0 must pay. The cry's charge line is optional in the core (body/core/cord.py). The language body's sources all pay, so its pins hold (checked at this build: default, served and switches, threaded and pinned).
- **The G1:** `SimAnatomy`'s rewards are `[FaceIncrement("face", clip=2), JointPain("pain", signs=(-1,), dopamine=False)]`; the amygdala's heads fall from 5 to 3 (face ±, pain −). The charge channel (2 numbers), its drain and effort integral, the weakness law (the limits × (0.3 + 0.7h)), the charger contact rule, the cry's charge line and her `charge_low` event are gone; the joints' limits are the declared ones every tick. The frame's truth loses `drain` and `fed`; the world's save loses `h`, `dh`, `drain`, `fed` (a world saved before A88 does not load: no life is kept).
- **The room:** the bottle (its three shapes), its dock (the pad, its ring, their materials) and its slosh are gone; the room rebuilt by `make_g1room.py` (596 geoms, 72 bodies). Every object left is hers to teach with or a registered test's (`teacher_of_reality.md` 2b).
- **Her words:** the 50 birth words are unchanged: "bottle" stays a word she knows and, with no bottle in the room, never shows (a first try made "rattle" a birth word in its place; it moved her trials, her foils and her labels at birth, where the rattle lies against its hand, so the word list was left alone: the environment's shape is the owner's call, the word list part of it). The meal's frames, intents (`feed`, `feed_more`, `feed_done`), routine words and `offer_bottle` act are gone, with the day plan's meal, its bedtime feed and its charge rule for absences (`dayplan.py`); the hand-over's "keep" branch (the bottle held in the palm until full) is gone. The measured tables `peak_lines.json` and `trial_lines.json` keep their bottle lines as measured data.
- **The stress path, as built:** a pain tick adds 0.5 to stress (its |term| 1 × stress_gain), decaying with a half-life of 240 ticks (36 s): the waking lesson's plasticity × (1 + stress/10) and each gate's choice flattened by the same factor for that long. No slow trace is added: the amygdala's tag carried into the night is the slow arm (`teacher_of_reality.md` 1; T-D registered).
- **Tests:** world 7 is `test_no_charge`; lane 9 is `test_no_meal`; the servo test drops the weakness sweep; the language tests' meal probes stand on her label of a bottle in their own scenes; the core tests' scripted frames carry no charge; `test_amygdala` 12 expects 3 heads; the day-saved test's pain runs through its save so the cry is under way there (the charge had kept it crying). The sim profile's digests are re-pinned (tools/pins/digests.txt).
- **Retired:** M6 ("more" at a low charge), risk 14, B10 and C10 (the bottle beside its hand). A17 and A76 stand as history.
- **Open (C78):** a want the room can meet for "more" (a game asked for again) once the teacher's lessons (Phase 2) exist; "bottle" among the first 50 with nothing to show (kept, or replaced at a boundary by a word the room can show).

**A89. The teacher's build, parts 2a and 2b: her eyes on its acts, her judgments of them** (the lead, 2026-09-26; `docs/audit/teacher_of_reality.md` 2c; the owner's word: "sim teachers going to have to be very good")
- **Why:** with her face the only reward (A88), the body learns from reward only what she can see and judge. Until this build she judged words alone (a met ask, a right name, an approximation, a vocal turn), and 4.3's motor rows were never built.
- **Her eyes (2a; `body/sim/lane.py` `_hands`, `_toys`, `_events`; percept.EVENT_KINDS):** read from the toys' and its hands' places and the room's sounds, as a person watching a baby reads them, never from its inside. New events: `lifted`, `shook`, `hit`, `threw`, `reach_nearer`, `half_roll`, `head_up`, `peekaboo_act` (their thresholds in lane.py, ours, disclosed: LIFT_M 0.05, SHAKE_MPS 0.3, SHAKE_GAP 20, HIT_GAP 10, THROW_MPS 1.0, HAND_REST_MPS 0.05, HAND_MOVE_MPS 0.15, MOVED_TICKS 5, REACH_BOOK_M 0.6, BOOK_LAST 10, NEARER_M 0.01, HEAD_UP_M 0.08, PEEKABOO_ACT (10, 5)). `got` is now its own reach and hold: the hand that touches the toy moved, or reached toward it, within MOVED_TICKS; a toy come to a still hand is not got. Her first tick reads what already lies against its hands and gets nothing from it. A toy against a toy is read (`_contacts`' toys), so a hit with what it holds sounds the struck toy's name. **Her notebook:** per toy, the hand's distance at the end of its last 10 reaches (a movement's end within 0.6 m of a toy it does not hold), the source of `reach_nearer`. All of it saved and restored with her lane (`state()["eyes"]`).
- **Her judgments (2b; `Conduct._motor_judgments`, consts.MOTOR_WORTH):** within the tick, from the percept's events, unless a formal trial is under way (A60b) or she is away. Worth 2: `got`, `rolled`, `sat`; worth 1: `lifted`, `shook`, `hit`, `head_up`, `peekaboo_act`; the approximations `reach_nearer` (of `got`) and `half_roll` (of `rolled`) worth 1 until the full act has been smiled at MASTERED_N 3 times on that object. Habituation to zero: the n-th smile for the same act and object is worth w·e^(−n/HABIT_TAU 10), none under HABIT_FLOOR 0.05: from 2, thirty-seven smiles, then none (the design's A2 fell to a floor of 1, which a child could farm forever: Knox and Stone 2015's positive circuits). A new object starts n again (a new place does not yet: disclosed). Her book ({kind: {object: n}}) and its log are saved with her conduct.
- **"Yes!" at once (`_choose` 2b):** on a judgment she says a confirm line that tick if her voice is free ("good. the ball!", "yes! good."): a sound onset the born orienting turns toward (A43), so its eyes come to her face and the smile is seen before social referencing is learned. Her smile itself waits for the look as before (4.3).
- **Frowns (stage 2):** a toy it threw (`threw`) frowns as a hit does (0.5, reads −1) with "no."; stage 1 does not. Hitting her stays impossible while her body passes through the child (A25c); a frown for it waits on a body of hers that collides (the reality-gap study's item 3).
- **Measured** (`test_sim_lang` motor judgments; `test_sim_lane` 10): "got ball" judged 2 and "good. the ball!" said the same tick; the same act 37 smiles from 2 down to 0.055, then none; another toy afresh; a reach nearer 1 until got is mastered, then "past mastery"; a ball set against a still palm is not got, after two ticks of the arm's own movement it is (and "lifted", in a raised hand); a dropped duck fell, not thrown; the book and the eyes' state survive a save. In the runner (`tools/sim_life.py`) her judgments are counted by kind and her smiles the child saw (its reading rising above 0), for her rulers.
- **Left to 2c and 2d:** her lessons (the rung ladder and its setups from her notebook) and her hands (guide and knee_over reopened at a boundary, A90); the copying readers (A52) and `hit_her` (A25c) stay unbuilt.

**A90. The teacher's build, parts 2c and 2d: her lessons, her hands** (the lead, 2026-09-26; `docs/audit/teacher_of_reality.md` 2c)
- **Her lessons (2c; `lang/dayplan.py` `_lesson`):** motor time runs them, and floor play gives one on LESSON_SHARE 0.4 of its offers. The rung is read from her conduct's book of the smiles she has given on a focus toy she sees, never from the child's inside. The reach and grasp rung: the toy set within its reach by a new intent, `set_near` ("here. the X." / "look. here. the X.") with her motion's `bring_back` at `lesson_dist`: LESSON_DIST0 0.10 m out from its near hand at level 0, LESSON_STEP 0.05 farther each time "got" on that toy has been smiled at since her last lesson on it, at most LESSON_LEVELS 7 (0.40 m), a level back after NO_PROGRESS 1,000 ticks with none. Once "got" on it has 2 × MASTERED_N smiles, the handle rung: `hand_over` ("here. a X.") puts it into its hand for lifts, shakes and hits; once those have 3 × MASTERED_N, the give rung: `ask_give`. Her levels are saved with her plan; `lesson_dist` with her motion. The roll, head-up and sit rungs have no setup act yet (the toy shown beside its head on the far side is to build): the roll is judged whenever it happens.
- **Her hands (2d; `parent_motion.NOT_AT_BIRTH`):** the guide (A8, A10; within 65 N) and the knee over (76 N) reopen: a coach guides a hand within the force a person can use. The pull-to-sit and the prop stay closed (34 kg, risk 4). While any act of hers that moves its body runs (HANDS_ON: guide, knee_over, turn, pull_to_sit, prop) the conduct judges nothing it does ("a guided act itself earns nothing", 4.3; the log says "her hands on it"); its own repeat after the guide ends earns the act's worth. Every guide is counted beside First 1 as A63 says (the intervention log). Her plan does not yet ask for a guide: the rung that would (the roll, after a block with no progress) waits on its setup act.
- **Her care at its distress (found by the plumbing day of 2026-09-26, `docs/audit/teacher_of_reality.md` 6):** the born body rolled onto its front under babble (tick 408 of the day, stress-flattened gates thrashing), and on its front its wrists hurt under its 34 kg (the 5 N·m gears back-driven: pain on 41 of the next 100 ticks, the cry, stress to 27), and she spent the day comforting it (attend after attend), so no lesson ran. A parent turns a baby stuck on its tummy: a new intent `turn_over` ("oh. up." / "up. up. up!") with her `turn` act (A7, kept at birth) answers its distress (face down DISTRESS_TICKS) before comfort does; `turn` is in HANDS_ON, so the roll back is not judged as its own. Her eyes steadied against a thrashing body: `head_up` needs the head held up HEAD_UP_TICKS 5 and counts once in HEAD_UP_GAP 100 (14 in 100 ticks before); a half roll counts once in ROLL_GAP 40.
- **Two more from the plumbing day of 2026-09-26 (A89, A90 amended):** (1) `got` needs a hold: the toy kept in that hand's touch GOT_HOLD 3 ticks running, the hand having moved or reached as the touch began (15 "got" in 1,500 ticks had been babble grazing the toys beside its hands); (2) she leans in as she smiles: the `confirm` and `confirm_act` intents carry `lean_in` to its periphery (A3), so the smile is where its eyes can reach it (2 of about 60 smiles were seen that day; the share seen is her first ruler).
- **Manners of her "yes!" (A89 amended):** not while a set of hers is under way (its lines are said in full, 4.5) and not while she judges an ask (its X unnamed, A51): the smile stands, the word is dropped and logged.
- **Measured** (`test_sim_lang` her lessons and hands; `test_sim_lane` 11): `set_near` composes "here. the ball." with the bring_back, her look at it and back to its eyes; `hand_over` composes with the hand-over; nothing judged while a guide runs, the roll after it worth 2; in a short day with a still child her motor time set the focus toys within reach at 0.10 m and one "got" smile raised the next lesson on that toy to 0.15 m; the levels survive a save.

**A91. Pain pays again** (the owner's decision, 2026-09-26, on the first plumbing day's finding C79: "mb i was wrong fix this ... biology does it this way")
- **The rule now:** her face pays (±, the smile and the frown) and its own pain pays −1. Biology pays for pain: nociception reaches the dopamine neurons as a dip through the lateral habenula (Matsumoto and Hikosaka 2007), and the dip carries the stress with it (the core's law since the diary: stress += 0.5 × the dip). So `JointPain` is declared paying (`dopamine` on, the default) and nothing else changes: the amygdala's pain− head, the tag, the born cry and her concern are as at A88.
- **Why:** with nothing paying for pain (A88), no value could learn to avoid it, and the born body's roll onto its front set off the cortisol loop (C79). A brake that biology has was missing by decision, not by architecture.
- **What stays from A88:** no charge, no bottle, no charger; the objects are for teaching; the core's `RewardSource.dopamine` switch stays built (a cortisol-only source is one line to declare) and unused by any body; the language body's pins are untouched. The sim profile is re-pinned (the scripted pain at ticks 60 and 147 pays again).
- **Test:** world 7 asserts both sources pay and the switch's default. T-D (`teacher_of_reality.md`) is retired: pain moves dopamine.

**A92. The newborn's prone pattern, a born reflex** (the owner's word, 2026-09-26: "add 1", on the lead's list of what biology has that the body lacked; built the same evening)
- **Why:** the first plumbing day (C79): the body rolled onto its front and lay with its wrists under its 34 kg, the 5 N·m gears back-driven, in pain on 12% of ticks, until she turned it over. Biology does not leave a newborn there: laid on its front it turns its head to one side and holds its arms flexed under its chest, the weight on the forearms and the chest, from birth (Prechtl and Beintema 1964; Prechtl 1977; the newborn's physiological flexor tone in prone, Bly 1994). Rolling over is learned, at 3–6 months, so no roll is born here.
- **As the cord has it (`body/sim/reflexes.py` `prone`, run by the world's apply after the grasp; the switch `righting`):** from the body's own afferent alone, the torso unit's accelerometer (the tick's noisy means): while its chest-normal component reads below −0.6 g (PRONE_G; the parent's own reading of "face down"), each arm's flexion joints (the withdrawal's: the shoulder's pitch and the elbow, FLEXION) take one small flexion step a tick, and the waist yaws one small step toward the side that is up (the accelerometer's y component: the head turn, on a body with no neck, A22), unless the own act that tick steps a joint the other way (the own act wins there, as it opens the grasp); an own step the same way, or a hold, sums with it. Logged as reflex in the truth's `spinal` ("prone" per effector), no gate eligibility. The legs keep their own acts (the newborn's flexed hips would drive the thigh housings into the pelvis here: C22's artifact), disclosed. Ours: the threshold 0.6 g, the small step, the arms and the waist.
- **Measured** (`test_sim_world` 20): on its back nothing fires; turned onto its front the pattern fires every tick on both arms and the waist and the elbows flex; an own step against it is kept on its joint; with the switch off nothing fires. What it does to the wrists' pain on its front is written down there (C80), and the second plumbing day measures it in the life.
- **What it is not:** a roll, a righting of the trunk, or a hand-written fix for pain: the newborn's pattern as its sources give it, on the afferent a newborn has.

**A93. The reverse value sweep: the night carries a reward's value back along the day's path** (the lead's build, 2026-09-26, on the owner's word "build it all get this thing learning"; the owner's question: "i think sleep big part do we have to make change to hpc rem or nrem")
- **The finding:** the night's value replay was one step and unordered: the critics took the day's last 32 transitions (`v_buf`) as a pile and nudged each value toward its neighbour's, so a smile at the end of a sequence reached the act that earned it one step a night. Biology does not do that: after a reward the hippocampus replays the path backwards, and more so the larger the reward (Foster and Wilson 2006; Ambrose, Pfeiffer and Foster 2016), and replay is prioritized by its gain (Mattar and Daw 2018). One reverse sweep carries the reward's value to the act in a night: risk 1, credit across the delay, is a night mechanism as much as a day one.
- **As built (`body/core/sleep.py` `_value_sweep`; the SLEEP switch `night_reverse`, on for the G1 in SIM_CFG, off for the language body, whose pins hold):** in the frames' night, before the value replay, each band's critic (the solved fast head apart) takes the day's transitions from the tape's bands (the bands the cortex received at tick t) and the record's net reward at t, and sweeps them backwards in time in chunks of `night_reverse_chunk` 64 ticks, one TD(0) step a chunk with the head as the later chunk left it (the differential bands against the reward rate, as by day); the day's tagged episodes' windows first, highest tag first (the gain), then the whole day. The night's report carries the chunks and ticks swept.
- **What it is not:** a new reward, a new critic or a change to the day's dopamine. REM and the hippocampus's write rule are as they were (the owner's rulings).
- **Measured** (`test_night` 8): two G1s at d 32 born alike, the same 150 ticks (smiles at ticks 20, 60, 100, 140), one night each: the value of the states 8 ticks before the smiles on band 3 rose to 0.0613 with the sweep against 0.0598 without (the report: 11 chunks, 205 ticks swept over 8 tagged windows and the day, 7 bands; the fast head is solved, not swept). A direction at d 32 after one night; the life's rulers (T-A, the motor kiwi) are the measure that counts.

**A94. The smile en face: when she judges an act her face goes onto its line of sight** (the lead, 2026-09-26, on the first learning day's rulers)
- **The finding:** on day 1 of life 1 the child saw 3 of her about 160 smiles. A smile is felt only while her mouth is inside its fovea (A1's face test); A3 had her place her face in its periphery, 15° off the fovea's line, so that turning to it would be the child's own act. On its back it looks at the ceiling, and the born orienting did not bring its eyes to her: her reward was not reaching the learner, and nothing about her face could be learned.
- **As built:** a new lean-in target, `child_line` (`parent_motion.face_reach(on_line=True)`: her mouth within FACE_ON_LINE_DEG 8° of its fovea's current line, LEAN_DIST_M 0.30–0.60 from its eyes, never nearer than FACE_MIN_M, the least bend, her body clear of it; its periphery when no pose reaches the line), used by the `confirm` and `confirm_act` intents alone: she smiles en face, the position parents take to engage an infant (Stern 1974, "Mother and infant at play"; Papoušek and Papoušek 1987, intuitive parenting: the en-face distance of about 25 cm). Her calls, asks, greetings and comfort keep A3's periphery: a look to her stays the child's act there, and an ask's answer is never cued by where her face is (A51).
- **Measured** (`test_sim_lane` 10): after her judgment of the grasp, her face passes the face test in its fovea within the smile's hold.

**A95. Her hands at night, and a wedged start** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from life 1's second dawn)
- **Found:** at the first dawn she stood a whole day at the room's edge and no lesson ran. Replaying the dawn save: she had gone to sleep holding two toys (the night's `sleep` released her holds on the child, not what her hands held), so at dawn "bring back" was refused ("both her hands are full") and clearing a spot to kneel was refused the same way; and standing up from the sofa's right cushion put her 12 cm from its arm, inside every clearance the floor planner allows a start, so no path to the child could begin ("no path on the floor to there"): every act of hers was refused for 9,700 ticks.
- **As built (`parent_motion.py`):** at dusk she sets down what she holds (a `clear` of each toy) before she walks to the sofa; a fetch with both hands full sets the toy she needs less aside first, then fetches; clearing a kneeling spot with both hands full sets one aside and plans the approach anew; and the walk planner, when her start cell is not free even at BODY_R + 2 cm, takes one step to the nearest free cell within 0.6 m and plans from there. A clearing planned anew drops the rest of its approach (its shuffle in), as a kneel planned anew does: the replay's stale shuffle ("a shuffle not begun from her tall kneel") came from the old approach's shuffle left behind the new one. On the dawn save she was at the child's side, kneeling tall with her face in its fovea, within 100 ticks; then she set the car aside and went on.
- **Also found (C81):** her `turn` of a face-down child fails on the stock G1 (`test_sim_parent` 16: "not turned within her caps in 2 s", its chest 4° from face down; 17° with the prone reflex, which helps). It fails at A90's commit too, before today's reflex and reward changes, though the same test passed this morning: a pre-existing fragility of her turn's caps against 34 kg, not the reflex's doing. Left open: her `turn_over` at its distress may fail the same way; the prone reflex and the pain that pays are the body's own answers meanwhile.

**A96. The smile brought to its line of sight** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from life 1's second day, its first 1,500 ticks on A94)
- **Found:** with A94 her smiles were still unseen: 1 of 8 judgments in 1,500 ticks reached the child (its reading rose once). Two causes, from the tick log. (1) The en-face lean's approach asked its kneeling spot only for the periphery reach (`need="lean"`), so where she knelt on her heels beside it, no pose put her mouth on its fovea's line, and she smiled from the periphery it never looked at (3 judgments at one spot, no face test in the 60 ticks after each). (2) Where a spot on its line was found, the way there was a stand, a walk and two re-planned kneels (the child's far side to its head: 479 ticks with three `lean_in` acts stacked), and an unseen smile eases off 20 ticks after its start (A3): every smile she set out with was gone before she arrived.
- **As built:** `_act_lean_in` asks for a spot from which a pose puts her mouth on its line (`need="lean_line"`: `_need_ok` runs `face_reach(on_line=True)` there); where no spot does (it looks at the floor, at a toy), the periphery spot as before A94 (`a["info"]["line_spot"] = False`). A new spot within `KNEE_ROUTE_M` 0.9 m and `KNEE_ROUTE_DEG` 100° of her facing is reached on her knees (up onto the tall kneel, a turn on them, the shuffle; the tall frames checked at quarter turns) instead of by standing and walking (0.6 m and 25° before): the way round a lying child is about 0.8 m and a quarter turn. And her feelings hold a smile she is bringing (`Pulse.bringing`: her `lean_in child_line` act under way, told by the lane each tick) until she is there (`arrived`), then 20 ticks as before, never past `queue_expiry` 40 ticks from its start; seen on the way, 10 ticks after the look as every seen smile. A save from before A96 loads with no smile brought.
- **Method, not a constant of the child's:** the parent's, hers to tune (the owner's word of 2026-09-04 and 2026-09-06): a person bringing a smile to a baby's face holds it until the baby looks (Stern 1974's positioning in the infant's visual field; Papoušek and Papoušek 1987). The 40-tick cap is the existing queue expiry, no new constant. Nothing of the child's changed.
- **Measured on a copy** (the dawn-2 save, ticks 48,000–49,500, the life's own run on A94+A95 against the same save replayed on A94+A95+A96; one seed, the same child):

  | | A94+A95 (the life) | +A96 (the copy) |
  |---|---|---|
  | ticks her face passed the face test (of 1,499) | 36 | 616 |
  | longest face-test run, ticks | 4 | 78 |
  | her judgments | 8 | 6 |
  | smiles the child saw (its reading rising) | 1 | 1 |
  | ticks walking, standing or turning | 123 | 126 |
  | her lines | 122 | 123 |
  | stress at the end | 2.36 | 2.71 |

  **The live day on A96** (life day 2, from the dawn-3 pair, its first 1,600 ticks): 29 judgments (24 echo, 4 right_name, 1 reach_nearer), 0 smiles seen, her face on its line 50 ticks: on this day's child her line is rarely reachable, and the child never turns its eyes (C82: the gaze's own act was the hold on 600 of 600 probe ticks; only the VOR moves its windows). By tick 5,500 of the same day: 152 judgments (31 right_name, 116 echo, 2 lifted, 1 hit, 1 reach_nearer) and 7 smiles seen, against 2 in all of day 1, its mood 2.5: the en-face position pays once the day gives judgments where she kneels. At its midday (12,000 ticks): 187 judgments (139 echo, 43 right_name, 3 lifted, 1 hit, 1 reach_nearer), 20 smiles seen, her face on its line 1,361 ticks (11%, against 59 ticks in day 1's 19,000), no pain, stress 1.5. The gaze is the other half (A98): the smiles it sees are the ones that fall where its windows already point.

  The same first smile (tick 48,184, from her tall kneel at its side) and the same walk round it (the spot on its line lay beyond the knee route: 479 ticks, three `lean_in` acts stacked, two judgments given on the way and lost). From tick 48,743 she kneels tall on its line at (−0.37, −1.26) and stays there: 28 face-test runs, 41% of the ticks, where the life's run had 2%. No judgment fell in that stretch (6 in all, the last at 48,651, before she was there), so the smiles seen are 1 and 1; the yield of the position is what changed, and the day will show whether the smiles follow. Not fitted to it: nothing of the child's was touched.
- **Tests:** lane 12 `test_smile_brought` (held while brought, at most 40; 20 when not; 10 after a look); lane 10 `test_her_eyes` unchanged (en-face within the hold, the reading rises).

**A97. A limb's decisiveness is earned; the cord's pattern sums** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from life 1's second day: the body's stillness)
- **Found (three probes of the dawn-2 save):** the child's hands were still (median speed 3 mm/s; its left arm pointing at the ceiling, hand z 0.51 m); every limb effector emitted ONE act (the left arm the same seven settings on 267 of 300 ticks, the rest its gate's holds), so nearly every joint sat at its range limit (left shoulder pitch −3.09 of [−3.09, 2.67]; 300 of 300 ticks at a limit on most joints); the cord's kick fired (70 ticks of 300) but was cancelled or clipped; the striatal actor heads were all zero (`actor` 0: the striatum never disposed); every effector's top probability per joint was 1.000, the gaze's and the voice's too. The act is the cortex's forecast of its own next act read at the mouth's sharpness (25 × (1 + mood/6), floor 8): the act it took, predicted, taken again, a fixed point per limb. Forecasting one's own act is trivially certain and says nothing of the world; the language body never met it because the parent's words fill its stream and its gate acts on a quarter of the ticks, where the limbs' gates had risen to 0.92 (every positive dopamine credited acting). Day 0's motion (42 toys lost, 28 lifts) was the born forecast's flatness, gone as the forecast learned its own acts.
- **As built (the body's side; the design's own laws, no new constant):** (1) `body/core/cord.py`: the spinal pattern's step SUMS with the own act on every joint, with it or against it, as A48 states ("summed at the cord with the limb's own act") and as motoneurons do; my cancellation rule ("an own act against the step cancels it") let a constant act cancel every return phase and ratchet the joint to its limit. (2) `body/core/mouth.py _motor_sharp`: a later effector with an inverse model reads its proposal at 1 + (the mouth's sharpness − 1) × its inverse model's reliability (`inv_gain`, the joints' mean kappa: 0 until 64 acts, 0 whenever its acts stop varying): born soft (sharpness 1: the forecast's own spread, the cord's patterns and the born biases weigh against it), decisive as it earns it. This is the design's own maturation law (docs/audit/pfc_maturation.md: born small, weighted by earned reliability; act_pred's lesson already weighs a label by act_inv's kappa) on the corticospinal side (Martin 2005, "The corticospinal system: from development to motor control"; Eyre 2007: the cortex's say over movement grows with development and use). Kappa needs variety, so a fixed point softens the readout that made it: a homeostat, not a rule. Recorded each tick by the runner: `pmax` (each effector's joints' mean top probability), `sharp`, `kappa`.
- **Amended by A104** (2026-09-27): the law 1 + (s − 1) × kappa re-sharpened the limbs at kappa 0.1 within half a day; the decisiveness now follows kappa's own scale, s^rel with rel the reliability's place between "fair" (0.2) and "almost perfect" (0.8).
- **Reached too:** the vocal tract (the effector `voice`, an inverse model of its ears), whose babble had the same fixed point (one articulation, "oh", its top probability 1.000): its decisiveness is earned the same way, which is the songbird's own case (variability injected until the song is learned, Ölveczky et al. 2005); on the copy its vocal judgments held (right_name 8, echo 4, vocal_turn 6 in 1,500 ticks against 5, 1 and 0). **Not changed:** the words effector (its readout is the lexicon's, at the mouth's law); the gaze (no inverse model: it reads as before, C82); the gates (0.92: the vigor law's doing, watched).
- **Measured on the dawn-2 copy** (300 ticks from the save, A94–A96 against A94–A97; the same child, one seed):

  | per limb | before A97 | with A97 |
  |---|---|---|
  | distinct acts in 300 ticks (arm_l / arm_r / leg_l / leg_r) | 2 / 2 / 2 / 2 | 61 / 45 / 65 / 52 |
  | its gate's hold share | 0.10–0.11 | 0.21–0.41 |
  | mean joint speed, rad/s (typical joint) | 0.001–0.03 | 0.05–0.22 |
  | left shoulder pitch at its limit, ticks of 300 | 300 | 266 |

  The old fixed act is still the commonest single act (the posture unwinds from the limits over the ticks), but the body moves again. Over 1,500 ticks from the same save (the reach probe: its hands against the toys, her judgments):

  | | A94–A96 | +A97 |
  |---|---|---|
  | its hands' speed, median m/s (left / right) | 0.003 / — | 0.139 / 0.137 |
  | ticks a hand moved faster than 0.15 m/s | 18 | 707 |
  | nearest toy to a hand, least m | 0.34 | 0.08 |
  | ticks a toy within 0.15 m of a hand (left / right) | 0 / 0 | 13 / 66 |
  | its motor events | 0 | reach_nearer 20, got 4, hit 3, lifted 1, half_roll 7, rolled 2 |
  | her judgments (motor / vocal) | 6 (0 / 6) | 55 (37 / 18) |
  | smiles the child saw | 1 | 2 |
  | ticks her face passed the face test | 616 | 79 |
  | its posture (back / side / front), ticks | 1500 / 0 / 0 | 1311 / 154 / 35 |
  | pain ticks | 0 | 166 |
  | her acts (ticks) | lean_in 546, show 215, walk 70 | attend 856, lean_in 455, walk 67 |

  The body that moves gets hurt: 166 pain ticks (11%), the fixed act still pushing joints into their limits with the pattern summed on top (day 0 had 4 pain ticks in 24,000; the collapsed day none), and she spent 856 ticks comforting it (attend) instead of leaning in, so her face passed the test less. Pain pays −1 (A91) and is the body's to learn away from; the day's stress and cries are watched (C83). Not fitted: the child's reward, senses and rulers are untouched; only what proposes and what sums changed.
- **Boundary:** a body change, so not on A96's day (the teacher's, dawn 3): applied at dawn 4 from the dawn-4 pair.
- **Tests:** motor 6 rewritten to the summing law (the cycle returns where it began on every declared joint); motor 12 `test_earned_decisiveness`.

**A98. The gaze's inverse model; a load with an organ born fresh** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from C82 and the live day on A96)
- **Found:** on the dawn-3 save the gaze's own act was the hold on 600 of 600 ticks (its top probability 1.000; the same self-forecast fixed point as the limbs', A97), so only the VOR moves its windows and the born orienting bias (±log 4 on logits of ±25) cannot turn them to her voice or face. The live day on A96 alone: 29 judgments in 1,600 ticks and 0 smiles seen.
- **As built:** `body/sim/anatomy.py`: the gaze declares an inverse model (`Gaze(..., inverse=True)`: act_inv of its own windows' motion, its sense the gaze state already in the body channel), so A97's earned decisiveness reaches it: born soft, the orienting bias weighs (a face in the periphery, a sound's onset, a sudden change turn the fovea), decisive as its inverse model earns kappa; kappa needs variety, so the hold cannot hold. The oculomotor system does learn its own saccades' consequences (the cerebellum's gain adaptation), and a newborn's looking is orienting-driven. `body/core/persistence.py _moments_aligned`: at a load whose recipe has parameters the saved life had not (the loader births them fresh, `strict=False`), each optimizer's saved moments are placed, in order, on the next parameter of their shape: a parameter born fresh in the middle takes no moments and shifts none onto a wrong shape (the first load crashed on that: Adam's moments of 30 numbers against 12). The identity on the same recipe. No new constant.
- **Measured on the dawn-3 copy** (600 ticks from the save, A97 alone against A97+A98; the same child, one seed):

  | the gaze | A97 | +A98 |
  |---|---|---|
  | distinct own acts in 600 ticks | 1 (the hold, 600 of 600) | 90 |
  | mean top probability per joint | 1.000 | 0.348 |
  | face test within 20 ticks after her "yes!"/"good" (5–6 lines) | 2 ticks | 0 ticks (8 in a first run whose loader had misplaced moments) |
  | its gate's p_act | 0.257 | 0.411 |

  The fixed point is broken (its eyes move by its own acts, the orienting bias weighs), but 600 ticks and six "yes!" lines do not show its fovea landing on her face after one. Over 1,500 ticks from the same save, A97+A98 together against the live day on A96 alone (its first 1,600 ticks, the same dawn-3 child):

  | | the live day, A96 alone | the copy, A96+A97+A98 |
  |---|---|---|
  | its motor events | reach_nearer 1 | reach_nearer 11, half_roll 9, rolled 4, head_up 3, got 1, hit 1, lifted 1 |
  | her judgments (motor / vocal) | 29 (1 / 28) | 27 (11 / 16) |
  | smiles the child saw | 0 | 1 |
  | ticks her face passed the face test | 50 | 54 |
  | face-test ticks within 20 of her "yes!"/"good" | — | 0 (9 lines) |
  | its hands' median speed, m/s (left / right) | still (A97's finding) | 0.199 / 0.122 |
  | its posture back / side / front, ticks | — | 1036 / 106 / 358 |
  | pain ticks | 0 | 116 |
  | her acts (ticks) | — | attend 1000, lean_in 249, walk 171 |

  The body moves, rolls (onto its front for 358 ticks, two distress spells) and is judged for it; the smile is not yet seen after her "yes!" (its eyes move, they do not yet find her: that is the learning to come, or C82's next question), and she comforts more than she teaches (C83). Applied at dawn 4 with A97 on the strength of the movement and the judgments; the smiles are day 3's question. The load births the gaze's inverse model fresh and every other optimizer's moments stay on their parameters (opt_inv: 32 kept, 4 born fresh; the identity everywhere else).
- **Boundary:** a body change, with A97 at dawn 4 (both the body's; the teacher's day was dawn 3). Tests: motor 13 `test_moments_aligned`; motor 12 covers the law.

**A99. Her smile at a word habituates** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from C85: life day 2's ledger)
- **Found:** day 2 (the teacher's day, A96) gave 284 judgments and 42 smiles seen, but 205 of the judgments were echoes and 71 right names of "oh", 8 the body's. A right name or an echo paid WORTH_RIGHT_NAME 2 every time (no count), where a motor act's smile falls as w·e^(−n/10) with the same object (A89, HABIT_TAU, HABIT_FLOOR: A2's fall with mastery). The scaffold's word outpaid the body twenty to one, and the dopamine that credits acting went to a voice saying one word.
- **As built (`conduct._owe_reply`):** the n-th right name or echo of the same word is worth 2·e^(−n/HABIT_TAU), none under HABIT_FLOOR (then the turn is answered as an echo: heard, no smile); a met ask pays in full (her test, spaced by her plan); a new word starts at 0 (`vocal_book`, saved; a save from before it starts every word at 0). No new constant: the motor rule's, applied to the word. 37 smiles per word before silence, their sum about 21 of an unbounded run. A person answers imitation (Goldstein and Schwade 2008) and stops smiling at the hundredth "oh" (A2).
- **Measured on the dawn-4 copy** (1,500 ticks, the same child as the live day 3's opening): 47 judgments (29 the body's, 18 the voice's: 9 vocal turns, 6 echoes, 3 right names), their worth 9 echo and right-name smiles paying 12.4 in all (the sixth echo of a word 1.2 instead of 2); 7 smiles seen, as the live day's; the body's share of the smiles' worth 27 of 48. In 1,500 ticks the difference is small by construction: the rule bites over a day (day 2's 205 echoes of "oh" paid 410; under A99 a word's echoes and right names pay at most about 21 in all).
- **Boundary:** a teacher change, for the teacher's day at dawn 5 (A97+A98, the body's, at dawn 4).
- **Tests:** lang 60 `test_vocal_habituation`; lang 5 unchanged (the first right name pays 2).

**A100. The shown toy comes down within its reach** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from C84)
- **Found:** after a `show` the toy stayed in her hand: the rattle in her right hand for 1,082 of 1,500 ticks on the dawn-2 copy and all of day 1, a hand lost to her (both full: A95's set-asides) and the toy to the child, whose nearest toy lay 34–58 cm from either hand.
- **As built (`parent_motion._plan_show`):** the show (the toy beside her face, shaken for its sound) ends with her arm back before her, then `put_near`: the toy set down beside the child's near hand at her lesson's distance, as the reach lesson's set-up does ("here"). A person who has shown a baby a toy hands it over or sets it within reach; every show is now a reach lesson's set-up by another road. No new constant.
- **Measured:** the dawn-4 copy's 1,500 ticks held no `show` (her day plan drew walks, leans and attends), so A99+A100 replayed A99 alone to the tick: the copy measures nothing of A100. On a fresh world, a `show block` ends with her hands empty and the block within her lesson's distance of the child's nearest hand (the check below the entry, run 2026-09-26). Day 4 measures it live: her holding through the day, toys within 0.15 m of a hand after her shows.
- **Boundary:** a teacher change, with A99 at dawn 5. Tests: parent `test_toys`, lane 10 and 11, lang 5 and 22 rerun; the full lang, lane and parent suites (the babble tests refused a set-down planned straight from the show pose, "her body would have jumped 0.60 m in a tick": the arm comes back first, as before A100, and the set-down is planned from there).

**A101. Her turn of a face-down child, by its far side** (the lead, 2026-09-26, from C81 and life day 3's second half)
- **Found:** day 3's second half was a face-down stall: after a distress event at tick 151,372 the child lay prone and still for 12,000 ticks (no event, no judgment, its eyes on the mat), she knelt beside it with her turn tried and refused (169 ticks). A7's turn "lifted" the near shoulder and hip from above, each hand placed by its own lean: a hand on top of a link cannot lift it (0 N through every hold of `test_sim_parent` 16's turn case), and the second hand's lean pulled the first hand off its hold (`slips` 1), so one hand pushed a hip alone at up to 82 N, the force ramp spent A7's whole 2 s climbing to its cap, and the chest turned 4–22°. A newborn cannot roll back from prone; the parent turns it.
- **As built (`_plan_turn`, `_ctl_turn`):** her hands reach over its back onto the FAR shoulder and FAR hip (the anchor's rays from beyond the child meet the far surfaces; her palms face her), both placed from one trunk pose (one reach phase for both hands, then both holds, then the push), and pull up and toward her together, so the body rolls about its near edge; the force builds to the brief caps within half a second (`TURN_RAMP_NPS` 400 N/s, ours: an initial push is exerted from the first moment) and the act may last 4 s (`TURN_MAX_S`, from A7's 2: the brief caps carry its first 2 s and the sustained caps the rest, by her caps' own law); done past its side (chest normal above TURN_SIDE_Z, or her hands slipping once it has rolled past its side: the far links roll up and over out of her palms there), where it lies on that side with its eyes toward her or settles onto its back (let go at its side it fell back; pushed on toward its back her hands slipped). On the prone child of the turn scenario: both hands on together at 70–88 N, the chest from face down through its side in 11 ticks, her effort's peak 177 N (under CAP_TWO_BRIEF 200), 0.3 s over the sustained cap (under BRIEF_S), its pelvis carried only by the roll.
- **Method, not the child's:** her motion and her strength within A7's sourced caps; the roll a person's; nothing of the child's changed.
- **Boundary:** a teacher change, with A99 and A100 at dawn 5 (the same restart, from the dawn-5 pair). Tests: parent 9 `test_the_turn` (now done, not refused), parent 16's turn case, `test_toys`; the parent and lane suites.

**A102. The turn owed from a distress event** (the lead, 2026-09-27, from life day 4's first 2,000 ticks)
- **Found:** the child lay prone again from the day's start; the distress event fired at tick 192,106 while she was saying "mama is here", and her choice runs only when her voice is free, so the one-tick event was dropped: no turn asked, and she comforted and leaned over a face-down child for 2,000 ticks (6 judgments, 0 smiles, her face never on its line). Day 3's stall had the same shape with a turn that could not roll it (A101); this one was a turn never asked.
- **As built:** the conduct remembers a distress event (`distress_due`, saved) and asks the turn at her first free tick while the percept says the child lies face down (`Percept.face_down`, from the lane's posture), and asks it again, at her lines' own spacing, whenever a turn has ended and it still lies face down (the restarted day's first turn ran 134 ticks and left it prone, and was never asked again: the distress event fires once a spell); one turn at a time; the debt is dropped once it is no longer face down (its own roll, or her turn). A person who is mid-sentence when the baby is found stuck finishes the word and turns it, and tries again if the first try fails. No new constant. The runner records each act's end with its reason (`acts_ended`), so a failed turn says why.
- **Boundary:** a teacher change, on the teacher's day (day 4, restarted from the dawn-5 pair with it; the 2,000 ticks lived without it are discarded, as a restart from a dawn save always discards). Tests: lang 61 `test_distress_owed`; the lang and lane suites.

**A103. The turn's grasp travels with the link; its spot chosen for both grips' reach** (the lead, 2026-09-27, from C88)
- **Found:** on the live child of day 4, every turn asked (32 in 6,000 ticks) was refused: 13 ran their 4 s without rolling it, 8 slipped, 11 could not reach a far grip (31–60 cm short). A hold was a palm on a point fixed on the link at engagement, judged slipped once her hand lay a hand's length (HOLD_SLIP_M) from that point: the far shoulder and hip roll up and over as the body rolls, and a flailing arm or leg moves them, so the point ran from her hand while her hand was still on the link.
- **As built:** (1) a turn hold whose hand still touches the held link (her hand's clearance to it within HAND_FREE_M) keeps its hold where the link has carried it (its grip offset re-anchored on the link) and slips only once it has left the link: a grasp round the limb's root, as a person's; (2) the hand that waits for the other rests on its link with a resting hand's force (TOUCH_N) instead of 0 N, so it rides the link as the child moves. On the still prone child the turn now ends on its BACK (chest normal 0.93; its side before). On the babbling prone child (the scenario, babbler seeds at p_rest 0.3), 0 of 6 (seeds 1, 5, 6, 8, 2, 3): five refused for reach ("her right hand cannot reach it from here": 31, 40, 52, 85, 100 cm short, the child having moved or rolled between her spot's choice and her hands' arrival), one ran its 4 s without rolling it. Tried and dropped: a kneeling spot chosen for both far grips' reach (a trunk solve at the candidate spot): it refused the usual spot the turn works from and chose worse ones (the still child ended on its side, the engagement 3.4 cm off), and gave 1 of 6 under babble.
- **What remains (C88):** the reach on a moving child: her spot and her hands are planned for the child as it lies at each step, and it moves between them; a person tracks the far grips through the reach. The live day's retries (A102) make each attempt anew.
- **Boundary:** a teacher change, for the teacher's day after A104's (dawn 7, from the dawn-7 pair). Tests: parent 9, 16 and the babble tests; the parent suite.

**A104. A limb's decisiveness on kappa's own scale** (the lead, 2026-09-27, from life days 3 and 4's rulers)
- **Found:** A97 read a limb's proposal at 1 + (the mouth's 25 − 1) × kappa. On day 3 the limbs' top probability per joint went from 0.29 in the day's first sixth (sharpness 1.5–1.7, kappa 0.04–0.06) to 0.94–0.99 by its third sixth (sharpness 4–6) as kappa rose only to 0.08–0.11, and stayed above 0.9 through day 4 (kappa 0.07–0.14): a slight reliability bought a near-deterministic readout, the fixed point of A97 returned, and the body's earning (36 motor judgments in day 3's first quarter) fell to 0 in its third and fourth. Kappa 0.1 is "slight" agreement on Landis and Koch's scale; a linear law from 1 to 25 mistakes it for a tenth of mastery, and a five-setting joint is decisive already at sharpness 3.
- **As built (`_motor_sharp`):** the readout's sharpness is the mouth's to the power of the reliability's place on kappa's established scale, rel = clip((kappa − 0.2) / (0.8 − 0.2), 0, 1): nothing earned below "fair" agreement (0.2), the mouth's sharpness from "almost perfect" (0.8), the square root of it at "moderate" (0.5) (Landis and Koch 1977, Biometrics 33:159; `KAPPA_FAIR`, `KAPPA_ALMOST_PERFECT`). The interpolation is geometric because a softmax temperature's natural scale is its logarithm. Kappa needs variety, so the law remains a homeostat, now one that lets the body keep its variety until its inverse model has earned more than a slight agreement.
- **Measured on the dawn-5 copy** (300 ticks from the save; the live day 4 from the same save ran at top probabilities 0.87–0.96):

  | per limb (arm_l / arm_r / leg_l / leg_r) | the live day 4 (A97's law) | A104 |
  |---|---|---|
  | distinct acts in 300 ticks | near one act (top probability 0.87–0.96) | 60 / 56 / 71 / 57 |
  | the gate's hold share | 0.03–0.1 | 0.61 / 0.69 / 0.48 / 0.35 |
  | the commonest act other than the hold | — | 8 of 300 ticks at most |

  The variety is back, and the gates, with acts that vary, settle lower (p_act 0.34–0.65 from 0.9+).

  **Live, day 5's first 2,000 ticks:** every limb at sharpness 1.0 (kappa 0.09–0.14, below "fair"), top probabilities 0.24–0.25, gates 0.18–0.79; 30 judgments of which 20 the body's (reach_nearer 11, rolled 6, got 1, hit 1, head_up 1), against 4 of 201 in all of day 4; 1 smile seen; and the moving body's cost again, 140 pain ticks (7%), cry 134, stress 20 (C83).
- **Boundary:** a body change, at dawn 6 from the dawn-6 pair, on a tree without the day's teacher changes (A103 waits for dawn 7). Tests: motor 12 rewritten to the scale, motor 2; motor 3's sanity floor on a living limb's kappa after 400 ticks lowered from 0.2 to 0.1 (it read 0.198 under the softer readout; the floor is not a claim about the learning rate); motor 13/13, sim_world 20/20; the sim profile re-pinned, the language digest reproduced.

**A105. A base left in a transition settles before the next act** (the lead, 2026-09-27, from life day 5's morning)
- **Found:** her turn was refused 85 times in day 5's first half, every one by the jump guard: "her body would have jumped 0.45 m in a tick (a planning fault: foot_R, phase turn)". The phase named is her own turn on her feet: her base had been left in the transitional mode `shuffle` by an act stopped where it was, the approach planner treats a base that is neither kneeling nor sitting as standing, so it planned a standing turn and walk from a kneeling body, the first frame jumped, the guard refused, the base stayed `shuffle`, and every retry (A102) met the same wall.
- **As built (`_settle_phases`, at an act's start):** a base in `shuffle` or `knee_turn` becomes her tall kneel where her pelvis is (the same height, no jump); one in `walk` or `turn` her standing there; a kneel half done (`kneel_down` at a fractional u) finishes to its nearer end by its own phase, and the act is planned after it (a `plan act` phase). No new constant. A person stopped mid-move stands or kneels where she is before she does the next thing.
- **Also:** the same settle right after a jump refusal, the loop's source: the guard cleared her phases mid-move and left the base there.
- **Boundary:** a teacher change, with A103 at dawn 7. Tests: parent 22 `test_a_stale_base_settles` (the settle on each transitional mode: a shuffle and a knee turn to the tall kneel where her pelvis is, a walk to standing, a half kneel to its own finishing phase with the act planned after, a settled base left alone; then the next act done with no jump); the parent suite.

### (B) The owner's calls, with recommended defaults

The nine decisions of the G1 amendment are settled (section 14). These are the environment's remaining shapes, each with the default the design uses until the owner says otherwise. B4, B14 and B18 were answered by the lead for the owner on 2026-09-24 (A37, A38, A40); he may overrule each. On 2026-09-25 B21 was answered by the owner's own word (A61), and B3 by the lead for him (A65).

| # | question | recommended default | what it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | At first the G1's hand could not hold four toys (the ball, the duck, the bear, the drum), so we made the cup smaller (0.8 of its size, 6.7 cm across) to let the hand hold it. That was the simulator's contacts creeping. With the contact setting chosen by physics (impratio 10, A32), the hand holds the ball, the duck and the full-size cup; only the bear (2 of 9 tries) and the drum (0 of 9) cannot be held. May we put the cup back to its own size, and leave the bear and the drum as they are? | Yes to both. The cup's shrink fitted the world to the body, and now has no reason; the bear and the drum are for looking at, pushing, hitting and naming. Until you say, the cup stays at 0.8. | Eight toys are holdable either way; the full-size cup held 9 of 9. |
| B2 | May we add four colour twins (a blue ball, a red block, a yellow cup, a green car), so we can test "the red ball" when it has never heard those words together? | Yes. | Four more toys; colour words come in once two toys share a colour (A28). |
| B3 | If the tick is too slow, may its eyes (a) see a simpler copy of its own body, (b) see the room without the sun's shadow, or (c) take the colour camera's picture from the left eye's view, about 15 mm from where the real colour camera sits? You would not see (a) or (c); (b) takes a piece of the complete reality you asked for. | **Answered by the lead for the owner (A65):** (a) yes, a lighter copy of its own meshes for its eyes only, the coarsest whose images stay within its sensors' noise and a pixel where its body is nearest its eyes (C69); (b) no, the sun's shadow stays (decision 3); (c) no, on the evidence: the RGB sensor sits 15 mm from the left imager's centre-line (RealSense's documentation), so colour cut from the left eye's render would miss the robot's parallax by 2.9° at 0.3 m and 5.7° at 0.15 m, 1.1–2.2 of the colour window's cells where its hands reach, and the real G1 would be a change of body (A36). (The first default was neither unless the heat-soaked tick passed 150 ms; then (a) first, then (c).) | (a) saves about 8–18 ms over the two grey eyes and about half as much again in the colour view: about 12–27 ms a tick (an estimate); with the exact levers the tick is about 65–135 ms, 1.1–2.3× (section 9's case C). (b) would have saved about 21 ms and (c) about 10–15. No 150 ms bar binds a lockstep life: a slower tick costs wall time only. |
| B4 | The real G1's two eye cameras see in grey; ours see in colour. Keep colour? | **Answered by the lead for the owner (A38):** no. Its two eyes are the D435's grey imagers, and its colour comes from the D435's own colour camera beside the left one, each with its noise. (The first default was yes.) | The robot's eyes are the sim's, so the move to the robot is a change of world. The colour camera costs a third view (B3). |
| B5 | The model does not say where the G1's microphones and speaker are. Put its ears on the sides of its head and its voice at the front? | Yes, until Unitree's documents say where they are; they are read before birth, since a different place on the robot would make the move a change of body (A36). | The ears' timing and loudness. |
| B6 | Keep the child's voice as built: a child-sized throat, pitch about 265 Hz? | Yes. | What you hear it say. |
| B7 | Nobody can carry the 34 kg child back, so the parent goes to it wherever it is. Keep the whole floor and the open hall as its play space, with no baby gate? | Yes. | Where it can end up. |
| B8 | Close the gap under the sofa, take away the low table's pretend shelf (the lying G1's chest is 1 cm under it), and put lost toys back in their basket each morning? | Yes to all three. | Two lines in the maker; one morning rule in the world. |
| B9 | Should the room echo? | Yes, if it costs under 1 ms a tick; otherwise no, and we report it. | The ears hear the room. Measured since (P2): each sound source costs about 0.37–0.4 ms a tick through the ears' spatializer, so six first-order echoes (the walls, the floor and the ceiling as image sources) would cost about 2.2–2.4 ms. Unless W5 finds a cheaper echo, the default gives none, reported. |
| B10 | Keep charging as a bottle it holds in its hand, about 5–6 times a day while it moves a lot? | Yes. | Charging by mouth would need a mouth the G1 does not have. |
| B11 | Keep the mat at 2.8 × 2.0 m? | Yes. | — |
| B12 | Your decision 1 lists "looking at it" among what raises her happiness, and also says smiles come only for completed, visible acts. Should the child just looking at her ever make her smile? | No. Looking raises her attention (her eyes and a greeting flash, which the child cannot feel as reward); only its acts earn a smile. A look she asked for (a call answered, "look at the drum") is an act and does earn one. | Otherwise looking would pay. |
| B13 | Keep the names "pip" and "mama", the Samantha voice, and no second adult? | Yes. A better voice is a download you would install yourself. | — |
| B14 | Should she tell where the child is looking from its fovea, as people read a baby's eyes, though a real G1 shows no eyes? (Claude's minute digest reads the same window.) | **Answered by the lead for the owner (A40):** no. She reads its trunk and hands with a person's error, as she could on the real robot, and so does the digest. (The first default was yes.) | She names the wrong toy more often, and asks are met less often: a look must show in its trunk or hands (risk 19). |
| B15 | Keep the living room at 5.2 × 4.6 m? For the 1.32 m G1 that is like a 2.9 × 2.6 m room for a baby. | Yes. More room comes with the house (section 16). | How often it meets a wall or furniture (A24). |
| B16 | Keep the parent as strong as a real person, so she can never lift it, slide it or sit it up? | Yes. It must rise by itself, even if sitting comes late. | Stronger, she could carry it through postures it has not reached (A25). |
| B17 | If the child's own hearing and voice never pass the tests for dropping the word tokens, keep the tokens? | Yes. They stay and are tested again every 5 life days (A29). | With a fixed date instead, it may lose words it knows. |
| B18 | The real G1 feels touch only in its hands. Should our G1 feel touch (and pain) on its whole body, like a baby's skin, though the robot has no skin there? | **Answered by the lead for the owner (A37):** no. Touch is felt in the Dex3 hands, as on the robot, and contact and pain elsewhere are estimated from the joints' efforts, as a robot's collision detector does (Haddadin et al. 2017). (The first default was yes, with an added skin or the joints' estimate named as the robot's options.) | Pain stays one of its three rewards, read from what the robot can feel; a blow is felt through the joints it loads. The old skin's rates (6–12% of babble ticks, mostly its own housings) are measured again under the joints' law, with the share from hulls meeting (C43, A54). |
| B19 | A life day is one simulated hour, so the sun crosses the window in an hour, not a day. Keep that? | Yes. A real-length day would make each life day 24 times longer in wall time. | How fast the light changes. |
| B20 | The real G1's motors hum as they work, and its microphones hear it. Add that sound, growing with each motor's effort? | Yes, if it costs under 1 ms a tick; otherwise no, and we report it. | It hears its own effort; nothing rewards it. |
| B21 | On this Mac the life runs at about 0.8–1.3× real time (on a quiet, cool machine; sustained speed is unmeasured). So an infant's first year of waking hours, about 3,925 life days, would take about 177–300 days of running around the clock, or 133–228 with our exact levers. A rented Linux machine with a graphics card might run it at 2.2–4.7× (49–106 days), but that is an estimate, unmeasured. May we spend on (1) a one-hour test on such a machine, about $1–2, and then, if it reaches 2.5× or better, (2) renting it for the life? | **Answered by the owner's own word, 2026-09-25 (A61):** "only local no cloud". No cloud or rented machine, no paid test and no spending, ever: seed 1 is born and lives on this Mac. (The first default was no spending now, and a paid hour only on his yes.) Claude's steering stays, served on his plan, never the metered API; if he means no call off this Mac at all, the fast layer runs alone (4.5). | Only how many calendar days the life takes: nothing the body senses or does changes (A59). On this Mac, with the exact levers and the lighter meshes for its eyes, 60 life days take about 1.6–3.3 calendar days of running around the clock and 200 about 5–11 (section 9). The rented machines' estimates and prices stay in git history (7bdc141). C63 is closed. |
### (C) Open until measured

| # | open question | measured in | what decides it |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Which child | — | settled: the stock G1 (the owner's decision 7) |
| C2 | Whether the face test's rays agree with a segmentation render inside the software fovea | W3: 2,000 babbled frames, the parent at 0.3–3.5 m | at least 95% agreement; below that, the render is used. Measured: 99.8% of the 975 frames whose geometry passes, her head kept inside the room (98.7–100% in each distance bin); the rays stay |
| C3 | Whether the fovea tells the toys and the face apart at 1.5 px a degree, under morning, midday and dusk light | W3's eye check (`tools/sim_eye_check.py`), again under W5's lights | identity at least 0.75 by A33's readout (a small nonlinear readout on the code through the channel's born projection into the core's d, at 67 training views a class, each light with and without the sun's shadow), the curve by views reported beside it; below that, the eye's own born constants (the fovea's size and code) are reconsidered before birth. The light and the shadow are never changed for it (5.4, B3). Measured on stand-in lights through the tool's projection into 256: 0.794–0.836, passing in all six settings (0.74–0.78 at 44 training views, 0.65–0.72 at 22); on the full code 0.840–0.860; linear readouts 0.52–0.64. - **Built (103bf06; motor 12, the suite 151).** A load gave back the organs, the store and the life dict and started everything else fresh: the body's random stream (re-seeded), the optimizers' moments (`opt_day`, `opt_inv`, `opt_value`, `opt_face`), 47 working attributes (`_orient_last` among them) and up to 16 keys of each motor effector's state; handing back everything but the stream, or everything but the moments, still differed at tick 300. So a motor body's save now holds the whole day (`blob["day"]`): the stream's state, each optimizer's moments, 119 working attributes, and per motor effector `cry_t`, `unit`, `cord_n`, `inv_kappa_pooled`, `chunk`, `acted_last`, `now`, `sense`, `fwd`, `err`, `buf`, `g_base`, `last`, `e_actor`, `inv_last`, `chunks`, `stops`, under the anatomy's signature. The rhythm's cache is recomputed from the tick. A motor save without a day says so once and starts the day fresh (R6h's rule for older saves); the language body's save is unchanged (its pins). The replay test: the G1 under SIM_CFG, learning on, d 32, the whole state at tick 300 identical in every section to the uninterrupted life for a save inside a day (150: mid-expiration, 6 units under way, a face held), a save at the night boundary (225), and a save at 150 lived through the night; the same save with its day stripped differs in organs, store, optim, rng and work. **The cost:** at d 512 (6 blocks) the save is 397.3 MB against 176.6 without the day, 214.2 MB of it Adam's moments; 6.4's disk rule and 9.5's peak must count it (the copies kept are the newest few). `Life.load(seed=…)` no longer seeds a motor body's stream when the save holds a day. **Found and carried:** the night's own save (to `save_path`) is written inside the tick, before the world applies that tick's acts and runs the cerebellum's sub-steps; the core's save is exact between ticks, so the sim's nightly save belongs at the tick's end: C74.
